# Tasks — Done Archive

Completed entries move here from `TASKS.md`'s `Backlog`, in the same commit that closes them out. This file is history, not a work queue — it is intentionally **not** part of the top-to-bottom read `TASKS.md` asks for when picking up new work, since a finished task has no bearing on what to do next. Consult it only when you need context on a specific completed task — its resolved decisions, exit criteria, or notes — that isn't already captured elsewhere (commit messages, code comments where warranted).

Entries keep their content as it was at completion. Don't retroactively edit a description or exit criteria after the fact; if something about a completed task turns out to be wrong or misleading, note that in a new entry or in code, not by rewriting history here.

### [T256] Fix the onboarding practice-minutes field's broken web focus styling

- **Area:** Onboarding
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** The practice-minutes onboarding field (`onboarding-duration-step.tsx`, rendered via the shared `TextField`/`Input` in `text-field.tsx` with `inputMode="numeric"` and `keyboardType="number-pad"`) shows a green background and no visible text cursor when focused on web. No other reported `TextField` usage in the app shows this, so it's likely specific to this field's numeric input configuration (a browser autofill/one-time-code heuristic on short numeric inputs is a plausible cause, worth checking first). Diagnose and fix so the field renders with the app's normal input styling and a visible cursor.
- **Exit criteria:** Confirmed in-browser (web, Chrome) that focusing and typing in the onboarding practice-minutes field shows the app's normal themed input background (no unexpected color) and a visible text cursor throughout. `text-field.test.tsx` and the onboarding wizard tests (`kentei-onboarding.test.tsx`) still pass, and any other `TextField` usage in the app is visibly unaffected by the fix. Met: `text-field.tsx` now resolves an `autoComplete` default of `'off'` for numeric fields (`inputMode`/`keyboardType` indicating numeric/decimal input) unless the caller supplies its own `autoComplete`, since the root cause is Chrome's default autofill/one-time-code heuristic activating on short numeric inputs with no explicit `autoComplete` attribute. Confirmed via a throwaway Playwright script against a local Expo web dev server that the onboarding minutes field now renders `autocomplete="off"` (previously the browser default `"on"`) with the themed row background (`rgb(223, 232, 202)`) and matching `color`/`caret-color` (`rgb(24, 56, 46)`) while focused and typed into, and that unrelated `TextField` usages (e.g. sign-in email/password) keep the unset default `autocomplete="on"`. `text-field.test.tsx` and `kentei-onboarding.test.tsx` pass unchanged. Note: Chrome's ML-backed autofill-assistant highlighting itself isn't reproducible in Playwright's vanilla Chromium, so the exact green-background regression couldn't be screenshotted before/after in this sandbox; the fix applies the standard, well-documented mitigation (`autoComplete="off"`) for this exact class of Chrome heuristic and is confirmed not to regress any other field.
- **Notes:** From S127. High priority: this sits in the onboarding funnel and a field that looks broken risks drop-off. If the fix requires special-casing numeric fields, prefer doing so in the shared `text-field.tsx` component (behind a prop) over a one-off override in `onboarding-duration-step.tsx`, so any other numeric `TextField` in the app gets the same fix.


### [T254] Fully hide the landing header bar on scroll, not just fade it

- **Area:** Landing page
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `landing-auth-cta.tsx`'s `HeaderBar` toggles only `opacity` (`opacity: ${$visible ? 1 : 0}`) based on `landing-page.tsx`'s scroll-driven `headerVisible` state, while its `background-color`, 2px `border-bottom`, and absolute top-of-screen position stay rendered at all times. The visible text/buttons fade out, but the bar's background/border container remains visibly present over the content once scrolled past the threshold. Change this so the entire header bar — text, buttons, background, and border — is fully hidden once scrolled past `HEADER_BAR_SCROLL_HIDE_THRESHOLD`.
- **Exit criteria:** `landing-page.test.tsx`'s `LandingPage header bar` describe block gains a test asserting the header bar has no visible rendered container (not just `pointerEvents: 'none'`) once scrolled past the threshold, and that it's restored when scrolling back near the top. Confirmed in-browser at both mobile and desktop widths that no header bar background or border is visible after scrolling past the threshold. Met: added `display: ${({ $visible }) => ($visible ? 'flex' : 'none')}` alongside the existing `opacity`/`pointerEvents` toggle on `HeaderBar`, so the whole container (background, border, text, buttons) leaves layout entirely once hidden rather than just fading its contents. Added `fully hides the header bar container` and `restores the header bar container` tests to the `LandingPage header bar` describe block asserting the resolved `display` style, alongside the untouched existing `pointerEvents`/reappear tests. Confirmed in-browser via a throwaway Playwright script against a local Expo web dev server at 375x812 and 1280x900: post-scroll computed `display` is `none` with the header's bounding rect empty (no background/border painted), and it restores to `flex` on scrolling back to the top.
- **Notes:** From S124. Follows the existing `opacity`/`pointerEvents` mechanism rather than replacing it, and node_modules needed a fresh `yarn install` in this worktree before Jest was runnable.

### [T253] Play the landing page's first screenshot entrance animation on mount

- **Area:** Landing page
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `landing-page.tsx`'s `ScreenshotEntry`/`useHasEnteredViewport` drives every "See it in action" screenshot's entrance animation off one shared `IntersectionObserver`. The first screenshot doesn't play its entrance animation on initial page load — it just appears — while every subsequent screenshot correctly animates in as the visitor scrolls to it. Diagnose why the first item's observer doesn't fire (or doesn't fire visibly) on mount and fix it so the first screenshot animates in immediately, without requiring the visitor to scroll first.
- **Exit criteria:** Confirmed in-browser (Expo dev server, `/`, no scroll) that the first screenshot in the "See it in action" section plays its `FadeInUp` entrance animation automatically shortly after page load. Confirmed every subsequent screenshot still animates in on scroll exactly as before. `landing-page.test.tsx`'s existing screenshot-showcase tests still pass.
- **Notes:** From S123. jsdom has no `IntersectionObserver`, so `useHasEnteredViewport`'s observer branch is untested today (tests exercise only the `hasEntered` default-true fallback) — this needs a real-browser diagnosis, not a Jest repro. A likely cause worth checking first: the first screenshot's image hasn't finished loading (and so has no laid-out height) at the moment the observer computes its initial intersection, making its bounding box collapse to zero and read as non-intersecting. Real-browser diagnosis via a throwaway Playwright script against a local Expo web dev server (multiple viewport sizes, instrumented `IntersectionObserver` callback logging, and network-throttled image loads) found the CSS `aspect-ratio` reserved by `expo-image` already gives the item a correct non-zero box before its image resource loads, so the notes' zero-height hypothesis didn't reproduce. The real gap: the first screenshot is often below the fold at typical viewport heights, so it never intersects the shrunk observed band without a scroll — but the desired UX is that it always plays immediately since it's the section's lead-in. Fixed by having `useHasEnteredViewport` accept a `skipObserver` flag, passed as `index === 0` from `ScreenshotEntry`, so the first item starts `hasEntered` true unconditionally (mirroring the existing non-web/no-`IntersectionObserver` fallback) and plays its `FadeInUp` as a normal mount animation, while every other index keeps the unchanged observer-gated, scroll-triggered path.

### [T251] Require password complexity at signup and reset

- **Area:** Authentication
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** Replace `password-policy.ts`'s current rule (`MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 10`, length-only) with: at least 8 characters, at least one uppercase letter, at least one number, and at least one symbol. Update `sign-up-screen.tsx`'s password hint text and any related validation error copy to state these actual rules instead of just a character count.
- **Exit criteria:** `validateNewPassword` rejects a password missing any one of the four rules and accepts one satisfying all four, with test coverage for each rule individually and combined. Submitting a password failing any rule on the Sign up screen shows an error identifying what's missing, before a network round trip. The Sign up screen's password hint states all four rules. `yarn tsc --noEmit` passes; existing signup/reset-password screen tests pass with copy assertions updated to the new hint/error text.
- **Notes:** From S121. `password-policy.ts` is shared by `sign-up-screen.tsx` and `reset-password-screen.tsx` via `validateNewPassword` — this ticket applies the new rule to both flows for consistency, since they already share the module. The user's request named only the signup screen's copy; this shared-scope choice was flagged in the story as an assumption pending confirmation. `MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH` is now 8, but the hosted Supabase Auth project's `minimum_password_length` config (10, per `supabase/config.toml`/`supabase/README.md`) was left unchanged as out of scope for this client-validation ticket — an 8-11 character password with full complexity would now pass client validation but could still be rejected server-side; flagging as a candidate follow-up rather than fixing here.

### [T250] Swap the shared text input's border and shadow colors

- **Area:** UI components
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `text-field.tsx`'s `InputRow` currently renders its border in an opaque theme color (`theme.text` unfocused, `theme.accent` focused) and its drop shadow in a separate, focus-independent translucent color (`inputShadow(theme.text)`, `SHADOW_ALPHA = 0.35`). Swap which color expression drives which visual: the border should render using the color/alpha currently applied to the shadow, and the shadow should render using the color currently applied to the border — preserving the existing accent-on-focus distinction on whichever role now carries it.
- **Exit criteria:** Confirmed in-browser (Sign up and at least one other `TextField`-using screen) that the border now renders in the color/translucency previously used for the shadow, the drop shadow now renders in the color previously used for the border, and focus still produces a visibly distinct state. No other functional change to any input. `text-field.test.tsx` and dependent screen tests still pass.
- **Notes:** From S120. Follow-up color-only gap on `Done` ticket T241 — same "one shared-component change, no per-screen verification needed beyond a couple of spot checks" scope T241 itself established. Verified via updated automated `text-field.test.tsx` assertions plus a throwaway Playwright script against a local Expo web dev server on both the Sign up and Sign in screens: the border now renders as a translucent, focus-independent `rgba(theme.text, 0.35)`, and the drop shadow now renders opaque and focus-aware (`theme.text` unfocused, `theme.accent` focused). Also removed the now-unused `$isFocused` prop from `InputRow`, since focus is read from the existing `isFocused` closure variable instead. `yarn tsc --noEmit` and scoped `yarn eslint` passed.

### [T249] Diagnose and fix the new-account welcome overlay not appearing

- **Area:** Authentication / Home
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** T208's `NewAccountWelcomeOverlay` (mounted in `src/app/_layout.tsx`, triggered by `auth-callback-screen.tsx`'s `router.replace({ pathname: '/app/home', params: { newAccount: '1' } })` on the default post-verification branch) does not render at all in the flow the user tested, even though T208's own browser verification passed when it shipped and the wiring looks correct on inspection today. Needs a fresh runtime diagnosis against the actual environment/flow the user tested, then a fix for whatever's actually broken.
- **Exit criteria:** Reproduce the failure via a real signup + email-confirmation-link round trip (in the environment the user tested in, if that differs from the local Supabase CLI stack T208 verified against). Identify the actual cause — e.g. the `newAccount` param never reaching `_layout.tsx` (stripped upstream, or interference from a hosting/rewrite layer on the dynamic `/app/home?newAccount=1` URL — see T227), a timing/state regression from a later change touching the callback screen (T224/T236), or something else — and fix it. Confirm afterward that the overlay renders exactly once on a real verification round trip and not on a routine sign-in, matching T208's original exit criteria.
- **Notes:** From S119. Follow-up regression/gap on `Done` ticket T208. Root cause confirmed by runtime reproduction: `src/app/_layout.tsx` mounts exactly once per browser page load and never remounts on in-app navigation, and `showNewAccountWelcome`'s lazy `useState` initializer only reads `newAccount` at that single mount. The real signup round trip lands the root layout on `/auth/callback` (no `newAccount` param present yet), and `auth-callback-screen.tsx`'s subsequent `router.replace({ pathname: '/app/home', params: { newAccount: '1' } })` is a client-side History navigation on Expo Router web, not a full page reload -- so the already-mounted layout's lazy initializer never re-fires, and the only post-mount effect just stripped the param without ever setting the state. T208's own tests only covered the param-present-at-mount case, matching a direct navigation to `/app/home?newAccount=1`, not this redirect-driven arrival -- explaining why it shipped and looked correct on inspection. Fixed by having the strip effect also call `setShowNewAccountWelcome(true)` (guarded by a renamed `hasHandledNewAccountParamRef`) so a post-mount `newAccount=1` arrival is picked up, not just one present at the very first render; kept the lazy initializer for the synchronous no-pop-in mount-time case. Added a regression test to `src/app/__tests__/root-layout.test.tsx` that mounts with no param, then updates `mockGlobalSearchParams` and re-renders, asserting the overlay now shows and the param is stripped -- reproducing T249's exact failure shape. T227's hosting/rewrite layer was ruled out: `/app/home` is a static route with no `vercel.json` rewrite entry, and the transition from `/auth/callback` is a client-side SPA replace, not a hard navigation. Confirmed against the real environment: a throwaway Playwright script (not committed) drove a real signup through a local Expo web dev server backed by the local Supabase CLI stack, extracted the Mailpit-delivered confirmation link's PKCE code, and navigated `/auth/callback?code=...` directly (no pre-warming of `/app/home`) -- verified the overlay now renders exactly once, the URL is stripped of `newAccount=1` without a manual refresh, it auto-dismisses, and a routine sign-in with the same confirmed account shows nothing. Re-ran the identical script against the pre-fix code to confirm it reproduces the reported defect (overlay never appears) before the fix, and passes after. Verified: targeted Jest (`root-layout.test.tsx`, `auth-callback-screen.test.tsx`, `new-account-welcome-overlay.test.tsx`), full `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and scoped `yarn eslint` all passed.

### [T248] Surface local durability failures and recovery

- **Area:** Local persistence UX
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Make failed local writes visible and retryable across profile, custom tricks, practice history, goals, and notes so the UI never silently acknowledges state that exists only in memory.
- **Notes:** Umbrella from D002. Completed via T248.1 (shared durability contract) and T248.2 (app-shell warning/retry surface).

### [T248.2] Add an app-level local-save warning and retry action

- **Area:** App shell / local persistence UX
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T248)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Aggregate the five domain durability handles behind a dedicated app-level contract and show one persistent, non-blocking but unmistakable warning whenever current changes are not safely stored on the device. Provide a retry action and copy that clearly distinguishes local-save failure from cloud-sync status.
- **Exit criteria:** A fully wired five-provider integration test and app-shell component tests prove that a failure in any domain shows one accessibly announced warning that remains visible across route changes; retry operates on every currently failed domain's latest snapshot without duplicate concurrent attempts; repeated failure keeps the warning and actionable guidance visible; and the warning disappears only after every current-generation failed write succeeds. Cloud synchronization success cannot hide or misrepresent an outstanding local-save failure.
- **Notes:** From D002. Depends on T248.1. Added `src/shared/persistence/use-app-local-durability.ts` -- a shared aggregation hook (`useAppLocalDurability`) calling all five `use*Durability()` hooks directly, plus a pure, unit-tested `deriveFailedDomainLabels` for precedence/labeling, mirroring `deriveSyncStatus`'s shape without importing anything from `src/shared/sync/`. `retryAll` guards duplicate concurrent invocations with a `useRef` flag and reads the five live statuses fresh at call time (via `useMemo` keyed on the five source values) so a retry always targets each domain's latest failed snapshot. Added `src/components/local-durability-warning-banner.tsx`: renders nothing when no domain is failing; otherwise a single `View` with `accessibilityRole="alert"`/`accessibilityLiveRegion="assertive"` naming the failing domains by label with copy that explicitly distinguishes local-save failure from cloud sync, plus a `Pressable` "Retry" control disabled while retrying. Wired as `<LocalDurabilityWarningBanner />` in `src/app/app/_layout.tsx`, mounted once inside `AppThemeProvider` and before `<AppTabs />` -- normal flex flow above the tab/nav box (not the absolutely-positioned nav layer), so it survives every route change under `/app/*` without remounting. Added `use-app-local-durability.test.ts` (pure derivation), `use-app-local-durability.retry.test.tsx` (dedup/retry-all with mocked domain hooks), `local-durability-warning-banner.test.tsx` (render/hide, alert naming, Retry disabling, no remount across a sibling prop change), and `src/app/app/__tests__/local-durability-warning.integration.test.tsx` (fully wired five real providers plus fake sync transports, forcing real `AsyncStorage.setItem` rejections for two domains: proves one alert names both failing domains, survives an in-tree route-stand-in change, and clears only once every failed write succeeds; a second test proves a concurrent double Retry press invokes each domain's own `retry()` exactly once and a still-failing retry keeps the warning and Retry control visible). Manually verified in a real Chromium browser at the supported 320px width (Playwright script driving a live `expo start --web` server): seeded a valid guest profile, forced `localStorage.setItem` to throw, added a custom trick through the real UI to trigger two real domain-write failures, confirmed the single alert-role banner (no horizontal overflow at 320px), keyboard Tab reaching and Enter activating the Retry control, the banner staying visible across an in-app tab navigation (Tricks to Home), and the banner clearing once the injected failure was lifted and Retry pressed again. Verified: full `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (test/lint/typecheck/web-export) passed.

### [T248.1] Retain and expose failed local writes

- **Area:** Shared local persistence
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T248)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Extend the identity-scoped persistence contract with a shared typed local-durability state and retry operation. Retain the latest unsaved snapshot after a failed effect-driven write, reuse the existing FIFO queue for retries, and expose the same contract through all five domain providers without creating separate ad hoc failure models.
- **Exit criteria:** Shared-hook and parameterized provider tests prove that returned and thrown save failures expose a failed state with the latest unsaved snapshot available for retry; rapid mutations and retries preserve write ordering and never replace newer state with an older failed snapshot; a later successful write clears the failure; and identity changes clear prior-identity status while preventing stale writes or completions from touching the new namespace. All five persistent domains expose the same typed durability handle.
- **Notes:** From D002. Depends on T247 so normal and imperative writes share one durable-outcome vocabulary. Keep failure state runtime-only and separate from cloud outboxes/`SyncStatus`; preserve each domain's reducer and persistence ownership. Logging may remain diagnostic but cannot be the only failure signal. Added `LocalDurabilityStatus<State>` to `src/shared/persistence/storage.ts` (a `saved`/`failed` union, the failed branch carrying the latest unsaved snapshot and a `retry(): Promise<ScopedSaveOutcome>`) and wired it into `use-identity-scoped-domain.ts`'s existing write-queue worker: durability is set to `failed`/`saved` from inside the worker itself (never from an external `.then()`), so the queue's existing strict serialization gives ordering safety for free -- no separate sequence-number bookkeeping needed. An identity switch clears durability synchronously during render (comparing the current `generation` against the last-rendered one, React's "adjust state during render" pattern) rather than inside the hydration effect, to satisfy the `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rule while still clearing before the new generation's load resolves. `retry()` recomputes `context.getGeneration()` at call time, mirroring `saveNow`. All five domain providers (`goal-provider.tsx`, `custom-trick-provider.tsx`, `note-provider.tsx`, `user-profile-provider.tsx`, `practice-history-provider.tsx`) destructure `durability` from their primary `useIdentityScopedDomain` call only (never the second, parallel envelope instance each domain's `use-*-domain-sync` hook uses for cloud sync bookkeeping) and expose it through a new, separate `use<Domain>Durability()` context/hook, deliberately not folded into the existing `<Domain>Sync` contexts. Added 7 new cases to `use-identity-scoped-domain.test.tsx` (returned/thrown failure, clears on later success, `retry()` success, rapid-mutation ordering in both the later-succeeds and both-fail shapes, and identity-switch clearing that a stale write's completion cannot resurrect) plus one new parameterized provider-level test file per domain (`goal-provider.durability.test.tsx`, `custom-trick-provider.durability.test.tsx`, `note-provider.durability.test.tsx`, `user-profile-provider.durability.test.tsx`, `practice-history-provider.durability.test.tsx`), each forcing a real `AsyncStorage.setItem` rejection filtered by that domain's own storage-key constant so a concurrent sync-envelope write is unaffected. Updated `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s shared-persistence-protocol section to describe the new durability handle and correct its now-stale "not surfaced ... or retried" line. Verified: `yarn jest` across all five touched domains plus `src/shared/persistence` (181 tests), full `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (test/lint/typecheck/web-export) passed.

### [T247] Prevent logout when pending-data discard is not durable

- **Area:** Authentication / local persistence boundary
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Make “Discard and log out” advance only after every affected outbox has been durably cleared. Give imperative identity-scoped saves a typed outcome that distinguishes successful persistence, failure, and identity-generation supersession; propagate those outcomes through all five domain discard operations and the sync coordinator so the logout guard can retain the prompt and report actionable failure instead of signing out.
- **Exit criteria:** Fault-injection tests covering the shared persistence hook, all five domain discard contracts, coordinator aggregation, and logout flow prove that a returned save failure, thrown write failure, or superseded generation identifies the unsuccessful domain, preserves that domain's outbox in memory and on disk, keeps the logout prompt open with actionable error copy, and never calls `signOut`. The same integration coverage proves a later successful discard clears the current identity's outboxes before signing out, cannot replay discarded operations after remount, and cannot advance from a stale identity result.
- **Notes:** From D001. Corrective follow-up to completed T158, whose successful-write coverage did not exercise persistence failure. Preserved the existing FIFO write queue and generation guards; no second writer, no general local-persistence feedback redesign (T248's scope). Added `ScopedSaveOutcome` (`saved`/`failed`/`superseded`) to `src/shared/persistence/storage.ts`/`use-identity-scoped-domain.ts` so `saveNow` never silently succeeds a failed or superseded write; each domain's `discardPending` (profile, custom tricks, practice history, goals, notes) now persists first and only dispatches `outboxDiscarded` on a durable save, returning a `DomainDiscardOutcome`; `src/shared/sync/discard-outcome.ts` (new) aggregates the five outcomes with superseded > failed > discarded precedence via `Promise.allSettled`, replacing `sync-coordinator-provider.tsx`'s prior `Promise.all` fan-out (load-bearing: `all` would have lost which domains failed on the first rejection); `logout-guard.ts` gained a `discardFailedDomains` field and a three-way `discardFinished` transition (discarded → signingOut, failed → prompt with the failed domains named via new `src/features/auth/model/discard-failure-copy.ts` copy rendered in `logout-prompt.tsx`, superseded → idle); `use-logout-guard.ts`'s discard effect now branches on the outcome instead of unconditionally proceeding to sign-out, with an explicit generation re-check closing the microtask race window and a `.catch` handling a hypothetical rejecting coordinator. Fault-injection coverage: `storage.test.ts` (worker return value reaches the caller, ordering survives a throw); `use-identity-scoped-domain.test.tsx` (returned-failure, thrown-failure, and generation-flip-during-save all resolve without rejecting); all five `use-*-domain-sync.test.tsx` (discard resolves `discarded`/`superseded` correctly, plus a new setItem-rejection case proving in-memory and on-disk preservation); `discard-outcome.test.ts` (full precedence matrix); `sync-coordinator-provider.test.tsx` (a new case failing only the custom-trick envelope proves partial success: the note domain still clears while custom tricks survives in memory and on disk); `logout-guard.discard-isolation.test.tsx` (a new fully-wired case proves a failed discard leaves the operation pending, unchanged in memory and on disk, across a real unmount/remount); `logout-guard.test.ts`/`use-logout-guard.test.tsx`/`account-section.test.tsx` (reducer transitions, driver branching including a rejecting coordinator, and the real UI showing the actionable failure copy without ever calling `signOut`, with a second press then succeeding). Deviation from the exit criteria's literal wording: the fully-wired `logout-guard.discard-isolation.test.tsx` proves the failed-discard-preserved-across-remount leg, but not "successful discard, then no replay after a further remount" — that specific fully-wired scenario surfaced a pre-existing interaction unrelated to this ticket's contract, isolated to mounting two independent `IdentityHandleTestProvider` trees for "the same" identity against an always-failing fake transport (see that test file's own comment for the investigation and reasoning). That exact property (a successfully discarded operation is durably cleared in memory and on disk, and does not reappear across a real unmount/remount) is proven instead with the existing single-tree harness in `use-custom-trick-domain-sync.test.tsx`. Verified: full `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (test/lint/typecheck/web-export) passed.

### [T245] Hide the trick list's scrollbar

- **Area:** Tricks
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `trick-directory.tsx`'s scrollable list defaults `hideScrollIndicator` to `false` (lines 46, 86, 141), and on web `showsVerticalScrollIndicator` alone doesn't suppress the browser's native scrollbar (that needs a CSS-level hide on the underlying scroll container). Hide the scrollbar across every `TrickDirectory` call site (Tricks route, Trick goals, multi-trick sesh entry, Onboarding selection) while keeping scrolling itself functional.
- **Exit criteria:** Met. Confirmed in-browser (via DevTools computed styles and manual `scrollTop` writes) that every `TrickDirectory` scroll container renders `scrollbar-width: none` and no visible scrollbar track/thumb on the Tricks route, the Trick goals "Add goal" picker, multi-trick sesh entry (both ≥768px and <768px layouts), and Onboarding's selection step (both layouts), while each container still scrolls programmatically. `trick-directory.test.tsx` and dependent tests pass; full `yarn tsc --noEmit` and scoped `eslint` pass.
- **Notes:** From S117. The ticket description's premise was inaccurate for the pinned `react-native-web@0.21.2`: `showsVerticalScrollIndicator={false}` already emits a real CSS `scrollbar-width: none` / `::-webkit-scrollbar{display:none}` hide on the scrolling element, no extra web-only CSS needed. The actual gap was that `GoalTrickPicker` (`all-trick-goals-screen.tsx`) never passed `hideScrollIndicator`, so it was the only surface with a visible scrollbar. Fix: removed the `hideScrollIndicator` escape hatch entirely from `TrickDirectory` and made `showsVerticalScrollIndicator={false}` unconditional, dropping the prop from the three call sites that used it.

### [T244] Remove "Up next" label from the sesh home focused trick card

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `sesh.tsx`'s trick choice card renders `` `Up next · ${trick.descriptor ?? 'Practice trick'}` `` for the preferred trick (line 499). Drop the "Up next · " prefix so the card shows just the descriptor.
- **Exit criteria:** Met. `sesh.test.tsx`'s "keeps the plain visit picker behavior unchanged" case now asserts `text` does not contain `Up next`. Confirmed in-browser at `/app/sesh` that the focused trick card shows only its descriptor ("RANK 1 · BASIC"), with no "Up next" prefix.
- **Notes:** From S116. Single-line copy change; `isPreferred` is retained since it still drives `ChoiceMeta`'s `$preferred` styling prop.

### [T243] Show returning-user CTA copy to an authenticated visitor on the landing page

- **Area:** Landing page / Authentication
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `landing-cta.tsx`'s `LandingCta` picks `FIRST_TIME_COPY` vs `RETURNING_COPY` solely from `useCachedOnboardingStatus()`'s `hasCompletedOnboarding` flag (lines 32-35), with no reference to auth status — an authenticated user whose cached onboarding flag reads false still sees "Start your first sesh." Factor `useAuthState()` into the copy decision so an authenticated user sees the returning-user copy regardless of the onboarding flag.
- **Exit criteria:** Met. A new `landing-cta.test.tsx` case asserts an authenticated `authState` renders `RETURNING_COPY` (`Welcome back` / `Jump back into practice.` / `Back to practice`, no `Start your first sesh.`) even when `hasCompletedOnboarding` is false, plus regression cases pinning the guest, `initializing`, and `passwordRecovery` rows to `FIRST_TIME_COPY`. Confirmed in-browser against a local Supabase stack (a confirmed test user signed in on `http://localhost:8090`, since port 8081 was occupied by another agent's server and sign-in does not need the fixed callback origin): a signed-in visitor with a guest cache reading `hasCompletedOnboarding: false` sees "Jump back into practice." / "Back to practice" plus the `Daily Dama` + `Log out` header bar on `/?from=app`, not "Start your first sesh." Regression-checked in the same session: logging out reverts to the first-time yellow panel, and a signed-out guest with an onboarded cache still shows the returning sage panel. No new console warnings beyond the two known-benign ones; one transient `401` on the initial post-sign-in `profiles` fetch (retried successfully) is pre-existing sync behavior unrelated to this change.
- **Notes:** From S115. Root cause: `useCachedOnboardingStatus` only ever reads the guest-scoped profile key, so a signed-in user's onboarding flag was structurally invisible to it — not a routing bug in `LandingCta` itself. Fix per `.claude/plans/T243.md`: `isReturning = authState.status === 'authenticated' || hasCompletedOnboarding`, inline in the component (no extracted copy-selection helper — rejected as scope creep for a one-line predicate). `initializing` deliberately excluded to keep the Node prerender and first client render byte-identical (no hydration mismatch); `passwordRecovery` excluded to match T242's conservative handling of that status on this surface. Known, accepted trade-off (observe-and-report, not fixed here): a signed-in visitor sees a brief first-time-copy flash before Supabase's async `INITIAL_SESSION` resolves post-hydration, the same class of trade-off T242 accepted for its header bar; it read as an ordinary load-in during manual verification, not a jarring flash, so no follow-up ticket was opened.

### [T242] Show an authenticated header bar with logout on the landing page

- **Area:** Landing page / Authentication
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `landing-auth-cta.tsx` currently renders `null` whenever `authState.status !== 'guest'` (lines 41-43), a deliberate choice documented in its own comment ("renders nothing once a visitor is authenticated"). Reverse that: when authenticated, render a header bar showing "Daily Dama" and a logout action instead of the guest sign-up/sign-in buttons.
- **Exit criteria:** Met. A new `landing-auth-cta.test.tsx` case asserts that an authenticated `authState` renders the "Daily Dama" header bar with a `Log out` control (and not the sign-up/sign-in buttons), plus in-flight and error-path cases; the guest, `initializing`, and `passwordRecovery` cases still assert no `Log out` control. `landing-page.test.tsx`'s equivalent test was strengthened to assert the `Log out` control's presence, not just the guest buttons' absence. Confirmed in-browser against a local Supabase stack: a signed-in visitor reaching `/` (via `?from=app`, since a direct `/` hit with a guest-onboarded cache still redirects to `/app/home`) sees the `Daily Dama` + `Log out` bar, clicking `Log out` shows a brief "Logging out..." disabled state then swaps back to the guest Sign up/Sign in bar in place with no navigation, and a reload after logout stays signed out. Also confirmed the authenticated bar does not regress the measured 320px width (`scrollWidth === clientWidth === 320`, no overflow).
- **Notes:** From S114. `Required`: reverses a documented deliberate design decision and introduces a new authenticated-only UI surface plus a logout action on the landing route — the same class of hydration/redirect-ordering risk this project's Planning rules already reserved for T200's landing-page routing change. The ticket's suggestion to reuse `AccountSection`'s `useLogoutGuard` does not work here: that hook calls `useSyncCoordinatorStatus()`, which throws outside `SyncCoordinatorProvider`, and that provider (plus the five domain providers) is mounted only under `/app`, never above the landing route (T145 forbids mounting persistence providers there). Implemented per `.claude/plans/T242.md`: `LandingAuthCta` now calls `useAuthActions().signOut()` directly, extended its render matrix from a guest/non-guest boolean to the full four-way `AuthState` (`initializing`/`passwordRecovery` still render `null` to avoid a flash of the wrong bar on reload), and extracted `LOG_OUT_ERROR_COPY` out of `account-section.tsx` into `src/features/auth/model/log-out-error-copy.ts` so both logout surfaces share it. This bypasses `useLogoutGuard`'s unsynced-changes confirmation prompt as an accepted, explicit trade-off (not an oversight) — `pendingCount` is unknowable without mounting the domain providers on `/`; `signOut({ scope: 'local' })` does not purge local storage, so this is not data loss, but a follow-up giving the landing logout unsynced-changes parity is worth proposing separately, not folded into this ticket.

### [T241] Add drop shadow and thicker border to the shared text input

- **Area:** UI components
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `text-field.tsx`'s `InputRow` (line 68-76) currently has a flat `2px solid` border and no shadow, which the user finds visually underwhelming against the background-matching fill. Add a drop shadow in the style of the landing page's shadowed components (e.g. `landing-cta.tsx`'s `CtaPanel`, `8px 8px 0 ${AppColors.ink}` via an explicit `style` prop — see that file's inline comment on why the CSS `box-shadow` shorthand can't be used with styled-components/native), but lighter/less bold, and increase the border width.
- **Exit criteria:** Met. Confirmed in-browser on Sign up and Feedback that fields show a visible drop shadow and a thicker (3px) border, that focus still swaps the border to the accent color with the shadow unchanged, and that the multiline Feedback "Message" field's shadow follows its grown row. No new console warnings (no `shadow*` deprecation, confirming the CSS-shorthand path was avoided). No functional change to any input; `text-field.test.tsx` and all dependent screen tests pass (full `jest --runInBand`: 157 suites / 1900 tests).
- **Notes:** From S113. User explicitly said this does not need per-screen verification — one shared-component change covers every input app-wide since T223's rollout. Implemented per `.claude/plans/T241.md`'s pre-measured facts: the shadow is passed as an explicit `style` prop at the `InputRow` JSX call site (styled-components/native drops `.attrs({ style })` and lowers the CSS `box-shadow` shorthand to react-native-web's deprecated `shadow*` props); the shadow color reads `theme.text` via `useContext(ThemeContext)` rather than `useTheme()`, since several existing auth-screen tests mount without a `ThemeProvider` and `useTheme()` throws in that case. Fixed the array-style-rejecting test helpers in `text-field.test.tsx` and six auth test files (`password-input`, `account-security-screen`, `sign-up-screen`, `sign-in-screen`, `forgot-password-screen`, `reset-password-screen`) to flatten styles via `StyleSheet.flatten` instead of requiring a plain object — the plan's pre-measured 8-test blast radius, confirmed exactly matched by the full suite run.

### [T216] Add a branded template for the email-change confirmation

- **Area:** Authentication / Supabase
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `AccountSecurityScreen`'s `changeEmail` flow (`auth-provider.tsx:233`) calls `client.auth.updateUser({ email: newEmail })`, which sends GoTrue's confirm-new-email message — the only other Auth email actually reachable through the app today besides signup confirmation and password recovery. Unlike those two, it still uses Supabase's generic default copy: the hosted project's `mailer_templates_custom_contents.MAILER_TEMPLATES_EMAIL_CHANGE_CONTENT` is `false`. Add `supabase/templates/email_change.html` following `confirmation.html`/`recovery.html`'s existing pattern (same branding elements, fully inline-styled, no `<style>` block or external resources) and wire it in `config.toml` via `[auth.email.template.email_change]`, then apply it to the hosted project the same way T153.3/T195 did.
- **Exit criteria:** Local: Mailpit receives the new template for a local email-change request, matching `confirmation.html`/`recovery.html`'s branding (cream/paper background, ink border, hard drop shadow, red uppercase eyebrow, ink-background/yellow-text button). Hosted: per `supabase/README.md`'s "Troubleshooting: Mailpit isn't proof of hosted email delivery" note, verify the *hosted* project separately — either a real `changeEmail` request's delivered message or, more directly, Resend's own delivery log for that send — not Mailpit alone. Met: `supabase/templates/email_change.html` added following `confirmation.html`'s branded pattern and wired via `[auth.email.template.email_change]` in `config.toml`. Local: a real signup/confirm/`updateUser({ email })` cycle against the local stack delivered the template to Mailpit with all five branding elements intact and a valid `type=email_change` confirmation link. Hosted: applied via a targeted `PATCH /v1/projects/{ref}/config/auth` (`mailer_subjects_email_change`/`mailer_templates_email_change_content` only), then the account owner pointed the app at the hosted project, signed in with a real confirmed hosted account, and triggered a real `changeEmail` request through `AccountSecurityScreen`'s UI — the branded template was confirmed delivered via Resend's own delivery log (not Mailpit). See `supabase/HOSTED_DEV_CONFIG.md`'s 2026-09-04 update.
- **Notes:** Not from a BACKLOG_STORIES story — scoped directly during T212's investigation (2026-09-02), which surveyed every GoTrue email type this app can currently trigger. Invite, magic link, reauthentication, and the various notification emails (password/email/phone-changed, MFA enrolled/unenrolled, identity linked/unlinked) are excluded: none of them are reachable by any current app flow (no admin-invite, OTP, MFA, phone auth, or OAuth identity linking), and the password-changed notification is disabled outright in `config.toml`, not just unbranded. If any of those flows is added later, it needs this same treatment.

- **Description:** `sesh.tsx`'s completion summary renders `PrimaryButton` ("Repeat sesh") and `SecondaryButton` ("Start another sesh") back to back (lines 1051-1052), each sized to its own text content today. Make "Repeat sesh" render at the same width as "Start another sesh".

Both buttons now wrap in a new `CompletionActions` container (`align-items: stretch`) and both pass `fill` to `Button`, so each stretches to the container's cross-axis size, which itself shrink-wraps to the widest child ("Start another sesh") — the standard flexbox equal-width-stacked-buttons pattern. `sesh.test.tsx` gained a `pressableAncestorByVisibleText` helper (parallel to the existing `pressByVisibleText`) to assert `fill === true` on both buttons' resolved props, since react-test-renderer doesn't perform real layout to compare pixel widths directly.

### [T239] Persist target laces across open-ended seshes

- **Area:** Sesh / persistence
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `sesh.tsx`'s open-ended "Target laces (optional)" field (`targetLandingCount`, lines 434-444) is local component state only. Persist the user's last-entered value so it's pre-filled the next time they open sesh setup. Decide which existing persistence domain (per `AGENTS.md`'s three-domain boundary — user profile, custom tricks, practice history) owns this value, or whether it needs its own small addition to one of them.
- **Exit criteria:** Met. A model/persistence test asserts that setting a target-laces value in one open-ended sesh setup and later reopening sesh setup shows that same value pre-filled. Confirmed in-browser: set target laces to 5, leave the sesh, return to sesh setup, and see 5 pre-filled (and confirmed it survives a full page reload too).
- **Notes:** From S111. Planning found the ticket's premise already false: this persistence was implemented by T048 (PR #59) in the user-profile domain, inside `lastSesh.plan.targetLandingCount` — the domain question this ticket posed was already answered, and no new domain or field was added here. What this ticket actually delivered was the missing end-to-end regression coverage the exit criteria describe (nothing previously asserted the prefill behavior itself) plus a `selectInitialSeshPlan` selector extracted into `src/features/profile/model/user-profile.ts`, next to its inverse `createLastSesh`, so the route's `lastSesh → SeshPlan` derivation (previously inlined in `src/app/app/sesh/index.tsx`) is unit-testable and shared with the route rather than duplicated in a test. As a small side effect of writing that selector as total, an invalid `challengeDurationMs` query param (e.g. a hand-edited `plannedDurationMs` that resolves to a non-integer or out-of-range minute count) now falls back to the last-sesh-derived plan instead of throwing a `RangeError` out of the route render. Known residual gap, left open: an intervening **timed** sesh still clears the remembered open-ended target, because it lives inside the `lastSesh.plan` discriminated union. Fixing that requires widening the `profiles_last_sesh_shape` CHECK constraint in `supabase/migrations/20260822193659_add_profiles_and_user_trick_states.sql`, updating the Supabase test suites, and an owner-authorized hosted migration — out of scope for this Medium ticket, whose exit criteria this gap does not touch. Recommend the owner file it as its own story if it should be fixed.

### [T219] Show the signup invite in the note prompt's slot on a first-ever guest Sesh

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** In `sesh.tsx`'s `CompletedSesh`, for a first-time guest's first-ever eligible Sesh completion (`showSignupInvite && signupInviteIsFirstOccurrence`), suppress `SeshNotePrompt` and render `InlineSignupInvite` in its place instead — after `LandingHistory`/the goal panel, before the "Repeat sesh" `PrimaryButton` — rather than its current position right after `SequenceSteps` (T210's placement, immediately below the trick name). Every other completion (non-first-occurrence, non-eligible, or ineligible for the invite at all) keeps rendering `SeshNotePrompt` unchanged in that same position.
- **Exit criteria:** Met. `sesh.test.tsx` has "renders the first-occurrence inline invite after the lace timeline and before \"Repeat sesh,\" replacing the note prompt (T219)" (asserts `SeshNotePrompt`'s "Add a note" does not render, and the invite renders after the lace-timeline metrics and before "Repeat sesh") and "keeps rendering SeshNotePrompt unchanged for a non-first-occurrence invite (T219)" (asserts the note prompt still renders, before "Repeat sesh"); full file passes. `CompletedSesh` now renders `{showSignupInvite && signupInviteIsFirstOccurrence ? <InlineSignupInvite /> : promptForNote ? <SeshNotePrompt ... /> : null}` in the single slot after `LandingHistory`/`SeshGoalsPanel` and before the "Repeat sesh" `PrimaryButton`; the old post-`SequenceSteps` `InlineSignupInvite` render (T210's placement) was removed. `yarn tsc --noEmit` and a scoped `yarn eslint` pass on the changed files (one pre-existing, unrelated `array-type` warning in `sesh.test.tsx`). Live-verified against the local Expo web dev server with a headless Playwright script (guest identity seeded with a completed-onboarding profile directly in `localStorage`, a direct-practice open-ended Sesh on a standard trick run to first-ever completion): the wrap-up screen's text order is the lace timeline (`Lace timeline`), then "Save this sesh for good.", then "Repeat sesh", with no "Add a note" text anywhere on the page.

Supersedes T210's placement (right after the trick name/`SequenceSteps`) — moves the invite further down the screen, replacing the note prompt's slot rather than sitting above the summary metrics. "First-time guest" scope is unchanged from T210 (first-ever completion only). The every-5th-occurrence `PostSeshSignupInvite` overlay (T201/T211) is unaffected.

### [T212] Make the branded confirmation email fix actually hold for real signups

- **Area:** Authentication / Supabase
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** T195's `PATCH /v1/projects/{ref}/config/auth` fix to the hosted project's confirmation-email template passed live verification on 2026-09-01, but a real signup now again receives raw/unstyled HTML. Diagnose why the hosted config didn't hold — a leading, unconfirmed hypothesis is that the fix was applied as a one-off runtime PATCH rather than through the repository's declarative `supabase/config.toml`, so a later config push or project change reverted it — and land a fix durable enough to survive normal operations, not just a repeated PATCH.
- **Exit criteria:** A real signup, in the environment the user actually tests against, receives the branded confirmation email (cream/paper background, ink border, hard drop shadow, red uppercase eyebrow, ink-background/yellow-text button) in the actual inbox — verified a second time. `supabase/RESEND_DNS_AUTH.md` documents what caused the previous fix to not hold, so a third recurrence doesn't require re-diagnosing from scratch. Met: the hosted config was never actually broken. A read-only `GET /v1/projects/ylyoqswauqyesuwmetnl/config/auth` found `mailer_templates_confirmation_content` matching `supabase/templates/confirmation.html` byte-for-byte with `mailer_templates_custom_contents.MAILER_TEMPLATES_CONFIRMATION_CONTENT: true` — T195's fix had held the whole time. The "raw/unstyled" report traced instead to local Mailpit testing being mistaken for hosted verification: the user confirmed Resend's own delivery log for a real `t197v...@mailinator.com` signup shows a `Sent -> Delivered` event with the fully branded HTML body intact (cream/paper background, ink border, hard drop shadow, red uppercase eyebrow, ink-background/yellow-text button), which is authoritative proof of what was actually sent through the real pipeline. Considered and rejected as the fix: `supabase config push`, which the CLI does support for declaratively syncing `config.toml` to the hosted project (the durable-fix direction this ticket's own hypothesis pointed at) -- but `config.toml`'s `[auth.email.smtp]` block is deliberately commented out (Resend's custom SMTP was wired directly on the hosted project via its own Supabase Integration wizard, never captured in `config.toml`, per `supabase/RESEND_DNS_AUTH.md`), so a full push would have pushed SMTP as disabled and silently broken Resend delivery entirely. No config or code change was needed or made.
- **Notes:** From S094. `Required` for the same reason T195/T197 were, but the runtime diagnosis this time found no actual hosted-config regression -- the prior "twice without success" reports were both looking at Mailpit, which this project's own docs already flag as non-authoritative for hosted verification (`supabase/README.md`) but which hadn't stopped two rounds of false-negative confusion in practice. Documented in a new "Troubleshooting: Mailpit isn't proof of hosted email delivery" subsection of `supabase/README.md` and a new entry in `supabase/RESEND_DNS_AUTH.md`, both cross-referencing this ticket, so a fourth recurrence doesn't require re-diagnosing from scratch either.

### [T207] Acknowledge a newly verified account on first arrival at Home

- **Area:** Home / Authentication
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `auth-callback-screen.tsx`'s default branch (the post-signup-email-verification redirect) and `sign-in-screen.tsx`'s redirect both land on `/app/home` via `router.replace` with no acknowledgment that the account was created and the user is signed in. Have the callback screen's default-branch redirect carry a one-time signal (e.g. a query param) that `home.tsx` reads on mount to show a dismissible success banner, then discards — this path only fires for a just-verified new account, not for `sign-in-screen.tsx`'s routine sign-in redirect, which is unaffected.
- **Exit criteria:** `home.test.tsx` (or equivalent) passes with a case asserting the banner renders when arriving via the new-account signal and does not render on a plain mount; confirmed in-browser that completing signup + email verification shows the banner once on Home, dismissing it (or navigating away and back) does not re-show it, and a regular sign-in shows no banner.
- **Notes:** From S089. `auth-callback-screen.tsx`'s default branch now redirects with `router.replace({ pathname: '/app/home', params: { newAccount: '1' } })` instead of the bare string target; `sign-in-screen.tsx`'s two `/app/home` redirects are untouched. `home.tsx` reads `newAccount` once via a lazy `useState` initializer (so a same-tick re-render of `useLocalSearchParams`'s fresh object identity can't re-derive it), then strips the param with a follow-up `router.replace('/app/home')` guarded by a ref so it only fires once. The banner is a small one-off dismissible card (not the `sesh.tsx` `CelebrationBanner`, which auto-dismisses and has no user-dismiss affordance) rendered above the onboarding/`DailyHome` content so it can show even mid-onboarding. New coverage in `home.test.tsx`: a plain mount shows no banner and never calls `router.replace`; mounting with the `newAccount` signal shows the banner, strips the param, and dismissing it removes it. `auth-callback-screen.test.tsx`'s three affected assertions were updated to expect the new object-shaped redirect target. `yarn tsc --noEmit` and `yarn lint` both pass; `yarn expo export --platform web` succeeds. Live-verified via a headless Playwright script against `yarn expo start --web`: a plain `/app/home` mount shows no banner; `/app/home?newAccount=1` shows it and strips the query param from the URL; clicking Dismiss hides it; reloading afterward keeps it hidden (no re-trigger).

### [T206] Fix Home rendering blank after logging into a populated account

- **Area:** Home / Authentication
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Logging into an existing, data-populated account redirects to Home, but nothing renders — and a manual refresh doesn't fix it either. This needs runtime diagnosis (not a known fix) through the post-sign-in identity-change path: `UserProfileProvider`/practice-history hydration timing, whether the sync coordinator's initial pull has settled before Home reads its data, and whether the blank render is a silent failure rather than genuinely empty state.
- **Exit criteria:** Signing into a real, existing account with practice history/tricks/goals data lands on a fully rendered Home screen showing that data, both on the initial post-login redirect and after a manual refresh — verified end-to-end against a real account.
- **Notes:** From S087. Confirmed root cause has three parts, all in `use-guest-import.ts`'s upload-phase driver, none of which are `home.tsx` or hydration-timing bugs as originally suspected: (1) the effect's own dependency array included `state.deduplications`, which changes mid-flight via the `existingMergePrepared` dispatch this same effect issues — React runs the *previous* effect invocation's cleanup (`cancelled = true`) before the re-invocation's early-return-via-`importStartedRef` guard kicks in, silently cancelling the still-in-flight original closure right after its own push actually succeeded, so no terminal reducer action was ever dispatched; this was the dominant live-repro'd cause, found only by tracing a real `'existing'`-target merge against the local Supabase stack, not by static reading. (2) The catch block's `domainUploadSettled` dispatch for `'profile'` only, on any thrown mid-upload error, could never satisfy `allDomainsSettled` across all 5 `SYNC_DOMAINS`. (3) `runEmptyGuestImport`'s non-throwing `{ status: 'paused', ... }` return (an ordinary per-operation push failure/conflict, not an exception) was silently ignored by the driver's `result.status !== 'completed'` early return. All three left `guestImportReducer` parked at `'uploading'` forever, and `isGuestImportPendingResolution` treats any `'uploading'` status as unconditionally pending — `home.tsx` renders `null` for as long as that holds, durably (the guest-import record persists via `useIdentityScopedDomain`), matching "manual refresh doesn't fix it either" exactly. Fixed: removed `state.deduplications` from the upload effect's dependency array (mirroring why `state.progress` was already excluded) and read it fresh via `latestRef` instead; added a new `uploadFailed` reducer action (mirrors `inventoryFailed`'s reset-to-`'idle'` shape, deliberately not a `domainUploadSettled` shortcut, which would vacuously mark a real failure as `'completed'` via `operationsSettled`'s empty-list fallback) and dispatch it from both the catch block and the `'paused'`-result branch. New coverage: `guest-import.test.ts` (`uploadFailed` reducer transitions), `use-guest-import.test.tsx` (a mid-upload `readExistingAccountSnapshot` failure now resolves to `'idle'` with an error, plus a pre-existing test's bare-stub transport mock — which had been unwittingly relying on the pre-fix wedge to stay parked at `'uploading'` for its own assertion — updated to a non-throwing stub), and a new `src/app/app/__tests__/home.guest-import-upload-failure.test.tsx` integration test (confirmed to fail against pre-fix code, passes post-fix) proving `HomeScreen` never renders neither-onboarding-nor-`DailyHome` content, both immediately and after a simulated remount. Live-verified end-to-end against the local Supabase Auth stack (Playwright driving real network calls, no mocks): seeded a real, populated `t206-existing-account@example.com` account (real signup via the admin API, full onboarding, one logged practice session) in one browser context; in a second, guest-touched context (partial/full guest onboarding, creating real local guest data), logged into that same account via the real sign-in form. Pre-fix, this reproduced the reported bug exactly (blank `Home`, confirmed stuck at `guestImportState.status === 'uploading'` for 30+ seconds via `localStorage`, unaffected by a real page reload). Post-fix, `Home` renders `DailyHome` fully (practice stats, last-sesh card, rotation list) immediately after the post-login redirect and again identically after a manual `page.reload()`. `yarn test --runInBand` (869 suites), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` all pass. Local Supabase stack reset (`db reset`) afterward to remove the fixture account.

### [T198] Stop the guest-import status panel from ever rendering visible UI

- **Area:** Guest conversion / onboarding
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `GuestImportPreview` (`src/features/guest-import/components/guest-import-preview.tsx`) still renders an "Import in progress..." panel while `state.status === 'uploading'`, and is still unconditionally mounted in `profile/index.tsx` — every other status branch already returns `null`. This directly contradicts T186/T186.1's "internally invisible" goal and is the most likely source of both the visible import text the user reports and the "elements painted very quickly" flash before landing on onboarding (see T199 for the onboarding-redirect part of that same report).
- **Exit criteria:** `guest-import-preview.test.tsx` passes with no case that renders visible text; confirmed in-browser that signing up with existing local data shows no import-status text or panel anywhere at any point during the flow. Met: confirmed every remaining `GuestImportPreview` status branch (`idle`/`cancelled`/`completed`/`inventorying`/`awaitingConfirmation`) already returned `null`, with only `uploading` still rendering the visible "Import in progress..." panel -- so the component was deleted entirely (along with `guest-import-preview.test.tsx`, which tested the now-nonexistent panel) rather than left as a dead shell, and its mount removed from `profile/index.tsx`. Verified in-browser against a running dev server (Playwright, no local Supabase signup required since the component and its only visible-rendering branch no longer exist in the bundle) that neither `/app/profile` nor `/` render "Import in progress" text anywhere, with no console errors.
- **Notes:** From S079 (the visible-UI/flash symptom) and S088. Since every remaining status branch of `GuestImportPreview` already returns `null`, the straightforward fix is deleting the component (and its mount in `profile/index.tsx`) entirely rather than keeping a dead shell around — confirm no other branch is reachable before doing so.

### [T189] Fix logout destination and reset session-scoped app state

- **Area:** Authentication / Landing
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `account-section.tsx` already calls `router.replace('/')` on successful logout (T175), but `/`'s `useRedirectOnboardedVisitorHome` immediately redirects back to `/app/home` via `use-cached-onboarding-status.ts`, which — like `read-cached-theme-background.ts` — still reads the legacy bare `USER_PROFILE_STORAGE_KEY` instead of the identity-scoped guest key T156.2 introduced, so both helpers see a stale/wrong-identity snapshot after logout. Update both helpers to read the identity-scoped guest key first with a bare-key fallback, mirroring `CachedAppThemeProvider`'s `readCachedGuestTheme`/`useCachedGuestTheme` (T170).
- **Exit criteria:** `use-cached-onboarding-status.test.ts` and `read-cached-theme-background.test.ts` (or equivalents) pass, including a test asserting a post-logout guest identity with no completed guest onboarding reads `hasCompletedOnboarding: false` and the default theme even when a stale bare-key snapshot exists; confirmed in-browser that logging out from an account with a custom theme lands on the landing page showing the default theme. Met: `read-cached-onboarding-status.ts`, `read-cached-theme-background.ts`, and `use-cached-onboarding-status.ts` (native branch) now read the identity-scoped guest key first, falling back to the bare legacy key, mirroring `read-cached-guest-theme.ts`'s established pattern. New regression tests were added to `read-cached-onboarding-status.test.ts`, `read-cached-theme-background.test.ts`, and `use-cached-onboarding-status.test.tsx` covering the fallback order and the exact post-logout stale-bare-key scenario; all pass. Manually verified against a local Supabase stack: signed up a real account, completed onboarding with the Blue custom theme selected, then logged out via `AccountSection` — landed on `/` showing the default cream theme and "Start your first Sesh" guest copy (no redirect to `/app/home`), confirmed again on a hard refresh of `/` (no blue splash flash).
- **Notes:** From S070, follow-up on T175. Preserved T145's hydration-flicker-free first paint on `/` — the existing `useSyncExternalStore` hydration-mismatch guard in `use-cached-onboarding-status.ts` was untouched; only the storage key(s) read after that boundary changed. This was the same "stale bare-key read" gap T170's own Notes had already flagged as an undiscovered follow-up candidate. `landing-cta.tsx` also consumes `useCachedOnboardingStatus` and got the fix for free with no separate change.

### [T185] Synchronize password-visibility toggles and use an icon instead of text

- **Area:** Authentication UI
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `password-input.tsx` currently owns its own `isVisible` state per instance, so sign-up's password/confirm-password toggles operate independently, and the toggle renders as `ToggleText` ("Show"/"Hide"). Lift visibility into shared state across all `PasswordInput` instances rendered together on one form (sign-up's password+confirm, reset-password's new+confirm, Account Security's current/new/confirm), and replace the text toggle with an eye-open/eye-closed icon.
- **Exit criteria:** `password-input.test.tsx` passes, including a test asserting that toggling visibility on one `PasswordInput` updates a sibling `PasswordInput`'s visibility when both share the new visibility context/state; updated tests on sign-up, reset-password, and Account Security confirm the shared toggle behavior end-to-end and that the control renders as an icon with an accessible label (not "Show"/"Hide" text). Met: `password-input.test.tsx` (6/6, including the new sibling-sync test and a not-shared-outside-a-provider control test) and the updated sign-up, reset-password, and account-security screen tests all pass; full auth suite 320/320; `yarn tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Notes:** From S066, follow-up on T171. Sign-in only has a single password field, so shared-visibility state there is a no-op — no behavior change on that screen (its own test file re-verified unchanged). Implementation: new `password-visibility-context.tsx` exports `PasswordVisibilityProvider` and `usePasswordVisibilityContext`; `PasswordInput` reads the context when present and otherwise falls back to its own local `useState`, so unwrapped fields (sign-in, account-deletion) keep prior per-instance behavior with no screen changes needed there. Sign-up wraps its password+confirm pair, reset-password wraps new+confirm, and Account Security wraps only its current/new/confirm change-password group — its separate change-email "Password" field is intentionally left unwrapped (single field, no sibling to sync with). `ToggleText` is replaced with an `EyeIcon` (`react-native-svg` `Path`/`Circle`/`Line`, open eye vs. slashed eye reflecting current visibility) whose stroke color resolves via `useContext(ThemeContext)` with an `AppColors.ink` fallback (not the `useTheme` hook, which throws outside a `ThemeProvider`) — same theming precedent T184 established for the surrounding field chrome. `accessibilityLabel`/`accessibilityRole` on the toggle are unchanged ("Show \<label\>"/"Hide \<label\>", role `button`), so the accessible-label exit criterion is met without new UI copy.

### [T184] Make auth-screen text and background theme-responsive, not just the button

- **Area:** Authentication UI
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Sign-up, sign-in, forgot-password, and reset-password all render their primary form via `FormScroll` directly, bypassing `<Screen>` (which already resolves `theme.background`) — only their initializing/redirecting placeholder branches use `<Screen>`. Wrap each screen's primary-form render in `<Screen>` (or otherwise apply the resolved theme background to `FormScroll`) without reintroducing T145's hydration-flicker regression, and move `FieldInput` (duplicated across all four screens) and `password-input.tsx`'s `InputRow`/`Input`/`ToggleText` off hardcoded `AppColors.paper`/`AppColors.ink` onto theme tokens, matching the pattern `account-security-screen.tsx` (T172) already uses.
- **Exit criteria:** Component tests for all four `/auth/*` screens and `password-input.test.tsx` pass, including an assertion that each screen's rendered background and field colors resolve from a non-default theme when one is set; confirmed in-browser with a seeded custom theme that sign-up, sign-in, forgot-password, and reset-password all show themed background and field colors, not only the button. Met: `password-input.test.tsx` and all four `/auth/*` screen test files now include a themed-render assertion (component-level `StyledThemeProvider` with a distinct-hex `DefaultTheme`) checking the primary form's `Screen` background and field background/border/text colors resolve from the active theme, not defaults; all pass (316/316 auth-suite tests). Confirmed in-browser via a Playwright script against a local Expo web dev server: seeding `guest:daily-dama:user-profile` with a custom `themeBackgroundColor`/`themeAccentColor` and loading `/auth/sign-up`, `/auth/sign-in`, `/auth/forgot-password`, and `/auth/reset-password` all rendered the seeded background and matching field border/text colors (not just the button); `reset-password`'s primary form (gated behind a real recovery session) is covered by its automated theme test, while its reachable placeholder branch already showed the themed background in the same run.
- **Notes:** From S065, follow-up on T170. `CachedAppThemeProvider` (T170) already supplies the resolved theme to this route group — this ticket is about screens actually consuming it, not a provider change. Reuse T170's `useCachedGuestTheme`/hydration-mismatch guard reasoning rather than inventing a new pre-hydration path. Also updates `password-input.tsx`, which S066/T185 also touches (visibility-toggle behavior only, not color) — sequence so the two don't collide on the same lines in-flight. Implementation: `password-input.tsx`'s `InputRow`/`Input`/`ToggleText` now read `theme.background`/`theme.text` instead of `AppColors.paper`/`AppColors.ink`. Each of sign-up, sign-in, and forgot-password wraps its primary-form return in a new outer `<Screen>` (default `stretch` align, matching account-security-screen.tsx's precedent) and converts its local `FieldInput` styled component to theme tokens plus a `.attrs(({ theme }) => ({ placeholderTextColor: theme.textMuted }))` placeholder color, removing the previously-inline `placeholderTextColor={AppColors.textSubtle}` prop on the email field. `reset-password-screen.tsx` only needed the `<Screen>` wrap (it has no hand-rolled `FieldInput`; both fields are `PasswordInput`). `ErrorText` (`AppColors.red`) stayed unchanged in all four screens, per the plan's explicit scope boundary. No provider or hydration-gating code changed.

### [T183] Present the signup re-invite as a dismissible modal

- **Area:** Sesh / guest invitation
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `PostSeshSignupInvite` (`sesh.tsx`) currently renders inline as the last item in the wrap-up screen's column. Convert it to a modal overlay the user must dismiss or act on, reusing the absolute-fill `View` overlay pattern `rank-up-celebration-modal.tsx` and `logout-prompt.tsx` already establish in this codebase (no RN `Modal` precedent here) rather than introducing a new modal mechanism.
- **Exit criteria:** `sesh.test.tsx` passes, including an assertion that `showSignupInvite` renders the invite as an overlay (not inline wrap-up content) and that both "Sign up" and "Dismiss" close it, for both the first-ever-Sesh case and the every-5th-session case. Met: `PostSeshSignupInvite` now renders as an `InviteOverlay` (absolute-fill, matching `rank-up-celebration-modal.tsx`/`logout-prompt.tsx`) rendered as a sibling after `CompletedSesh`'s `ScrollArea`, not inside `ActiveContent`. New tests assert the invite node is absent from the `ScrollView` subtree and present at the top level, for both the first-ever-Sesh case and the every-5th-session (6th completion) case, and that pressing either "Sign up" (now `router.push`-driven instead of `Link asChild`, so its `onPress` can also dismiss) or "Dismiss" closes the overlay in both cases. `sesh.test.tsx` passes (`yarn test --runInBand --runTestsByPath src/features/sesh/components/sesh.test.tsx`), plus `yarn tsc --noEmit` and `yarn eslint` on both changed files (0 errors).
- **Notes:** From S064. Pure presentation change — `isRegularSessionInviteEligible`'s eligibility logic (T166) and its dismiss/sign-up handlers are unchanged. Switched the "Sign up" action from `Link asChild` to `useRouter().push`, since `Link asChild` overrides the child's own `onPress` (documented precedent: `trick-directory.tsx`'s `RowAction` comment), which would have prevented composing navigation with dismissal.

### [T175] Send the user to the landing page after logout

- **Area:** Authentication
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `account-section.tsx`'s `handleLogOut` calls `signOut()` but performs no navigation today — the user stays wherever they were (typically Profile) once `AuthProvider`'s listener flips them to `guest`. Add an explicit `router.replace('/')` on successful sign-out.
- **Exit criteria:** `account-section.test.tsx` passes, including a test asserting a successful sign-out navigates to `/`. Confirmed: `account-section.test.tsx` passes (24 tests), including a new test asserting `router.replace("/")` is called once sign-out completes successfully, and two new tests confirming it is not called on a failed sign-out or a cancelled logout prompt. Full `yarn tsc --noEmit` and scoped `eslint` also pass.
- **Notes:** From S055. T158 (guarded logout prompt) landed first and moved the actual `signOut()` call out of `account-section.tsx`'s own `handleLogOut` into the UI-agnostic `useLogoutGuard` hook (`use-logout-guard.ts`). Rather than adding `expo-router` to that hook (which would require mocking it in `use-logout-guard.test.tsx` too and couples a deliberately UI-agnostic hook to navigation), the redirect is implemented in `account-section.tsx` itself: a `useEffect` watches the guard's `state.status` for the `signingOut -> idle` transition with `errorCategory` still `undefined`, which only happens once `signOut()` has actually succeeded, and calls `router.replace("/")` there. A failed sign-out also returns to `idle` but carries an `errorCategory`, so the effect correctly leaves the user in place to see the error and retry.

### [T179] Require current-password confirmation before changing email

- **Area:** Authentication
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `account-security-screen.tsx`'s change-email form only collects a new email today; `changeEmail` on `AuthProvider` takes `(newEmail: string)` with no password parameter. Add a current-password field to the same form, change `changeEmail`'s signature to accept and verify it, and block the change with a clear message on an incorrect password.
- **Exit criteria:** `auth-provider.test.tsx` and `account-security-screen.test.tsx` pass, including tests asserting an incorrect current password blocks the email change with a clear error and never calls the underlying Supabase update, while a correct one proceeds as today. Confirmed: both files pass, including three new `changeEmail` provider tests mirroring `changePassword`'s sign-in-then-update ordering, wrong-password, and offline-sign-in-step cases, plus new component tests for an empty current password and a wrong current password on the email form. All 293 auth-domain tests and full `yarn tsc --noEmit` pass.
- **Notes:** From S060. Resolved during refinement: one combined form (password + new email submitted together), not a two-step re-auth-then-change flow. `Required` because it changes `AuthProvider`'s shared `changeEmail` action signature. Extends T154.1. `changeEmail` now re-signs-in with the account's own email and the submitted current password (mirroring `changePassword`'s existing pattern) before calling `updateUser`, short-circuiting with the sign-in error on a wrong password. The new password field is labeled "Password" (not "Current password") in the Change-email section, to avoid an `accessibilityLabel` collision with the Change-password section's own "Current password" field, since both are visible in the DOM at once.

### [T160] Expose synchronization health and record-level conflict recovery

- **Area:** Synchronization UX
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Make pending work, failures, offline state, freshness, retry, and conflicts understandable without allowing one bad record to stop unrelated synchronization.
- **Exit criteria:** Confirmed by completed T160.1-T160.3: a trustworthy serializable sync-status model with a Profile retry surface; per-record conflict resolution for profile, custom-trick, goal, and note domains scoped strictly to its own record; and a cross-domain fault-injection suite proving a stale-revision or tombstone conflict on one record never blocks or resurrects an unrelated record, retries stay idempotent after a lost acknowledgement, and two independently-mounted browser fixtures converge on identical domain state after a genuine conflict. The full repository verifier passes all four steps (tests, lint, strict typecheck, web export). Native validation is deferred by active web-only project scope.
- **Notes:** Umbrella from S037. Complete T160.1-T160.3. Depends on T157. Child completion evidence remains in this file under T160.1-T160.3; no additional product code is required for umbrella closure.

### [T160.3] Prove stale revisions and tombstones cannot block or resurrect unrelated data

- **Area:** Synchronization conflict testing
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add a cross-domain fault-injection suite for concurrent edits, stale revisions, delete-vs-edit tombstones, lost acknowledgements, and conflicts interleaved with valid operations.
- **Exit criteria:** The suite proves only the conflicting record pauses, accepted operations advance and apply server metadata, stale clients cannot resurrect tombstoned goals/notes/custom tricks, retries remain idempotent, and conflict resolution converges two browser fixtures on identical domain state. Confirmed: filled per-domain parity gaps (profile's existing sibling-conflict coverage, a goals/notes lost-acknowledgement idempotency test, and custom-tricks' hard-delete-vs-edit shape -- a retryable 'failed' outcome, never a resurrecting insert, unlike goals/notes' SyncConflictRecord tombstone-conflict shape); added a fully-wired cross-domain integration suite proving one conflicting record pauses while interleaved valid edits across every other domain apply in the same run and resolving it disturbs no sibling; and added a two-live-browser convergence suite sharing one simulated cloud per domain that proves concurrent edits, a genuine stale-revision conflict, 'keep-server' resolution, and an out-of-band lost-acknowledgement replay all converge to bit-for-bit identical domain state with zero remaining pending/conflicting operations. Focused sync suites and the full verifier pass all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export). Native validation is deferred by active project scope.
- **Notes:** From S037. Depends on T160.2 and T155.8.

### [T159] Merge guest data into new or existing accounts
