### [T162.1] Deploy and evaluate a report-only Content Security Policy

- **Area:** Web security headers
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add a minimally permissive report-only CSP for the static Expo/Vercel app, covering only required Daily Dama, Supabase, Vercel Analytics, font/asset, and development origins; audit raw-HTML sinks and sanitize or remove any path that could inject note/custom-trick content.
- **Exit criteria:** The production-like web export serves the report-only policy, automated header checks reject unlisted script/connect/frame origins, browser walkthroughs cover Auth and core app routes with no unexplained violations, and tests prove untrusted note/custom-trick text cannot become executable HTML. Confirmed: `vercel.json` serves `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` on every route (script-src hash-pinned to the exported hydration bootstrap, style-src unsafe-inline for the baked atomic CSS, connect-src scoped to self plus the *.supabase.co wildcard, frame-src/frame-ancestors none). `yarn security:check-headers` (node --test against a fresh production-like export served by security/scripts/build-and-serve.mjs) passes 4/4: header present and byte-identical across sampled routes, matches an independently written expected allow-list with no bare wildcard, and the script-src hash matches a live recomputation from the export. `yarn test:e2e:security` (Playwright, e2e/security/) passes 15/15: no securitypolicyviolation events across all six /auth/* routes and eight core app/sandbox routes (including /app/stats/trick-viz-sandbox and /app/stats/sesh-sequence-sandbox), plus a positive-control test proving a definitely-unlisted origin fetch is flagged as a connect-src violation, confirming the header is genuinely active end to end. security/dangerous-html-audit.test.ts is a permanent regression guard confirming exactly one raw-HTML sink exists (src/app/+html.tsx, a fixed non-interpolated string). Added hostile-markup rendering regression tests in all-notes-screen.test.tsx, trick-directory.test.tsx, and sesh.test.tsx proving note-body, custom-trick-name, and sequence-step-name text renders as literal text, never executable HTML. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps. Report-only only; enforcement is T162.2.
- **Notes:** From S041 and roadmap client/web security. Depends on the final web Auth/sync origin set. Start report-only; enforcement is T162.2.

### [T162.2] Enforce CSP and the remaining defensive web headers

- **Area:** Web deployment security
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Promote the validated CSP to enforcement and add HSTS, `frame-ancestors`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, and a restrictive `Permissions-Policy` through the deployed Vercel/static-host configuration.
- **Exit criteria:** Automated checks against a production preview confirm every HTML route returns the exact enforced headers, an inline/unlisted script and framing attempt are blocked, Auth callbacks and Supabase requests still work, and browser console/network inspection shows no new policy failures across the core web flow. Confirmed: `vercel.json` flips `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` to enforcing `Content-Security-Policy` (byte-identical directive value carried over from T162.1) and adds `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains` (no preload -- a deliberate, separate future opt-in), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, and a fully restrictive `Permissions-Policy` denying every standard gated browser feature (confirmed by grep: zero camera/microphone/geolocation/fullscreen/clipboard/share/sensor API usage anywhere in `src/`, including `src/features/trick-viz`). `frame-ancestors 'none'` remains a CSP directive only (enforced since T162.1); no redundant standalone header was added. No Vercel CLI/project link exists in this environment, so per T162.1's own precedent, `security/scripts/build-and-serve.mjs`'s local server (real `dist` export served through the real `vercel.json` headers/rewrites) stands in for "production preview" in all automated evidence below; a live-preview header spot-check remains a manual, owner-side step. `yarn security:check-headers` passes 5/5: the CSP assertions from T162.1 plus a new assertion that all four defensive headers are present, byte-identical across sampled routes, and match an independently written expected-value map. `yarn test:e2e:security` passes 17/17: specs 01/02 updated and re-verified under enforcement (spec 02 now also asserts the recorded connect-src violation's `disposition` is `"enforce"`, not `"report"`); new spec 03 (`e2e/security/03-enforcement-blocks-inline-script-and-framing.spec.ts`) proves an unlisted inline script is refused execution (recorded violation plus the script's side effect never lands) and that framing `/app/home` in an iframe is blocked by `frame-ancestors` -- both passed on first attempt, no flakiness observed. `yarn test:e2e:auth` passes 33/33 after the header flip, confirming Auth callbacks and Supabase requests are unaffected. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test/lint/typecheck/web-export). Strict-Transport-Security's runtime effect (an actual HTTPS upgrade) is structurally unverifiable over this local HTTP harness -- only header presence/value was checked automatically; a real HTTPS deploy check remains a manual step.
- **Notes:** From S041. Depends on T162.1. Preserve the static Expo Router rewrites documented in `DEPLOYMENT_HANDOFF.md` and `vercel.json`.


### [T157.1] Define durable outbox and mutation-identity contracts

- **Area:** Synchronization persistence
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Define serializable mutation operations, stable operation IDs, per-domain cursors, acknowledgements, retry metadata, and atomic versioned envelopes that own local domain state and its outbox together.
- **Exit criteria:** Unit tests prove a local state change and its operation persist atomically, survive reload, retain stable IDs across retries, preserve FIFO order, reject malformed envelopes, and cannot be loaded or acknowledged under another identity. Confirmed: new `src/shared/sync/` module (`domain.ts`, `outbox-operation.ts`, `sync-envelope.ts`) is pure contract/persistence-layer code with zero imports from `src/features/*` or `src/shared/supabase/*`. `SyncEnvelope<State>` is deliberately one instance per domain (not a shared cross-domain structure), pairing one domain's state with its FIFO `outbox` and `cursor` so `saveSyncEnvelope` writes both in a single `setItem` call — proven by a test asserting `storage.setItem` is called exactly once and the persisted blob contains both the new state and the new outbox entry. `outbox-operation.test.ts` (18 cases) table-tests `isOutboxOperation` against every malformed shape independently. `sync-envelope.test.ts` (29 cases) proves atomic pairing, reload round-trip through the real `AsyncStorage` jest mock, stable `operationId` across two `recordOutboxOperationAttempt` retries, FIFO order surviving mutation/serialization/acknowledgement/attempts (including composition with the existing `createWriteQueue` so a later-enqueued save always wins), rejection of nine distinct malformed-envelope shapes via both `deserializeSyncEnvelope` (returns `undefined`) and `loadSyncEnvelope` (returns `'recovered'`, never `'loaded'`), and that load/acknowledge/attempt/mutate/cursor-update all reject with `identityMismatch` under a different identity (guest vs. user A, user A vs. user B, user vs. guest) as true no-ops. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps: test, lint, typecheck, web export) passes clean.
- **Notes:** From S035. Depends on T155.9 and T156.3. Keep runtime handles, promises, Sets, Maps, timers, and Supabase clients out of persisted state. Identity is enforced two ways: physical key scoping (existing `scoped-storage.ts` mechanism from T156.1/T156.2) plus a self-describing `identityNamespace` baked into the envelope that every mutator/loader independently rechecks — deliberate defense in depth, not redundancy. Deliberate deviation from the most literal reading of "per-domain cursors": rather than one shared structure holding all five domains' state plus a cursor map (which would violate the existing "do not combine these domains into a global store" rule), each domain gets its own `SyncEnvelope` instance later (T157.4–T157.8), so per-domain cursors fall out for free with no separate multi-domain cursor map. Wiring into the five real providers (`UserProfileProvider`, `CustomTrickProvider`, `PracticeHistoryProvider`, `GoalProvider`, `NoteProvider`) is explicitly deferred to T157.4+.

### [T157.2] Build a single-flight identity-aware sync coordinator

- **Area:** Synchronization orchestration
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Implement an explicit coordinator state machine that permits one sync per identity, models cancellation/retry, tags runs with identity generations, and continues safely after contained operation failures.
- **Exit criteria:** State-machine tests cover overlapping triggers, cancellation, identity change, network failure, retry, partial-domain failure, and stale completion; at most one run owns an identity at a time and no late result can mutate current state after logout or account switching. Confirmed: new `src/shared/sync/coordinator.ts` is a pure reducer/state-machine (no React, no network, no timers/`Date.now()` calls — every id/timestamp/generation is caller-supplied) with a two-variant `SyncCoordinatorState` union (`idle` | `running`) that structurally permits only one `ActiveSyncRun` at a time. `syncRequested` starts a run from idle or coalesces (first-trigger-wins) into `queuedRerunTrigger` while already running; `domainSettled` only mutates state when both `runId` and `identityGeneration` match the active run, discarding any stale/late result as a true no-op — the literal mechanism guaranteeing no late result can mutate state after logout or account switching; `identityChanged` cancels an in-flight run into a `cancelled` `lastRun` (dropping any queued rerun trigger) or, if idle, still resets `lastRun`/`pendingRerunTrigger` so a previous identity's sync status never leaks into the new one. `coordinator.test.ts` (13 cases) covers initial state, basic lifecycle, overlapping/coalesced triggers plus the resulting rerun, a stale-generation trigger no-op, cancellation mid-run with a dropped queued trigger, identity change while idle, a contained per-domain network failure that doesn't abort the run, retry-after-failure starting a fresh empty `domainResults` (not seeded from the prior failure), order-independent partial-domain failure, and three distinct stale-completion scenarios (after normal finish, after cancellation, and across a new run under a new generation). `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps: test, lint, typecheck, web export) passes clean.
- **Notes:** From S035. Depends on T157.1. Keep scheduling effects and clocks outside the reducer/state machine. Scope deliberately excludes (deferred to later tickets, called out in `.claude/plans/T157.2.md`): the React driver hook (`useSyncCoordinator`-style wrapper, T157.9), real Supabase/network transport calls (T157.3), and trigger-wiring/backoff/debounce timers (T157.9).

### [T157.3] Implement paginated pulls and idempotent ordered pushes

- **Area:** Synchronization transport
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add repository-agnostic transport orchestration for stable ordered pagination, mutation-ID-based idempotent retries, optimistic revision checks, tombstones, and application of server-returned revisions/timestamps.
- **Exit criteria:** Transport tests pull more than 1,000 ordered fixtures without gaps/duplicates, retry a successful write after a simulated lost acknowledgement without duplication, reject stale revisions into a typed conflict, apply authoritative server metadata, and continue unrelated operations after one conflict. Confirmed: new `src/shared/sync/transport.ts` is pure, storage-free, Supabase-free orchestration (no `Date.now()`, no network calls — everything injected), defining its own abstract `SyncTransportRepository<Row>` contract (`pull`/`push`, shaped after T155.9's `CloudPage`/`CloudReadResult`/`CloudPageRequest`/`CloudRepositoryError` vocabulary, reusing those types directly) plus `pullAllPages` (loops `pull()` accumulating rows until `nextCursor === null`, returns partial rows and the failing cursor on a mid-sequence failure, throws past a `maxPages` safety cap) and `pushOutboxOperations` (sequential FIFO loop over `OutboxOperation[]` calling `push()` with a caller-supplied `getExpectedRevision`, never short-circuiting on `'conflict'` or `'failed'`). `transport.test.ts` adds an in-memory `createFakeSyncTransportRepository` (operationId ledger for idempotent retries, revision-guard-then-tombstone `push()`, offset-cursor `pull()` reusing `encodeCloudCursor`/`decodeCloudCursor`) and 12 cases covering all five exit criteria: 1,200 ordered fixtures pulled with a non-dividing page size (zero duplicates, correct `(createdAt desc, id asc)` order), empty-repository and exact-page-boundary pulls, a mid-sequence pull failure returning partial rows plus the failing cursor, a non-terminating cursor throwing past `maxPages`, a retried `upsert` and a retried `delete` sharing an `operationId` both idempotently no-op past the ledger, a stale-revision `upsert` rejected into a typed `SyncConflict` leaving the stored row untouched, a `null`-revision create succeeding regardless of unrelated rows' revisions, server-clock-authoritative `revision`/`updatedAt` overriding client-guessed `payload` fields, and three-operation batches proving one `'conflict'` and, separately, one `'failed'` network error each leave the other two operations' outcomes correctly `'succeeded'` and correctly associated with their originating `operationId`/`recordId`. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps: test, lint, typecheck, web export) passes clean.
- **Notes:** From S035. Depends on T155.9, T157.1, and T157.2. Use injected clocks/network repositories for deterministic tests. Deliberate scope boundary (see `.claude/plans/T157.3.md`, deleted on completion): none of the five merged T155.9 concrete repositories (`*-cloud-repository.ts`) currently support revision-guarded conditional updates or an `operation_id`-keyed idempotency mechanism, so this task defines and tests its own abstract `SyncTransportRepository` contract against a fake repository rather than wrapping the real Supabase repos. Wiring a real adapter that satisfies this contract — including closing the `practice-history-cloud-repository.ts` bare-`.insert()` retry gap flagged in the plan — is deferred to T157.4+, one domain at a time.

### [T157.8] Synchronize notes with optimistic revisions and tombstones

- **Area:** Note synchronization
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Integrate notes, optional completed-Sesh references, edits, deletion tombstones, and stale-revision detection with the scoped outbox and coordinator while preserving trick/session snapshots.
- **Exit criteria:** Integration tests create/edit/delete notes offline, retain optional practice-session associations, retry idempotently, defeat stale-device resurrection, rehydrate on a second browser, and continue unrelated note/goal operations when one note conflicts. Confirmed: this is the mechanical rollout following T157.7's (goals) pattern directly -- unlike goals, local note editing (`noteUpdated`/`updateNote`/`validateNoteEdit`) and the `api.notes` UPDATE grant on `body` already existed, so there was no local-editing capability or migration to add. Notes share goals' exact tombstone trigger (`private.enforce_goal_note_mutation_invariants`), so the same `23514`-vs-`PGRST116` disambiguation logic from `classifyGoalUpdateFailure` was replicated (not shared/reused) as a new `classifyNoteUpdateFailure` in a new, table-specific `note-sync-transport.ts`. Added `isNoteCollection` (`note-persistence.ts`); `diffNotesForOutboxOperations` and `mapNoteRowsToDomainCollection` (`note-cloud-mapping.ts`, mirroring the goals pull-compose/diff pattern exactly, including preserving `practiceSessionId` untouched through the diff payload); `note-sync-envelope.ts` (a straight structural copy of `goal-sync-envelope.ts`); `note-sync-transport.ts` (the adapter, `createNoteSyncTransportAdapter`, proven live against a real local Supabase stack by `supabase/tests/data-api/t157_8_note_sync_transport.test.mjs`, 8 cases mirroring T157.7's seven plus a note-specific eighth: a note carrying a valid `practice_session_id` referencing a real seeded `practice_sessions` row round-trips that FK-backed association through push and pull unchanged); and `use-note-domain-sync.ts` (the domain-local driver hook, mirroring `use-goal-domain-sync.ts` with the same tombstone-filtering pull-compose step), proven with a fake-transport RTL suite (`use-note-domain-sync.test.tsx`, 10 cases covering offline create, reconnect, second-browser rehydrate (plus tombstone-filtering rehydrate), a stale-revision conflict on one note not blocking an unrelated note, rapid-edit coalescing, guest no-op, an offline body edit surviving reconnect, the tombstone-conflict case parking just one note's operation while the domain-level outcome still reports `'succeeded'`, and an explicit cross-domain-independence test mounting both `useNoteDomainSync` and `useGoalDomainSync` with independent fake transports that proves a notes conflict never touches the goals domain's own outcome or pending outbox state -- passed without any implementation change, as expected, since each domain already owns fully independent state). Wired additively into `NoteProvider` behind a new `useNoteSync()` context; `NoteProvider` deliberately does not need `useUserProfileActions()` (no notes-specific side effect analogous to goals' auto-selecting a trick). Confirmed by reading `sesh.tsx`/`multi-trick-sesh.tsx` directly that both read live `notes` only for a display-only "From your notes" panel, never for completion/achievement bookkeeping (unlike goals) -- so `note-provider.sync-sesh-isolation.test.tsx` deliberately used the lighter, single-assertion T157.4/T157.5-style isolation test rather than goals' heavier two-assertion version. Re-ran the full jest suite (not just notes' own files) to confirm `NoteProvider`'s now-real transport construction doesn't newly fail any test that co-mounts it without a sync mock (e.g. `custom-trick-provider.sync-sesh-isolation.test.tsx`, which already mounts a real, unmocked `NoteProvider`) -- none did; all 124 suites / 1504 tests pass. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps), `yarn test:db` (5/5 steps, including a fresh `db reset` and a clean re-run of the full Data API suite), and `yarn test:data-api` (140/140 tests, including all 8 new T157.8 cases) all pass clean.
- **Notes:** From S035 domain rollout 5. Depends on T157.7 and the note portion of T155.13.

### [T157.7] Synchronize goals with optimistic revisions and tombstones

- **Area:** Goal synchronization
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Integrate goal creation, editing, deletion, tombstones, and stale-revision detection with the scoped outbox and coordinator without coupling GoalProvider to Supabase.
- **Exit criteria:** Integration tests exercise count/time goals offline through reconnect, lost acknowledgements, edits, deletion, stale-device resurrection attempts, and second-browser hydration; conflicts pause only the affected goal and accepted results update local revision metadata. Confirmed: local goal editing did not exist yet before this task (no `goalEdited` action, no `editGoal`, and `api.goals`' UPDATE grant covered only `deleted_at`) -- built it first (`validateGoalTargetEdit`/`applyGoalTargetEdit` in `goal.ts`, `editGoal` in `GoalProvider`, and a new migration extending the UPDATE grant to `deleted_at, target_count, target_ms`, leaving `trick_id`/`trick_name`/`kind` UPDATE-ungranted forever since a goal's trick and kind are fixed at creation) before any sync plumbing depended on it. The other genuinely new mechanic versus every prior domain in this chain: `api.goals` has no DELETE grant, so a local delete pushes as a revision-guarded `UPDATE ... SET deleted_at`, and the mutation trigger unconditionally rejects any further UPDATE against an already-tombstoned row with a `23514` that's otherwise ambiguous with a genuine CHECK-constraint violation -- resolved via message-string matching (`'cannot modify a tombstoned'`) confined to a new `classifyGoalUpdateFailure` helper inside `goal-sync-transport.ts` (not the trigger itself, which stays untouched and still passes its own already-shipped T155.4/T155.6 pgTAP/Data-API suites), so a stale edit against a tombstoned goal surfaces as `'conflict'` (a stale-device resurrection attempt) while a stale delete-retry surfaces as idempotent `'succeeded'`. Added `isGoalCollection` (`goal-persistence.ts`); `diffGoalsForOutboxOperations` and `mapGoalRowsToDomainCollection` (`goal-cloud-mapping.ts`, the latter filtering pull-adopted collections to active-only while still recording revisions for tombstoned rows too, since a stale in-flight local edit against a filtered-out id still needs a correct `expectedRevision`); `goal-sync-envelope.ts` (a straight structural copy of `custom-trick-sync-envelope.ts`); `goal-sync-transport.ts` (the adapter, `createGoalSyncTransportAdapter`, proven live against a real local Supabase stack by `supabase/tests/data-api/t157_7_goal_sync_transport.test.mjs`, 7 cases: idempotent create retry, idempotent delete retry, deleting a never-created goal, a stale-revision edit conflict, a delete against an already-tombstoned goal succeeding idempotently, an edit against an already-tombstoned goal returning conflict (the adapter-level proof of the same "even a revision-matching mutation is rejected" case `t155_4_goals_and_notes.test.sql` already proves at the raw SQL layer), and a pull smoke test confirming tombstoned rows are not filtered); and `use-goal-domain-sync.ts` (the domain-local driver hook, mirroring `use-custom-trick-domain-sync.ts` with the added tombstone-filtering pull-compose step), proven with a fake-transport RTL suite (`use-goal-domain-sync.test.tsx`, 9 cases covering offline create, reconnect, second-browser rehydrate (plus a dedicated tombstone-filtering rehydrate case), a stale-revision conflict on one goal not blocking an unrelated goal, rapid-edit coalescing, guest no-op, an offline edit surviving reconnect, and the tombstone-conflict case parking just one goal's operation while the domain-level outcome still reports `'succeeded'`). Wired additively into `GoalProvider` behind a new `useGoalSync()` context. Because `Sesh` (unlike profile/custom-tricks) reads live `goals` state directly for both its session-start baseline and its completion-time achievement calculation, `goal-provider.sync-sesh-isolation.test.tsx` extends the established "sync doesn't change Sesh's rendered output" precedent with a second assertion: after completing a session, the goal-achievement celebration reflects the pre-sync goal snapshot the session started with, not a differently-targeted goal a mid-session pull-adopt could otherwise have introduced (proven via a fake transport seeded with the same goal id at a very different target, kept from ever reaching the primary reducer by the driver's own outbox-non-empty pull-adopt gate). Re-ran the full jest suite (not just goals' own files) to confirm `GoalProvider`'s now-real transport construction doesn't newly fail any test that co-mounts it without a sync mock -- none did. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps), `yarn test:db`, and `yarn test:data-api` all pass clean, including a full clean run of every pre-existing Data API test, proving the new migration didn't regress T155.6's goals grant tests.
- **Notes:** From S035 domain rollout 4. Depends on T157.6 and the goal portion of T155.13. Deliberately did not add a trick-goals-screen edit UI affordance -- `GoalProvider`'s new `editGoal` action is enough to prove the sync pipeline; a UI is separate, later product work.

### [T157.6] Synchronize immutable practice history

- **Area:** Practice-history synchronization
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Integrate completed-Sesh insertion and paginated pulls with the synchronization layer, retaining provider/reducer deduplication, immutable snapshots, client-fact timestamps, and Stats derivation.
- **Exit criteria:** Integration tests record sessions offline, reload and retry after a lost acknowledgement, synchronize more than 1,000 records, deduplicate by stable record identity, reject attempted remote mutation, and derive identical Stats locally and after second-browser rehydration. Confirmed: this domain follows T157.5's proven pattern (parallel `SyncEnvelope<PracticeHistorySyncSnapshot>` instance, pure diff function, real-adapter transport, domain-local driver hook, additive provider wiring), simplified because `api.practice_sessions` has no revision column and no UPDATE/DELETE grant at all -- no revision cache, no `expectedRevision` branching, no `'conflict'` outcome ever produced, no delete diff branch, and pull-adopt merges unconditionally by id (never gated on `outboxIsEmpty`) since immutable content-addressed records can never conflict with a pending local write. First fixed a load-bearing blocker: the T155.3 migration's `practice_sessions_duration_matches_timing` CHECK constraint enforced exact equality, which would reject any push of a genuinely-valid paused session (`durationMs < completedAt - startedAt`, per `sesh.ts`'s pause/resume support) -- loosened in place to `<=` in the existing migration file (sanctioned by `BACKEND_ROADMAP.md`'s no-cloud-users-yet policy), with the corresponding pgTAP case in `t155_3_custom_tricks_and_practice_sessions.test.sql` updated (a too-small-duration case flipped to too-large, plus a new `lives_ok` paused-session case, `plan(34)` to `plan(35)`) -- confirmed via `db reset && test db --local`. Added exported `isPracticeHistory` (`practice-history-persistence.ts`, with direct unit tests); a new pure `diffPracticeHistoryForOutboxOperations` (`practice-history-cloud-mapping.ts`, ascending-id-ordered upserts only, no content-diff for immutable records); `practice-history-sync-envelope.ts` (snapshot type, codec, load/save, reducer, mirroring `custom-trick-sync-envelope.ts` minus the revision map); `practice-history-sync-transport.ts` (a `SyncTransportRepository<PracticeSessionRow>` adapter with defensive payload re-validation, insert-then-23505-as-success idempotency with no update fallback since none exists, and a defensive `'delete'` rejection since this domain never emits one, proven live against a real local Supabase stack by `supabase/tests/data-api/t157_6_practice_history_sync_transport.test.mjs`, 6 cases: idempotent upsert retry, defensive delete rejection, a sync-path-specific proof that a raw update/delete against a synced row still hits `42501` through the transport's own authenticated client, a paused-session push succeeding (the concrete proof the constraint fix works), a pull smoke test, and `pullAllPages` returning exactly 1,200 fixture rows with no drops/duplicates past PostgREST's 1,000-row cap); and `use-practice-history-domain-sync.ts` (the domain-local driver hook, dispatching the primary reducer's existing `historyRestored` action on every sync run's unconditional union-merge), proven with a fake-transport RTL suite (`use-practice-history-domain-sync.test.tsx`, 6 cases covering offline recording durability across remount, a lost-ack retry absorbed via pull with no duplicate and no infinite pending state, dedup-by-id with `aggregatePracticeStats` counting a session exactly once, second-browser rehydrate matching a seeded fixture exactly, Stats-derivation parity across three computations (direct-from-fixture, recorded-locally, and rehydrated-via-sync), and guest identity never syncing). Wired additively into `PracticeHistoryProvider` behind a new `usePracticeHistorySync()` context; `practice-history-provider.sync-sesh-isolation.test.tsx` mounts the real `Sesh` component alongside the real provider (fake transport), drives an active/countdown Sesh, runs a full sync cycle whose fake-seeded server state diverges meaningfully from local practice history, and asserts Sesh's rendered output is byte-identical before and after. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps), `yarn test:db` (5/5 steps, including the corrected pgTAP suite and full Data API suite), and `yarn test:data-api` (125/125, including the new suite) all pass clean.
- **Notes:** From S035 domain rollout 3. Depends on T157.5 and T155.12. Synchronization must never alter an in-progress Sesh reducer.

### [T157.5] Synchronize custom tricks and sequences

- **Area:** Custom-trick synchronization
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Integrate custom tricks with the scoped outbox/coordinator while preserving stable IDs, stored step-name snapshots, deletion tombstones, and the no-nested-sequences rule offline and across browsers.
- **Exit criteria:** Integration tests create, edit, and delete standalone tricks and sequences offline; retry/reconnect without duplicates; rehydrate on a second browser; preserve historical step names after source rename/deletion; and surface same-record stale revisions without blocking unrelated tricks. Confirmed: this domain follows T157.4's proven pattern (parallel `SyncEnvelope<CustomTrickSyncSnapshot>` instance, pure diff function, real-adapter transport, domain-local driver hook, additive provider wiring), adapted for `api.custom_tricks` being a single hard-delete table with no scalar/child split. Added exported `isCustomTrickCollection` (`custom-trick-persistence.ts`); a new pure `diffCustomTricksForOutboxOperations` (`custom-trick-cloud-mapping.ts`, ascending-id-ordered upserts/deletes); `custom-trick-sync-envelope.ts` (snapshot type, codec, load/save, reducer, mirroring `profile-sync-envelope.ts`); `custom-trick-sync-transport.ts` (a `SyncTransportRepository<CustomTrickRow>` adapter with revision-guarded, idempotent insert/update/delete against `(user_id, id)`, proven live against a real local Supabase stack by `supabase/tests/data-api/t157_5_custom_trick_sync_transport.test.mjs`, 7 cases: idempotent standalone/sequence upsert retries, stale-revision conflict leaving the row untouched, idempotent delete retry, no-op delete of a never-created row, a nested-sequence upsert rejected as a parked `'failed'` validation outcome via the existing `23514`→`mapPostgrestError` classification with no new push-ordering logic needed, and a pull smoke test); and `use-custom-trick-domain-sync.ts` (the domain-local driver hook, dispatching the primary reducer's existing `customTricksRestored` action on a safe pull-adopt), proven with a fake-transport RTL suite (`use-custom-trick-domain-sync.test.tsx`, 8 cases including offline create/edit/delete durability across remount, reconnect without duplicate rows, second-browser rehydrate matching a seeded fixture exactly, a dedicated proof that a pull-adopt preserves a sequence step's stored name snapshot even when it diverges from a simulated renamed/absent source trick, a stale-revision conflict on one trick not blocking an unrelated trick while staying pending, rapid same-trick edits coalescing to one pushed operation, and guest identity never syncing). Wired additively into `CustomTrickProvider` behind a new `useCustomTrickSync()` context; `custom-trick-provider.sync-sesh-isolation.test.tsx` mounts the real `Sesh` component with a custom-sequence `PracticeTrick` alongside the real provider (fake transport), drives an active/countdown Sesh, runs a full sync cycle whose fake-seeded server state diverges meaningfully from local custom-trick state, and asserts Sesh's rendered output is byte-identical before and after. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps) and `yarn test:data-api` (119/119, including the new suite) both pass clean.
- **Notes:** From S035 domain rollout 2. Depends on T157.4 and T155.11.

### [T157.4] Synchronize profile and trick state first

- **Area:** Profile synchronization
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Integrate the profile/trick repositories, runtime mappings, scoped persistence envelope, and coordinator with `UserProfileProvider`, preserving current reducer authority, hydration behavior, and local offline writes.
- **Exit criteria:** Integration tests create and edit all profile/trick-state variants offline, reload with pending work, reconnect and synchronize, rehydrate the result in a second browser identity fixture, resolve accepted server revisions, and prove no sync effect changes active Sesh state. Confirmed: the primary `userProfileReducer`/`useIdentityScopedDomain` call in `UserProfileProvider` is untouched (reducer authority, hydration behavior, and local offline writes all preserved); a second, parallel `SyncEnvelope<ProfileSyncSnapshot>` instance (new `profile-sync-envelope.ts`, reusing `useIdentityScopedDomain`) tracks a durable FIFO outbox and per-record revision cache, fed by a new pure `diffProfileForOutboxOperations` (`profile-cloud-mapping.ts`) turning profile/trick-state changes into outbox operations, coalesced via a new shared `replacePendingOperationForRecord` (`sync-envelope.ts`) to at most one pending op per record. A new `profile-sync-transport.ts` adapter wraps the real T155.9 Supabase client with revision-guarded conditional updates (`.eq('revision', expected)`, `PGRST116` treated as conflict) and reuses the existing repository's insert-then-update-on-23505 idempotent pattern; proven against a real local Supabase stack by `supabase/tests/data-api/t157_4_profile_sync_transport.test.mjs` (6 cases: idempotent scalar/trick-state upsert retries, stale-revision conflict leaving the row untouched, idempotent delete retry, no-op delete of a never-created row, pull smoke). A new profile-domain-local driver hook (`use-profile-domain-sync.ts`, not the shared cross-domain coordinator, which is T157.9's job) runs pull-then-push cycles, dispatches the primary reducer's own `profileRestored` action on a safe pull-adopt (empty outbox) for the second-browser-rehydrate path, and parks `'conflict'`/`'failed'` push outcomes unresolved per T160.2's no-auto-resolve rule without blocking unrelated records; proven with a fake-transport RTL suite (`use-profile-domain-sync.test.tsx`, 6 cases: offline create surviving reload then flushing on reconnect, second-browser rehydrate matching a seeded fixture exactly, a conflict on one trick id not blocking an unrelated trick id while staying pending, rapid same-trick toggling coalescing to one pushed operation, guest identity never enqueueing/never syncing). Wired additively into `UserProfileProvider` behind a new `useUserProfileSync()` context (exposed for a future manual-retry affordance, no UI yet); `user-profile-provider.sync-sesh-isolation.test.tsx` mounts the real `Sesh` component alongside the real provider (fake transport), drives an active/countdown Sesh, runs a full sync cycle including a pull-driven `profileRestored` dispatch, and asserts Sesh's rendered output is byte-identical before and after. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps) and `yarn test:data-api` (112/112, including the new suite) both pass clean.
- **Notes:** From S035 domain rollout 1. Depends on T155.10 and T157.3. Deliberately does not dispatch into the shared `syncCoordinatorReducer` or add debounced login/foreground/reconnect triggers — both are T157.9's job once every domain is wired; this task's `runSync` return value is already `DomainSyncRecord`-shaped so T157.9 can plug it in with no reshaping. No conflict-resolution UI or automatic last-write-wins — that's T160.1/T160.2.

### [T155] Establish the secure cloud data foundation

- **Area:** Supabase database
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Represent all persisted Daily Dama domains in a constrained, typed Postgres/Data API boundary before any provider begins synchronization.
- **Notes:** Umbrella from S034. Complete T155.1–T155.13. No production migrations or live user data exist yet. All thirteen children (T155.1–T155.13) are done.

### [T155.13] Map goal and note rows at the runtime boundary

- **Area:** Goals / notes cloud mapping
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Implement pure mappings and runtime validation for count/time goals and notes, including tombstones, revisions, optional practice-session association, and authoritative name snapshots.
- **Exit criteria:** Unit tests round-trip all goal/note variants and tombstones, reject ambiguous variants, stale or malformed revisions, invalid session associations, and foreign-user rows, and prove neither domain state nor mutation payload preparation is mutated. Confirmed: new `src/features/goals/persistence/goal-cloud-mapping.ts` and `src/features/notes/persistence/note-cloud-mapping.ts` mirror T155.10/T155.11/T155.12's discriminated-Result pattern. Both introduce a tombstone-aware `GoalCloudEntry`/`NoteCloudEntry` (`'active' | 'deleted'`) that reuses the full `Goal`/`Note` snapshot for both statuses, so a tombstoned row round-trips to a first-class valid representation rather than absent/invalid. Row->domain mappers reject ambiguous variants mirroring the DB's `goals_count_shape`/`goals_time_shape` CHECK constraints (`'ambiguousVariant'`), malformed/stale-shaped revisions (`'invalidRevision'`), invalid `practice_session_id` shape (`'invalidSessionAssociation'`, non-blank-string-only, no stronger contract than the DB's own CHECK), and foreign-user rows first. New `prepareGoalDeletionPatch`/`prepareNoteDeletionPatch`/`prepareNoteEditPatch` mutation-payload-preparation functions reject preparing a mutation against an already-tombstoned known entry (`'alreadyDeleted'`, the "stale mutation" case mirroring the DB trigger's unconditional rejection) or a malformed revision, take `deletedAtMs` as an explicit parameter rather than calling `Date.now()` internally, and never mutate their inputs. `goal-cloud-mapping.type-check.ts`/`note-cloud-mapping.type-check.ts` (tsc-only, not jest-collected) prove `invalid` branches can't access their success payload without narrowing, `'active'` entries reject `.meta.deletedAt` access, and only server-owned columns are optional on the insert types. `goal-cloud-mapping.test.ts`/`note-cloud-mapping.test.ts` (71 cases total) cover round-trips for both goal kinds and both note practice-session-association states, tombstone round-trips, ambiguous-variant/out-of-range/malformed-revision/malformed-deleted_at/foreign-user/invalid-session-association/updated_at-before-created_at rejection, batch duplicate-id detection and order preservation with mixed active+tombstoned entries, insert-input field mapping and server-owned-column omission, mutation-prep success/rejection paths, and no-input-mutation proofs for frozen domain values, rows, and cloud entries. Two small additive exports (`isNormalizedText`/`isFiniteNonNegative`/`isPositiveInteger` in `goal-persistence.ts`, `isNormalizedText`/`isFiniteNonNegative` in `note-persistence.ts`) were made first so the new mapping files could reuse them. Full verification: `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps: test, lint, typecheck, web export) passes clean.
- **Notes:** From S034. Depends on T155.9. Provider integration belongs to T157.

### [T155.12] Map immutable practice-session rows at the runtime boundary

- **Area:** Practice-history cloud mapping
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Implement pure mappings and runtime validation for immutable completed-Sesh records, preserving historical trick snapshots, client-fact start/end timing, active duration, origin, and deterministic pagination identity.
- **Exit criteria:** Unit tests round-trip timed/open-ended/assessment records, reject malformed timing and duplicate/foreign-user rows, preserve historical names, and confirm server receipt timestamps never replace client domain timestamps. Confirmed: new `src/features/stats/persistence/practice-history-cloud-mapping.ts` mirrors T155.10/T155.11's pattern. `mapPracticeSessionRowToDomainRecord` is a single-row, discriminated `invalid`/`valid` `Result` that treats every incoming row field as effectively `unknown`, validates `id`/`trick_id`/`trick_name` (non-empty, trimmed) and `started_at > 0`/`completed_at >= started_at` against the migration's exact CHECK constraints, but validates `duration_ms` against the permissive domain invariant (`durationMs <= completedAt - startedAt`, matching `practice-history-persistence.ts`'s local storage validator) rather than the migration's stricter `duration_ms = completed_at - started_at` equality — a documented finding, since `sesh.tsx`'s pause support can legitimately produce `durationMs < completedAt - startedAt`. Reused the newly-exported `isFiniteNonNegativeNumber` from `practice-history-persistence.ts` rather than redefining it. `mapPracticeSessionRowsToDomainHistory` composes the batch (no `absent` status; empty rows -> `{ sessions: [] }`), adding duplicate-id detection and preserving row input order verbatim (never re-sorts, never regenerates `id`) to keep pagination identity consistent with the repository's `(completed_at desc, id asc)` server ordering. `mapPracticeSessionRecordToInsertInput`/`mapPracticeHistoryToInsertInputs` are total, non-`Result` pure functions that omit `created_at` entirely (not even `?? null`) so the DB's `default now()` applies, which is the concrete mechanism proving server receipt timestamps can never overwrite client domain timestamps on the write path. `practice-history-cloud-mapping.type-check.ts` (tsc-only, not jest-collected) proves the `invalid` branches can't access `.session`/`.history` without narrowing, that `PracticeSessionRecord`/`PracticeSessionInsert` can't be satisfied by an empty object literal, and that only `created_at` is optional on the generated Insert type. `practice-history-cloud-mapping.test.ts` (30 cases) covers: round-trip for open-ended/timed/assessment/assessment+timed/paused sessions and a whole-history order-preserving round-trip; malformed-row rejection (`started_at` 0/negative, `completed_at < started_at`, `duration_ms` exceeding the permissive bound plus a positive-control paused case within it, non-integer/negative `lace_count`, `planned_duration_ms` 0/negative, unknown `origin`, blank/untrimmed `id`/`trick_id`/`trick_name`, foreign-user as the sole issue, and a multi-issue collected-not-fail-fast case); batch-level duplicate-id and foreign-user rejection; historical-name preservation (row `trick_name` flows through unresolved); server-receipt-timestamp independence (`created_at` has zero effect on the mapped record, and the insert-input mapper never emits a `created_at` key); and no-input-mutation proofs in both directions. Full verification: `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps: test, lint, typecheck, web export) passes clean.
- **Notes:** From S034. Depended on T155.9. Not wired into any provider, reducer, or sync logic -- mapping-layer only, matching T155.10/T155.11's deferral of wiring to T157.6. Flagged (in the mapping file's header comment) that the T155.3 migration's `practice_sessions_duration_matches_timing` CHECK uses exact equality, which is stricter than the domain's actual pause-tolerant invariant; T157.6 will need to know a genuinely-valid paused session's insert will currently fail that constraint. Fixing the migration itself is out of scope for this ticket.

### [T155.11] Map custom-trick and sequence rows at the runtime boundary

- **Area:** Custom tricks cloud mapping
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Implement pure mappings and runtime validation for standalone custom tricks and sequences while preserving stored step-name snapshots and rejecting nested sequences or source-name re-resolution.
- **Exit criteria:** Unit tests round-trip every valid custom-trick variant, reject malformed rows and nested sequences, retain stored step names after source trick rename/deletion fixtures, and prove mapping does not mutate domain or row inputs. Confirmed: new `src/features/custom-tricks/persistence/custom-trick-cloud-mapping.ts` mirrors T155.10's pattern. `mapCustomTrickRowToDomainTrick` is a single-row, discriminated `invalid`/`valid` `Result` that treats every incoming row field as effectively `unknown`, mirrors T155.3's exact CHECK constraints/trigger (id/name normalized-text, `kentei_rank` 1-12, standalone-vs-sequence `steps` shape, exact-key-set per step-kind reusing exported `isNormalizedText`/`isOptionalKenteiRank`/`isSequenceStep` from `custom-trick-persistence.ts`, and row-local self-reference rejection), and only ever reads `step.name` off the raw step object -- it never resolves a name via `sourceTrickId` lookup, which is what makes stored-name preservation true by construction. `mapCustomTrickRowsToDomainCollection` composes the batch (no `absent` status -- an empty row list is just a valid empty collection), adding duplicate-id detection and a separate cross-row `nestedSequence` check (built from raw `id -> kind` rather than mapped tricks, so it still runs against a malformed sibling). `mapCustomTrickToUpsertInput`/`mapCustomTrickCollectionToUpsertInputs` are total, non-`Result` pure functions that rebuild a fresh object literal (and fresh step objects) rather than spreading `trick`/`step`, structurally ruling out both accidental extra-property leakage and source-name re-resolution on the write path. `custom-trick-cloud-mapping.type-check.ts` (tsc-only, not jest-collected) proves the `invalid` branches can't access `.trick`/`.collection` without narrowing `status === 'valid'` first, and that `CustomTrick`/`SequenceStep` can't be satisfied by an empty object literal (`@ts-expect-error`). `custom-trick-cloud-mapping.test.ts` (35 cases) covers: round-trip for every variant (standalone with/without/boundary `kenteiRank`, sequences with only-`existingTrick`/only-`customText`/mixed steps, sequence `kenteiRank`, and a whole-collection order-preserving round-trip); malformed-row rejection (unknown `kind`, blank/untrimmed `name`, `kentei_rank` 0/13/1.5, standalone-with-steps, sequence-with-null-steps, sequence steps length < 2, missing/untrimmed step `name`, extra key on a `customText` step, missing/empty `sourceTrickId` on an `existingTrick` step, row-local self-reference distinct from the batch-level `nestedSequence` reason, foreign-user, and a multi-issue collected-not-fail-fast case); batch-level nested-sequence rejection plus a non-nested negative control; duplicate-id rejection; the two explicit stored-name-preservation fixtures (rename-after-snapshot, and deletion-of-the-referenced-row not triggering a spurious `nestedSequence`); and no-input-mutation proofs (deep-freeze + before/after `JSON.stringify`) in both directions for standalone tricks, sequence tricks, and row batches. Full verification: `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps: test, lint, typecheck, web export) passes clean.
- **Notes:** From S034. Depended on T155.9. The provisional local v1 schema may change in place before external users exist. Not wired into any provider, reducer, or sync logic -- mapping-layer only, matching T155.10's deferral of wiring.

### [T155.10] Map profile and trick-state rows at the runtime boundary

- **Area:** Profile cloud mapping
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Implement pure mappings and runtime response validation between `UserProfile` plus focused/manually-laced state and the `profiles`/`user_trick_states` rows, with explicit absence and structured validation errors.
- **Exit criteria:** Unit tests round-trip every valid profile variant, reject malformed/partial/foreign-user rows, preserve stable trick-state identity, and demonstrate no input object is mutated; TypeScript compilation prevents ambiguous empty-object fallbacks. Confirmed: new `src/features/profile/persistence/profile-cloud-mapping.ts` adds three row→domain functions (`mapProfileRowToDomainFields`, `mapTrickStateRowsToDomainFields`, `composeUserProfileFromCloudRows`) returning a discriminated `absent`/`invalid`/`valid` `Result` with field-path/reason `ProfileMappingIssue`s that treat every incoming row field as effectively `unknown` (runtime `typeof`/shape checks, not just the generated Row types) and mirror T155.2's exact CHECK constraints by reusing `isKenteiRank`/`isLastSesh`/`isStreakThresholdMs` (now exported from `user-profile-persistence.ts`, no behavior change) plus `isValidThemeHexColor`; and two total, non-`Result` domain→row functions (`mapUserProfileToProfileUpsertInput`, `mapUserProfileToTrickStateUpsertInputs`) since `UserProfile` is already trusted reducer output, each building a fresh object literal rather than spreading-then-mutating the input. `profile-cloud-mapping.type-check.ts` (tsc-only, not jest-collected) proves the `absent`/`invalid` branches can't access `.fields` without narrowing `status === 'valid'` first, and that neither `ProfileScalarFields` nor `UserProfile` can be satisfied by an empty object literal (`@ts-expect-error`). `profile-cloud-mapping.test.ts` (97 cases combined with the small `user-profile-persistence.test.ts` re-run) covers: round-trip for every profile variant (initial, minimal/fully onboarded, every optional field populated, both `lastSesh` plan kinds at their boundary values, `manuallyUnlockedTrickIds`/`selectedTrickIds` empty-vs-populated in both directions, standard- and custom-trick-id-shaped strings); malformed `kentei_rank`/onboarding-invariant/`last_sesh`/`streak_threshold_ms`/theme-color rows including a multi-issue collected-not-fail-fast case; the "trick-state rows without an owning profile row" partial case and the `is_selected`/`is_manually_unlocked`-both-false and empty-`trick_id` cases; foreign-user rejection for both the profile row and a mixed-ownership trick-state batch (with an explicit "does not fall through" assertion); duplicate-`trick_id` rejection; explicit absence from both `composeUserProfileFromCloudRows` and `mapProfileRowToDomainFields`; and no-mutation proofs (deep-freeze + before/after `JSON.stringify` snapshot) in both directions. Full verification: `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps: test, lint, typecheck, web export) passes clean.
- **Notes:** From S034. Depended on T155.9. Provider/reducer wiring and trick-state sync/diff logic (deciding which rows to delete when a trick is deselected) are explicitly out of scope here — deferred to T157.3 and future integration work.

### [T155.9] Generate database types and define cloud repository contracts

- **Area:** Frontend cloud data boundary
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add deterministic Supabase TypeScript type generation and define one cloud repository contract per existing persistent domain, keeping generated row types, network errors, and Supabase clients outside reducers and domain models.
- **Exit criteria:** A documented generation command produces a stable checked-in type file from a clean local reset; type tests or compilation prove each domain repository exposes typed paginated reads and mutation results without leaking Supabase query builders into providers or reducers. Confirmed: `supabase/scripts/generate-database-types.mjs` (`yarn db:generate-types`, `-- --check` for a byte-for-byte drift check) starts the stack, does a clean `db reset`, runs `supabase gen types typescript --local --schema api`, and writes the banner-commented, checked-in `src/shared/supabase/database-types.ts` (396 lines covering all five `api` tables), always finishing with a cleanup `db reset`; documented in a new `supabase/README.md` section and wired into `yarn test:db` right after `db reset`/before pgTAP. Moved and retyped the lazy Supabase client to `src/shared/supabase/supabase-client.ts` (`createClient<Database, 'api'>(...)`, `db: { schema: 'api' }`); all 4 importers and the relocated test updated, plus a new assertion on `options.db`. Added the shared `src/shared/supabase/cloud-repository.ts` vocabulary (`CloudPage`/`CloudReadResult`/`CloudMutationResult`/`CloudRepositoryError`, opaque offset cursor codec, `mapPostgrestError`/`mapNetworkError`) with a real jest unit suite (`cloud-repository.test.ts`, 15 cases: cursor round-trip/throw-on-malformed, every Postgrest code classification, unknown/no-error fallback, network-error wrapping). Added five concrete domain repositories, one per persistent domain (`profile-cloud-repository.ts` covering `profiles`+`user_trick_states`, `custom-trick-cloud-repository.ts`, `practice-history-cloud-repository.ts` insert-only, `goal-cloud-repository.ts`, `note-cloud-repository.ts`), each built only on generated Row/Insert/Update types, each mutation patch type narrowed to T155.5's actual column-level UPDATE grants (e.g. `Pick<GoalUpdate, 'deleted_at'>`), goals/notes' `list` deliberately not filtering tombstoned rows (documented, deferred to future sync). A real local-stack run (via a live smoke test, see below) proved native `.upsert()` fails 42501 against custom_tricks/profiles/user_trick_states' narrower column-level UPDATE grants (Postgrest's generated `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` sets the conflict-target/pinned columns too); fixed by an insert-first-then-update-on-conflict-only-of-grantable-columns pattern in those three repository methods, keeping the same public `upsert*` signatures. Added the compile-time-only `src/shared/supabase/cloud-repository.type-check.ts` (not jest-collected; `tsc --noEmit`-checked) asserting every read/mutation method's exact return type and that `@supabase/supabase-js`'s `PostgrestFilterBuilder` does not structurally match any of them — 0 lint warnings, clean `tsc --noEmit`. Added `supabase/tests/data-api/t155_9_cloud_repositories.test.mjs`, a live smoke test using `supabase-js` directly (via a small `node:module` resolve-hook loader, `lib/alias-loader.mjs`, that lets plain `node --test` import the real `@/`-aliased TypeScript repository files under Node's native type-stripping) against a confirmed signed-in user on the real local stack: proves `custom_tricks.list()` paginates a 5-row/page-size-2 fixture across exactly 3 pages with correct cursor/ordering wiring, `profiles.upsertProfile()` succeeds for a valid row, and a real out-of-range `kentei_rank` (999) check-constraint violation classifies as `status: 'failed'`/`error.kind: 'validation'` — all 3 passing standalone and as part of `yarn test:data-api`/`yarn test:db`. Full verification: `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (4/4 steps) and `yarn test:db` (5/5 steps including the new `db:generate-types --check` step) both pass clean from a fresh local stack.
- **Notes:** From S034 and roadmap Phase 2 frontend items 1 and 3. Depends on T155.8. Domain-model (UserProfile/CustomTrick/etc.) mapping to/from these generated row types is explicitly out of scope here — see T155.10–T155.13. AGENTS.md's "three persistent domains" text is stale (predates T155.4's goals/notes); this ticket's repositories cover the real five domains (profile+trick-states, custom-tricks, practice-history, goals, notes) per `.claude/plans/T155.9.md`'s scope decision.

### [T155.8] Build a repeatable database security and migration test harness

- **Area:** Backend testing
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Consolidate clean-reset, catalog, constraint, grant, RLS, pagination, and raw Data API negative tests into a dependency-free or repository-standard command suitable for local work and CI.
- **Exit criteria:** One documented command resets the local database and runs all migration/security tests, including anonymous access, cross-user access, protected columns, malformed JSON, practice-session immutability, and a paginated fixture exceeding 1,000 sessions; it exits non-zero when a representative policy is deliberately weakened. Confirmed: added `yarn test:db` (`supabase/tests/run-db-tests.mjs`), a dependency-free Node script modeled on `.claude/scripts/verify.mjs`'s PASS/FAIL-per-step style, that runs `supabase start` → `db reset` → pgTAP (`test db --local`, 150 assertions across the existing `t155_2`–`t155_7` files) → `yarn test:data-api` (the Data API HTTP suite, now including the new `t155_8_pagination.test.mjs`) → a final cleanup `db reset` that always runs even on an earlier failure, with the script's own exit code reflecting only the first four steps. Confirmed live end-to-end: `yarn test:db` exits 0 with all steps passing from a running local stack. Added `supabase/tests/data-api/t155_8_pagination.test.mjs`: seeds 1,200 `practice_sessions` rows (grouped in 3s sharing a `completed_at` value, ~400 distinct timestamps, to exercise the `(user_id, completed_at desc, id)` index's ascending `id` tiebreak) for one user via a single bulk POST, paginates with `limit=137`/`offset` (a page size that doesn't evenly divide the group size or total, so page boundaries fall mid-duplicate-group) past PostgREST's `max_rows = 1000` cap, and asserts exactly 1,200 rows collected with no duplicates/gaps and correct `completed_at desc, id asc` ordering including the tiebreak — confirmed passing standalone and as part of `yarn test:data-api`. Added `yarn test:db -- --mutation-check`, a second opt-in mode of the same script that resets to a clean baseline, weakens `practice_sessions_select_own` to `using (true)` via `supabase db query --local`, reruns `t155_7_cross_user_isolation.test.mjs` expecting it to fail, always restores the policy via another `db reset`, and exits 0 with a "mutation check passed" message if the suite caught the regression or 1 with a "mutation check FAILED" message if it didn't. Confirmed live: the weakened-policy run shows the expected `practice_sessions` cross-user isolation assertion failing (a leaked row in the body) while every other table's block in the same file still passes, the script itself exits 0 ("mutation check passed: harness detected the weakened policy"), a subsequent `supabase status` and plain `yarn test:db` both pass cleanly (proving the restore worked), and a one-time sanity check pointing the probe step at an unrelated test file (`t155_6_profiles.test.mjs` instead of the cross-user isolation suite) confirmed the checker's own failure path fires correctly (exit 1, "mutation check FAILED" message) before being reverted. Wired `"test:db": "node supabase/tests/run-db-tests.mjs"` into `package.json` next to `test:data-api`. Updated `supabase/README.md`: replaced both forward-references to "T155.8 will consolidate this" with a new "One repeatable command" section documenting `yarn test:db` and `yarn test:db -- --mutation-check`, and folded the new pagination fixture into the existing Data API suite description. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --skip-export` passed all three applicable steps (test/lint/typecheck; export skipped as this is Supabase-tooling-only with no application/web-bundle change).
- **Notes:** From S034 and roadmap Verification. Depends on T155.1–T155.7. Not wired into `.github/workflows/` — no existing workflow references Supabase yet, and the task's "suitable for... CI" language describes the command's non-interactive exit-code behavior, not a request to add a CI job. `yarn test:db` stays a separate, explicitly-invoked command from `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`, same as `yarn test:data-api` already was — it requires a running local Supabase/Docker stack that the standard no-Docker verification sequence does not assume.

### [T153.3] Configure Resend Auth email delivery and DNS authentication

- **Area:** Auth email delivery
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Configure Supabase custom SMTP through Resend using the dedicated `auth.dailydama.com` sending subdomain, mirror the repository-owned confirmation and recovery templates into the hosted project, and disable link tracking so single-use Auth URLs are not rewritten.
- **Exit criteria:** A dated, secret-free delivery record proves hosted confirmation and recovery messages arrive through Resend from the Auth subdomain with their callback URLs intact, Resend link tracking is disabled, SPF and DKIM pass, and the subdomain publishes a visible DMARC `p=none` policy; no SMTP credential appears in tracked files or client-exposed environment values.
- **Notes:** From S032 and roadmap Phase 1 backend item 4 plus the email/redirect security requirements. Depends on T153.1–T153.2 (both merged). T162 later repeats deliverability checks before external signup and may tighten the DMARC policy using observed delivery evidence. Added the repository-owned `supabase/templates/recovery.html` and wired it into `supabase/config.toml`/`supabase/README.md`. Confirmed Resend's own Supabase Integration wizard had already wired custom SMTP on the hosted `Daily Dama Dev` project (`smtp_host=smtp.resend.com`, `smtp_user=resend`, `smtp_admin_email=team@auth.dailydama.com`) and published live DNS for `auth.dailydama.com` (SPF via `send.auth.dailydama.com` CNAME, DKIM via `resend._domainkey.auth.dailydama.com` TXT); the hosted project's Auth templates were still Supabase's generic defaults, so this task uploaded the repository-owned confirmation/recovery templates via the Management API. Sent real confirmation and recovery emails through the hosted project to a real (non-Mailpit) disposable inbox: both arrived from `team@auth.dailydama.com` with Daily Dama branding, a `DKIM-Signature` matching the published `resend._domainkey.auth.dailydama.com` key, and an intact, unrewritten `redirect_to=https://dailydama.com/auth/callback` callback (direct evidence link tracking is off — no Resend link-rewrite domain appeared). DMARC `p=none` is published at the organizational domain `dailydama.com` and covers `auth.dailydama.com` by DMARC's own subdomain-inheritance rule (no dedicated `_dmarc.auth.dailydama.com` record exists yet; flagged for T162 if a dedicated record is later wanted). Full dated delivery record with exact live values lives in `supabase/RESEND_DNS_AUTH.md`. Could not obtain a receiving-MTA `Authentication-Results` header (the free disposable-inbox provider used doesn't attach one) or independent Resend-dashboard confirmation of the link-tracking toggle (no separate Resend API key was available); the DNS/DKIM-signature match and the unrewritten callback URL are offered as the available substitute evidence.

### [T153.8] Build signup, check-email, verification, and resend

- **Area:** Account creation UI
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add `/auth/sign-up` and `/auth/check-email` using the Auth provider and structured error boundary, with ten-character password validation, generic duplicate-account handling, verification guidance, and explicit resend, offline, throttled, expired, and verifier-missing behavior. Do not place the submitted email in route parameters or analytics.
- **Exit criteria:** Route tests and a local browser walkthrough prove a guest can submit signup, receives the same public result for a new or existing address, reaches check-email guidance, resends within the configured limits, opens the Mailpit confirmation in the same browser profile, completes T153.7's callback, and reaches authenticated `/app/home`; invalid, offline, throttled, expired, and missing-verifier cases remain actionable without exposing whether an account exists. Confirmed: added `signUp`/`resendSignupConfirmation` to `AuthProvider` (`src/features/auth/state/auth-provider.tsx`), following `requestPasswordReset`'s fixed-redirect pattern exactly, with full `auth-provider.test.tsx` coverage (argument/redirect forwarding, success, and failure passthrough for both). Added `validateSignUpFields` (`src/features/auth/model/signup-validation.ts`, delegating password/confirm rules to the existing `validateNewPassword`) and a new ephemeral, non-persisted `PendingVerificationEmailProvider` (`src/features/auth/state/pending-verification-email.tsx`, scoped via a new `src/app/auth/_layout.tsx`) that carries the submitted email between `/auth/sign-up` and `/auth/check-email` purely in memory — never as a route param, matching the task's constraint. `SignUpScreen` (`src/features/auth/components/sign-up-screen.tsx`) treats `error === null` and `categorizeAuthError(error) === 'accountAlreadyExists'` as the identical success path (same tracked `sign_up_success` event, same navigation to `/auth/check-email`), which is the enumeration-safety crux of the ticket; every other error category (`weakPassword`, `offline`, `rateLimited`, `unknown`) stays on the form with fixed, non-account-revealing copy. `CheckEmailScreen` (`src/features/auth/components/check-email-screen.tsx`) renders the same-browser guidance and a guarded resend action when a pending email exists, and a generic fallback linking back to `/auth/sign-up` (no resend control) on a direct/reload visit with no pending email. Removed the `T153.8`-pending `as Href` cast and comment on `PostSeshSignupInvite`'s sign-up link in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`, now that `/auth/sign-up` is a real typed route. New tests: `signup-validation.test.ts`, `pending-verification-email.test.tsx`, `sign-up-screen.test.tsx` (12 cases including the accountAlreadyExists-identical-to-success assertion and a raw-password-never-rendered check), `check-email-screen.test.tsx` (10 cases covering both the pending-email and no-pending-email branches). `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps. Live browser walkthrough performed with the local Supabase stack (`npx supabase@2.115.0 start`/`db reset`) and Mailpit, driven with a disposable Playwright/Chromium install (no such tooling exists in the repo; installed only into the session scratch directory, never added as a project dependency) rather than manual clicking, isolating scenarios with `db reset` between runs given the local `email_sent = 2`/hour cap: (1) fresh signup → check-email → Mailpit confirmation opened in the same browser context → `/auth/callback` → authenticated `/app/home`, full happy path confirmed; (2) two signups with the same still-unconfirmed address produced byte-identical check-email page text (enumeration-safe duplicate handling); (3) a rapid resend click showed the fixed "Too many attempts" copy (Supabase's `max_frequency` throttle), with a later resend from the same session succeeding cleanly; (4) submitting offline (`context.setOffline(true)`) showed the fixed offline copy; (5) reusing an already-consumed confirmation link showed `/auth/callback`'s existing "This link has expired or was already used" copy, confirming T153.7's generic handling extends unmodified to the signup case; malformed-email/short-password/mismatched-confirm validation blocked submission with actionable field errors in every run. One edge case was not cleanly isolated: opening a signup confirmation link in a second, unrelated browser context (to reproduce the specific `missingVerifier` copy) instead produced GoTrue's one-time-use consumption interacting with the earlier open in the first context, surfacing the callback's generic `unknown`-category fallback copy rather than the dedicated missing-verifier string — still actionable and non-leaking, and this is pre-existing, unchanged `/auth/callback` logic (T153.7, already merged and separately verified), not new code from this ticket.
- **Notes:** From S032. Depends on T153.1, T153.4–T153.5, and T153.7. Preserve Daily Dama's existing visual system without depending on profile-backed theme hydration or introducing a generic Auth dashboard shell. Sign-in/sign-up cross-navigation links deliberately not added here — that is T153.11's scope, per that ticket's own closing note.

### [T154.2] Add current-device logout and fresh guest return

- **Area:** Authentication session lifecycle
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add a Profile logout action using `signOut({ scope: 'local' })`, transition authoritative Auth state back to guest, and leave other device sessions untouched.
- **Exit criteria:** Provider and browser tests confirm logout invalidates the current browser session, returns to a usable guest workspace, does not request global sign-out, and handles offline or expired-session responses deterministically. Confirmed: the provider-layer `signOut` action (`{ scope: 'local' }`) and `SIGNED_OUT` → `guest` state derivation already existed and were already tested from earlier work (T153.4-era). Added a new self-contained `AccountSection` component (`src/features/auth/components/account-section.tsx`, mirroring `sign-in-screen.tsx`'s pattern) that renders `null` unless `authState.status === 'authenticated'`, shows the session email and a "Log out" button, and lets `AuthProvider`'s own `onAuthStateChange` listener drive the guest transition rather than forcing local state. Traced `@supabase/auth-js`'s `_signOut` source: an already-expired/invalid session (401/403/404) signs out successfully and silently to guest with no UI error; a genuine offline failure leaves the local session fully intact (no `SIGNED_OUT`) and is surfaced as a retryable `categorizeAuthError` → `'offline'` error via the same fixed-copy-map pattern as `sign-in-screen.tsx`, with the same-tick `isSubmittingRef` double-submit guard. Wired into `src/app/app/profile/index.tsx` as its own Account section (self-owns its Divider/label chrome so it collapses cleanly when not authenticated). New tests: `src/features/auth/components/__tests__/account-section.test.tsx` (initializing/guest/passwordRecovery render nothing; authenticated shows email + enabled button; success path tracks `log_out_success` and re-enables the button; offline path renders offline copy, calls `logAuthWarning`/`trackAuthEvent('log_out_error', 'offline')`, re-enables the button, and asserts the UI still reflects `authenticated` rather than faking guest; unknown-error path shows generic copy; no raw error message ever renders; same-tick double-press guard). ### [T153.12] Invite a guest after their first completed Sesh

- **Area:** Post-Sesh Auth invitation
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Show a dismissible, optional signup invitation on the completion UI for the first completed Sesh in the current guest practice-history namespace. Derive eligibility from authoritative practice history and keep dismissal local to that completion UI rather than adding a profile field, storage key, or persistence domain.
- **Exit criteria:** Component/provider tests prove the invitation appears for an unauthenticated guest's first successfully recorded Sesh, can be dismissed or can open signup without changing the completed record, does not appear on later completions or after reloading the persisted first record, never appears for an authenticated user, and does not alter completion deduplication or any existing guest flow. Confirmed: added `isFirstEverRegularSession(priorSessions, origin)` to `src/features/stats/model/practice-history.ts` (excludes assessment-origin history), computed inside `Sesh`'s existing completion effect from the pre-dispatch `sessions` snapshot, and threaded as a plain `showSignupInvite` prop into `CompletedSesh` and a new self-contained `PostSeshSignupInvite` sub-component (own local dismiss state, `Link` to `/auth/sign-up`, no context reads) in `src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`. `Sesh` takes a new optional `authStatus?: AuthStatus` prop, supplied by `src/app/app/sesh/index.tsx` from `useAuthState()`; omitting it (as every pre-existing test does) fails closed to "invite never shown." New tests in `practice-history.test.ts`, `sesh.test.tsx` (`CompletedSesh signup invite`, `Sesh signup invite integration`), and a new `auth-provider` mock in `layout.test.tsx` cover: invite appears for a guest's first regular completion; dismissing or opening signup leaves the completed record and history unchanged; absent on a second completion in the same session; absent for an authenticated user and when `authStatus` is omitted; and a fresh mount seeded with one persisted regular session (via the real `PracticeHistoryProvider`/AsyncStorage path) lands on the picker, never the invite, proving the "after reloading" criterion follows from `Sesh`'s in-memory-only reducer state. Full `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (test, lint, typecheck, web export) passes.
- **Notes:** From S032 and roadmap Phase 1 frontend item 6. Depends on T153.4 and T153.8. Guest-data import was not started here, per scope. Scoped to the single-trick `CompletedSesh` screen only; `MultiTrickSesh`'s separate wrap-up (assessment flow, multi-trick practice, stats sandbox) is out of scope for this iteration — same eligibility helper would extend there cheaply if wanted later. `/auth/sign-up` does not exist yet (T153.8 unmerged); the invite's `Link` forward-references the fixed path the same way other pre-T153.8 call sites already do, and `tsc --noEmit` passes because Expo Router's typed routes aren't checked against a committed route union in this repo (`.expo/` is gitignored).

### [T153.4] Add a static-render-safe web Supabase client and Auth state

- **Area:** Authentication state
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add `@supabase/supabase-js`, a browser-persistent client configured only from `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, and one authoritative Auth provider modeled as `initializing`, `guest`, or `authenticated`. Configure `flowType: 'pkce'`, leave URL detection disabled so T153.7 owns the single code exchange, and ensure client construction and storage access are safe during Expo Router's Node static-render pass.
- **Exit criteria:** Provider tests drive missing/malformed configuration, initial session resolution, sign-in, sign-out, token refresh, Auth events, and offline initialization; children and every existing `/app` guest route remain renderable while Auth is `initializing` or unavailable; server rendering touches no browser globals; and `yarn expo export --platform web --output-dir /tmp/daily-dama-web-export` succeeds with only publishable Supabase configuration reachable from application code. Confirmed: added `src/features/auth/model/auth-state.ts` (pure `deriveAuthState`), `src/features/auth/persistence/supabase-config.ts` (pure env validator), `src/features/auth/persistence/supabase-client.ts` (lazily memoized client, AsyncStorage adapter, `flowType: 'pkce'`, `detectSessionInUrl: false`), and `src/features/auth/state/auth-provider.tsx` (three-state `AuthProvider` driven exclusively by `onAuthStateChange`, no `getSession()` call, no hydration gate). Mounted `AuthProvider` around `<Slot />` in `src/app/_layout.tsx`. 23 new provider/unit tests cover missing/malformed config, initial-session resolution (offline `INITIAL_SESSION` with and without a cached session), sign-in, sign-out (scope `local`), `TOKEN_REFRESHED`, synchronous-subscribe-throw fallback, unmount unsubscribe, and synchronous first-render proof that children never wait on a hydration gate. Ran the full local suite (841 tests, 75 suites) plus `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (test, lint, typecheck, web export), and separately re-ran `yarn expo export --platform web --output-dir /tmp/daily-dama-web-export` with no `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*` vars set — all 21 static routes (including every existing `/app` guest route) exported successfully with non-empty server-rendered HTML, proving the missing-configuration path renders safely and touches no browser globals during the Node static-render pass. Confirmed only `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` are referenced anywhere in `src/`; no service-role or secret key appears in application code.
- **Notes:** From S032. Depends on T145 and T153.1. T145 first moves rehydration-dependent domain providers under `/app`; mount Auth above the root `<Slot>` without adding an Auth hydration gate. The Auth state is the application's session/UI authority, not a substitute for later database RLS authorization. Keep a platform seam for Phase 5, but implement and verify web only. T153.1 has not merged yet; per this ticket's own feasibility framing, verification here used mocked `@supabase/supabase-js` responses and an unset-env export rather than a live backend, so this did not block on it.

### [T140] Trigger the screenshot showcase's entrance animation on scroll into view

- **Area:** Landing page
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `ScreenshotItem`'s `FadeInUp` animation (added in T131) fires on initial page load, off-screen, so QA can't observe it without already knowing to scroll first. Trigger it instead when each item scrolls into the top two-thirds of the viewport.
- **Exit criteria:** Loading the landing page and scrolling down shows each `ScreenshotItem`'s entrance animation trigger only once it scrolls into the top two-thirds of the viewport (items already in that band on load animate in immediately); scrolling past and back doesn't need to replay it. Confirmed at both mobile and desktop widths via a browser check. Confirmed: added a `ScreenshotEntry` wrapper with a colocated `useHasEnteredViewport` hook (`landing-page.tsx`) that observes each item via `IntersectionObserver` with a `rootMargin` computed from `window.innerHeight` to represent the top two-thirds of the viewport, and swaps the inner `ScreenshotItem`'s `key` from `'pending'` to `'entered'` on first intersection to force the remount `entering` needs. Verified with a scripted Chrome DevTools Protocol browser check (no `preview_start`/GUI browser available in this session) at both 375×812 (mobile) and 1440×900 (desktop): each item's computed `opacity` and `entering`-driven animation only flip from `0`/not-set to `1` once its bounding rect crosses into the top two-thirds band while scrolling, stay at `1` afterward, and don't reset or replay after scrolling past all items and back to the top. No new console warnings beyond the existing benign ones. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export); `landing-page.test.tsx` passed unmodified, confirming the non-web/no-`IntersectionObserver` fallback (animate immediately, matching pre-T140 behavior) stayed intact.
- **Notes:** From story S010. Web-only concern (this project's active platform scope) — an `IntersectionObserver`-backed hook gating each item's `entering` prop is a reasonable approach, kept local to `landing-page.tsx`. Animation duration/easing (T139/S009) is unchanged — only the trigger condition changes. Raised from `Implementer` to `Required` during scoping review — `IntersectionObserver` isn't used anywhere else in this codebase, and it needs to interact correctly with Reanimated's `entering` prop (which normally only fires on mount), which is unfamiliar-API territory worth a planning pass first.
