### [T200] Add sign-up/sign-in entry points to the landing page

- **Area:** Landing / navigation
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** The sign-up screen is currently only reachable after completing onboarding (the Kentei rank picker), which makes it impossible for a guest with existing local data to create an account without first going through onboarding again, and blocks any true "brand-new guest" signup test. Add direct sign-up/sign-in entry points to the landing page (`landing-cta.tsx` or a sibling landing component) so a visitor can reach `/auth/sign-up` / `/auth/sign-in` without completing onboarding first.
- **Exit criteria:** From the landing page (as a not-yet-onboarded visitor), tapping a new sign-up/sign-in control navigates to `/auth/sign-up`/`/auth/sign-in` directly. A visitor who signs up this way with existing local data gets it imported per T198/T199; a visitor with no local data who signs up this way still sees onboarding normally afterward, unchanged from today. Confirmed in-browser for both cases.
- **Notes:** From S080. The user's own suggested direction. This adds a parallel entry point only — it does not remove or change the existing onboarding-gated in-app flow for guests who continue using the app without an account. Added `LandingAuthCta` (`src/features/landing/components/landing-auth-cta.tsx`), gated only on `useAuthState()` (`'guest'` renders, everything else renders nothing) exactly like `GuestAccountSection`'s pattern, deliberately with zero dependency on `useCachedOnboardingStatus`/`useRedirectOnboardedVisitorHome`/`LANDING_ARRIVAL_PARAM`, rendered in `landing-page.tsx` just below `LandingCta`. Confirmed the tab bar already hides Profile (the only prior entry point) until onboarding completes, and that a pre-onboarding visitor never gets redirected away from `/`, so this new control is unconditionally reachable pre-onboarding with no redirect race. Live-verified against the local Supabase stack via a scripted Playwright walkthrough (no interactive browser preview available in this run; port 8081, the stack's fixed `redirect_to` target, was occupied by a concurrent agent's dev server, so the app under test ran on port 8090 per T199's own precedent for this exact conflict): (1) a brand-new guest with no local data sees both controls at `/` and each navigates directly to `/auth/sign-up` / `/auth/sign-in` without visiting onboarding; (2) a returning onboarded guest still redirects straight to `/app/home` before the new component ever mounts, unchanged; (3) a guest with existing local data (custom trick-ID selections seeded directly into `guest:daily-dama:user-profile`, bypassing the picker so `hasCompletedOnboarding` stays `false`) reaches the new landing entry point and, on signing into a real pre-confirmed local Supabase account, has that data land in the cloud (`api.user_trick_states`), confirmed by direct DB query, with no visible guest-import panel; (4) a truly empty-data guest signing into a fresh account still sees `KenteiOnboarding` afterward, unchanged. The full `/auth/sign-up` real-signup-with-email-confirmation round trip specifically (as opposed to `/auth/sign-in` against a pre-provisioned account) was not independently re-executed given the port constraint, since `use-guest-import.ts`'s import trigger is verified to key off any transition to a signed-in `identity.kind === 'user'` regardless of whether it arrived via sign-up-confirmation or sign-in, and the `/auth/sign-up` route itself (reached directly from the new button, not through onboarding) was independently confirmed to render the real sign-up form rather than onboarding. One further finding: for a not-yet-onboarded guest with seeded data, `KenteiOnboarding` correctly continues to render after import (not `DailyHome`) — this is correct, not a regression, since the imported profile faithfully carries over `hasCompletedOnboarding: false` from the seed; a truly onboarded guest is unreachable from `/` at all per (2)'s redirect, so this is the only data shape actually reachable through this entry point.

### [T199] Stop showing onboarding again to a guest whose data just got imported

- **Area:** Guest conversion / onboarding
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** T186.1 was supposed to gate onboarding on existing local data so a guest with data lands on Home after signup, not the Kentei rank picker. The user reports being sent to onboarding anyway, immediately after signup, with several elements painting first. This needs runtime tracing (not a known fix) through `use-guest-import.ts`'s auto-confirm sequencing, the guest→user identity-generation switch, and `UserProfileProvider`'s hydration/write timing, to find why `hasCompletedOnboarding`/`kenteiRank` don't read as complete by the time the post-signup redirect/render evaluates them.
- **Exit criteria:** A real signup (through the actual auth flow, not a seeded fixture) for a guest identity with existing local practice/profile data lands on Home directly, with no onboarding screen shown at any point, verified in-browser end-to-end against a real account.
- **Notes:** From S079 (the onboarding-recurrence symptom). Same general bug class as T189 (a post-identity-change read racing or missing the just-written state) but on the opposite transition (guest→user after import, not logout). Confirmed root cause: `useSyncCoordinator`'s login pull fires once per identity generation and settles (against a genuinely empty new account) before `use-guest-import.ts`'s upload phase pushes the guest's profile to the cloud row, and nothing ever re-triggers a second pull afterward -- the local primary `UserProfile` reducer never adopts the just-imported `hasCompletedOnboarding`/`kenteiRank`. Fixed in `src/app/app/home.tsx`: an `importId`-guarded effect calls `useUserProfileSync().runSync('login')` once guest-import reaches `'completed'`, and a new `isResyncingAfterImport` flag is folded into the existing `importPending` render gate so onboarding can't paint during that resync. Covered by a new integration test (`home.guest-import-resync.test.tsx`) driving a real guest-import state machine + a real (mocked-transport) profile resync through `HomeScreen`, confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code. Live-verified against the local Supabase Auth stack via a real signup (real `/auth/v1/signup`, real Mailpit confirmation email, real PKCE code exchange) for a guest with seeded local profile+practice-history data: Home rendered `DailyHome` directly with `KenteiOnboarding` never painting, stable across a reload. Port 8081 (the local stack's fixed redirect_to target) was occupied by an unrelated long-running dev server outside this worktree, so the app under test ran on port 8090 and the sign-up/callback network calls were driven directly via a second `supabase-js` client instance injected into the same page (sharing the app's default session storage key) instead of clicking through the sign-up form/callback route -- every network call (signup, email delivery, verify, PKCE exchange) was real. The equivalent staleness gap likely affects the other 4 sync domains (customTricks, practiceHistory, goals, notes) too, but nothing currently gates rendering on them the way Home gates onboarding, so no user-visible flash was observed for those in this live check; flagged as a possible follow-up rather than folded into this fix, per the plan's explicit scope boundary.


### [T164] Make the onboarding theme step's background actually preview

- **Area:** Onboarding
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `src/app/app/home.tsx` renders `<Screen background={profile.hasCompletedOnboarding ? undefined : AppColors.yellow} ...>` — hardcoding the mustard-yellow background for the entire duration of onboarding regardless of the theme preset the user just previewed via `setThemeColors` (T146). Make the background reflect the live-previewed `themeBackgroundColor` instead of this fixed fallback.
- **Exit criteria:** `yarn test --runInBand` passes, including a test asserting that selecting a non-default preset during onboarding changes the rendered background color away from `AppColors.yellow`; confirmed in-browser via the Expo dev server that tapping a preset visibly changes the onboarding screen's background, not just its text/accent. Confirmed: `src/app/app/home.tsx` now reads `profile.themeBackgroundColor ?? AppColors.yellow` instead of the fixed fallback; a new `home.test.tsx` test drives the full onboarding flow through theme selection and asserts the rendered `Screen`'s background color changes from `AppColors.yellow` to the selected preset's background. `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn eslint` on the changed files all pass. Verified live in the Expo web dev server via a scripted Playwright walkthrough (no interactive browser preview available in this run): driving onboarding to the theme step showed the resolved background as `rgb(244, 201, 77)` (`AppColors.yellow`) before selecting a preset, and `rgb(216, 232, 247)` (the Blue preset's `#d8e8f7`) immediately after tapping it — a real, visible background change, not just text/accent.
- **Notes:** From S043 — a regression against T146's own exit criteria ("background/accent visibly change"), not new scope. T165 (S044) was not yet claimed/implemented in this repo at the time of this task; only `home.tsx`/`home.test.tsx` were touched here.

### [T162.3] Add dependency, secret, and built-bundle scanning to CI

- **Area:** CI security
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Extend `.github/workflows/ci.yml` with pinned, least-privilege dependency and secret scanning plus a built-web-bundle check for service-role keys, SMTP secrets, Auth sessions, and other forbidden server values.
- **Exit criteria:** CI passes on the clean repository, fails on representative committed-secret and generated-bundle fixtures in an isolated test, reports actionable locations without echoing secret values, and runs for pull requests to `development`/`main` without gaining write permissions. Confirmed on the real PR (#221) run, not just locally: after three iterations to fix real-CI-only false positives (see Notes), all five jobs (`verify`, `dependency-audit`, `secret-scan`, `bundle-secret-scan`, `secret-scan-self-test`) passed on the actual GitHub Actions run, and the `secret-scan` job's logged `GITHUB_TOKEN Permissions` block showed exactly `Contents: read`, `Metadata: read`, `PullRequests: read` — no write scope anywhere. Added four new `ci.yml` jobs, all SHA-pinned (`actions/checkout@11bd719...` v4.2.2, `actions/setup-node@39370e3...` v4.1.0, `gitleaks/gitleaks-action@e0c47f4...` v3.0.0) and running under a new workflow-level `permissions: contents: read` (with a job-level `pull-requests: read` addition on `secret-scan` only, see Notes); `gitleaks-action`'s PR-comment feature is disabled (`GITLEAKS_ENABLE_COMMENTS: "false"`) rather than granted `pull-requests: write`. `security/scripts/check-dependency-audit.mjs` hand-parses `yarn audit --json` NDJSON (Yarn Classic's `--level` flag does not gate its exit code, confirmed locally) against a small dated allowlist (`security/accepted-dependency-advisories.json`, the four current Metro/bundler-devDependency high/moderate advisories with no upstream patch); passed in CI. `security/scripts/check-bundle-secrets.mjs` builds a fresh export via the existing `buildExport()` and scans it with the pinned-by-digest `gitleaks` Docker image via the new `security/scripts/gitleaks-cli.mjs` helper; passed in CI. `.gitleaks.toml` extends gitleaks' default ruleset with a single `[allowlist]` table (regexes plus a small `commits` list, see Notes) for confirmed false positives; passed in CI (`secret-scan` job, full git-history scan). `security/scripts/verify-secret-detection.mjs` (`node:test`) is the isolated-test exit criterion: it builds a synthetic, clearly-fake Supabase-service-role-shaped JWT (its base64 segments derived from plain JSON at runtime, never written as a literal) into a throwaway scratch git repo and a throwaway copy of a real `dist/` export at test-run time (never committed to real history), asserts gitleaks flags both plus a clean control passes, and ran as its own `secret-scan-self-test` CI job on this PR — passed. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` also passes all four standard steps locally (test, lint, typecheck, web export).
- **Notes:** From S041 and roadmap client security. Select free tooling compatible with the existing GitHub workflow; do not add paid observability. Found while planning that a repo-local (not OS-tmpdir) scratch directory is required for the self-test's Docker-based checks to work under macOS Docker Desktop's default file-sharing scope; documented in `security/README.md` and `.gitignore` (`/.security-scratch/`). Two deviations from the plan, both discovered only from real CI runs (a purely local `gitleaks detect` run against the identical commit range did not reproduce either): (1) `gitleaks-action` needs `pull-requests: read` (not just `contents: read`) to list a PR's commits on `pull_request` events, failing with "Resource not accessible by integration" otherwise — added a job-level `permissions: pull-requests: read` override on `secret-scan` only, still read-only. (2) `gitleaks-action` pins its own bundled gitleaks binary (v8.24.3, confirmed from the job log) independent of the v8.30.1 image `security/scripts/gitleaks-cli.mjs` pins for the bundle-scan/self-test jobs; v8.24.3's TOML parser only recognizes the legacy singular `[allowlist]` table and silently ignored the originally-written `[[allowlists]]` array-of-tables entries (no parse error, just never applied) — `.gitleaks.toml` was rewritten to the singular form, confirmed against both versions locally (v8.30.1 via a fresh 817-commit clone, and v8.24.3 pulled directly and run against the exact PR commit range) before the real CI run went green. Also found and fixed a self-inflicted instance of the same class of bug: an early draft of `verify-secret-detection.mjs` embedded its fake-JWT fixture as a literal (then as literal string constants), which the real repo history scan correctly flagged on its own source file; fixed by deriving the fixture's base64 segments from plain JSON at runtime instead.

### [T153.10] Build the forgot-password request flow

- **Area:** Password recovery request UI
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add `/auth/forgot-password` with a generic recovery-request response, exact callback initiation, and explicit loading, validation, offline, throttled, and resend guidance. Complete recovery through the existing T153.7 callback and T153.6 reset route without putting an email or destination URL into route parameters.
- **Exit criteria:** Route tests prove known and unknown addresses receive the same public response and offline, invalid, throttled, and repeat submissions are deterministic; a local same-browser Mailpit walkthrough requests recovery, exchanges the PKCE code once through `/auth/callback`, reaches `/auth/reset-password`, changes the password, signs in with the new password, and leaves no email, code, password, or token in URLs, application logs, or Analytics. Confirmed live against the local Supabase/Mailpit stack (`npx supabase@2.115.0 start` / `db reset`, Colima/Docker): a pre-verified test account was created via the GoTrue admin API (no sign-up UI exists on this branch), and a headless Chromium walkthrough (Playwright, since no interactive browser preview was available in this run) drove the full path on `http://localhost:8081` — submitting `/auth/forgot-password` rendered the generic confirmation copy and fired exactly one `POST /auth/v1/recover`; the Mailpit email's link resolved through GoTrue's `/auth/v1/verify` to `http://localhost:8081/auth/callback`, which exchanged the code and `router.replace`d to `/auth/reset-password` with an empty query string and hash (no `code`/token ever persisted in the address bar); submitting a new password showed "Your password has been updated."; re-visiting the exact same recovery link afterward landed back on `/auth/callback` with `otp_expired`/"This link has expired or was already used", proving the code is single-use, not idempotently replayable; clearing the `sb-*` local-storage keys and signing in again at `/auth/sign-in` with the new password reached `/app/home`. Across the whole run, the only place the raw email appeared was the app's own required `POST /auth/v1/recover` body (expected, not a leak); Vercel Analytics debug-mode console output showed `auth_callback_success`/`auth_callback_error`/`forgot_password_requested` events carrying only name/category with the callback URL's `code` param rendered as `[redacted]`; no raw email, password, code, or token appeared in browser console output or the Expo/Metro terminal log. One planned check (reloading `/auth/reset-password` mid-flow) was dropped after confirming it does not apply here: `deriveAuthState`'s `INITIAL_SESSION` handling deliberately treats a reloaded, cached recovery session as plain `authenticated` rather than `passwordRecovery` (see `auth-state.test.ts`), so a reload correctly shows the same terminal "link no longer valid" copy rather than "smuggling" the code through a URL — this is existing, intentionally tested behavior, not a defect introduced or found here. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps after merging `origin/development` (T153.1's `supabase/` config, T154.2's sign-out, T156.x identity work) into this branch.
- **Notes:** From S032. Depends on T153.4–T153.7. Supabase's same-browser/device PKCE verifier limitation is explained in the recovery guidance and missing-verifier error. All code/UI implementation and Jest coverage were completed on this branch prior to this final pass; this pass closed the one remaining exit-criteria clause (the live Mailpit walkthrough) once Docker/Colima was available. Also fixed two unrelated pre-existing baseline typecheck breaks surfaced while merging `origin/development` in: `account-section.tsx` (T154.2) had a non-exhaustive `Record<AuthErrorCategory, string>` missing the `missingVerifier` category this branch introduces, and `sesh.tsx` (T153.12) already referenced a not-yet-built `/auth/sign-up` typed route (confirmed present on `origin/development` itself, independent of this branch, via a standalone detached-HEAD `tsc` run) — cast with `as Href` and commented pending T153.8.

### [T153.9] Build sign-in and failure states

- **Area:** Sign-in UI
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add `/auth/sign-in` using the Auth provider and structured error boundary, with generic credential failure plus explicit loading, validation, unverified-email, offline, throttled, expired-session, and success behavior while leaving all guest routes accessible.
- **Exit criteria:** Route/provider tests and a browser walkthrough prove a verified user can sign in and reach `/app/home`, unknown-email and wrong-password attempts receive the same public credential response, and unverified, invalid, offline, throttled, and repeat-submit cases remain deterministic without placing credentials or account facts in URLs, logs, or analytics; a guest can always leave the route and continue into `/app`. Confirmed: this is UI-only — `AuthProvider.signInWithPassword` and every needed `AuthErrorCategory` (including the enumeration-safe `invalidCredentials` collapse of `invalid_credentials`/`user_not_found`) already existed from T153.4/T153.5, so no provider or error-model changes were made. Added `src/features/auth/model/sign-in-validation.ts` (pure `validateSignInFields`, 5 tests) plus `src/features/auth/components/sign-in-screen.tsx` (follows `reset-password-screen.tsx`/`auth-callback-screen.tsx`'s structural pattern: loading branch for `initializing`, an immediate `router.replace('/app/home')` redirect branch for an already-`authenticated` visitor, and the form otherwise) and a thin `src/app/auth/sign-in.tsx` route. The submit-error copy map renders one fixed string (`'Incorrect email or password. Try again.'`) for `invalidCredentials` regardless of whether the underlying code was `invalid_credentials` or `user_not_found`, proven by a dedicated same-copy assertion in `sign-in-screen.test.tsx` (16 tests) alongside loading, authenticated-redirect, empty-field validation, unverified-email, offline, rate-limited, expired-session, success (exact trimmed-argument call + single `/app/home` replace), a guest "Continue as guest" escape hatch (never calls `signInWithPassword`), no-raw-password-in-rendered-text, and `logAuthWarning`/`trackAuthEvent` call assertions. Found and fixed a real re-entrancy race while writing the repeat-submit test: two synchronous `onPress()` calls in the same tick both read the same stale `isSubmitting` closure value (React doesn't re-render between them), so the state-only guard the plan sketched let both calls through; added a synchronous `isSubmittingRef` alongside the `isSubmitting` state (which still drives the button's disabled/label UI) so a same-tick double submit is actually blocked — the added test reproduces the race and would have failed (timeout) against the state-only guard. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export); a separate `yarn expo export --platform web` run confirmed `auth/sign-in.html` among the 24 static output routes. Per the plan, "browser walkthrough" here means the mocked-Jest + static-export verification used by every prior T153.x UI ticket, not a live Supabase round trip — T153.1's local Supabase environment has not merged (blocked on Docker unavailability in this sandbox).
- **Notes:** From S032. Depends on T153.4–T153.5. Sign-in is separate from T153.8 because it is an independently testable route and security surface. Followed the committed plan (`.claude/plans/T153.9.md`, deleted in this commit) with one deviation beyond the ref-guard fix above: reused the existing `expiredOrInvalidLink` category for a sign-in-specific "Your session has expired. Sign in again to continue." message, a judgment call (there's no live Supabase codepath yet to verify a real `session_expired` sign-in scenario against) flagged for correction once T153.1's live instance is available. Does not link to `/auth/sign-up` or `/auth/forgot-password` (T153.8/T153.10 have not merged) — deferred to T153.11.

### [T153.7] Build the fixed-destination PKCE callback foundation

- **Area:** Authentication callbacks
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add `/auth/callback` as the sole owner of manual PKCE code exchange. Exchange each code once, derive the completed Auth flow without accepting an arbitrary destination, route successful signup confirmation only to `/app/home` and password recovery only to `/auth/reset-password`, and clear sensitive callback parameters before subsequent navigation or analytics.
- **Exit criteria:** Automated route/provider tests prove exactly one exchange for a valid confirmation and recovery code, the two fixed success destinations, safe handling of missing, malformed, expired, reused, offline, and verifier-mismatched codes, and rejection of an attempted `next`/open redirect; a static web export emits the callback route without accessing browser globals, and the final browser URL plus application logs and Analytics contain no code or token. Confirmed: `AuthProvider.exchangeCodeForSession` (`src/features/auth/state/auth-provider.tsx`) calls `client.auth.exchangeCodeForSession(code)` and reads the runtime-only `redirectType` field (cast, documented, since the SDK's public TS types omit it) to distinguish recovery from signup-confirmation codes, without watching shared Auth state (race-free); `auth-provider.test.tsx` covers success with both `redirectType` values and error pass-through. `AuthCallbackScreen` (`src/features/auth/components/auth-callback-screen.tsx`, wired via the thin `src/app/auth/callback.tsx` route) reads only `code`/`error`/`error_code` from `useLocalSearchParams` (never a `next`/destination param — there is no code path that could forward one), also checks `window.location.hash` for the same error codes (web-only, effect-only, guarded by `Platform.OS`) to catch Supabase's inconsistent hash-borne expired-link redirects, and guards the exchange with a `processedCodeRef` one-shot ref plus a separate `isMountedRef` (flipped only on true unmount, decoupled from the main effect's own dep-driven cleanup) so a late resolution or a params-identity-changing re-render can't double-exchange or navigate after unmount. `router.replace` (never `push`) lands on exactly `/app/home` or `/auth/reset-password`, leaving a clean final URL with no query string. `auth-callback-screen.test.tsx` (12 tests) proves: a valid confirmation code exchanges once and redirects to `/app/home`; a valid recovery code exchanges once and redirects to `/auth/reset-password`; an injected `next` param is never read or forwarded; missing and malformed (array) `code` never exchange; query-string `error`/`error_code` and hash-fragment `error_code` (via a scoped `window`/`Platform.OS` stub matching the existing `use-cached-onboarding-status.test.tsx` pattern) are categorized without exchanging; expired/reused/verifier-mismatch (`flow_state_expired`, `bad_code_verifier`) and offline (`TypeError: Failed to fetch`) errors show fixed category copy with no raw error text ever rendered or logged unredacted; and a params-identity-changing re-render (a fresh `useLocalSearchParams()` object each render, same `code` value — the realistic trigger for the guard, not a contrived StrictMode double-invoke) still exchanges and navigates exactly once. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export), and the export confirmed `auth/callback.html` among the static output routes without any Node-side `window`/Supabase-client access.
- **Notes:** From S032 and roadmap email/redirect security. Depends on T153.4–T153.6. The successful email-change destination is deliberately added later by T154.1. Production and local callback URLs must match T153.1–T153.2 exactly. Followed the committed plan (`.claude/plans/T153.7.md`, deleted in this commit) with one deviation: the plan's suggested `cancelled` flag set from the main effect's own cleanup would have been poisoned by React's normal cleanup-before-re-run semantics on a params-identity-changing re-render (not just true unmount), silently skipping the eventual navigation after the one-shot ref already blocked the re-exchange; split into a separate `isMountedRef` tied to its own `[]`-deps effect so only a genuine unmount suppresses the async continuation.

### [T153.6] Build the recovery-session password reset route

- **Area:** Password reset UI
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add the public static route `/auth/reset-password`, but permit password submission only while the Auth provider holds a valid password-recovery session. Provide confirmation, mismatch, weak-password, expired-session, offline, loading, and completion states through the structured Auth boundary.
- **Exit criteria:** Route/provider tests prove a valid injected recovery session can set a compliant new password exactly once, while a direct visit without recovery state, an expired session, mismatched or weak input, offline submission, and repeat submission are handled deterministically without exposing a password, session, or email in the URL, logs, or analytics; the web export emits the reset route. Confirmed: `deriveAuthState` (`src/features/auth/model/auth-state.ts`) now derives a distinct `passwordRecovery` status off Supabase's real `PASSWORD_RECOVERY` event, threading `previous` state through so a later `TOKEN_REFRESHED` cannot silently promote a recovery session to `authenticated`; `AuthProvider.completePasswordRecovery` (`src/features/auth/state/auth-provider.tsx`) independently refuses `updateUser` unless `stateRef.current.status === 'passwordRecovery'`, returning a `session_not_found` error otherwise (provider-level tests in `auth-provider.test.tsx` cover both the reject-when-not-recovering and forward-both-success-and-failure cases). `auth-errors.ts` gained `session_not_found`/`session_expired` mapped to `expiredOrInvalidLink`. New `password-policy.ts` provides the shared `MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH`/`validateNewPassword`. The route (`src/app/auth/reset-password.tsx`, thin wrapper) and `ResetPasswordScreen` (`src/features/auth/components/reset-password-screen.tsx`) render loading/expired-invalid/form/completion branches keyed off `useAuthState()`, with a local `completed` one-shot flag plus the state model's own reload-drops-recovery behavior together making repeat submission impossible; `reset-password-screen.test.tsx` proves a valid injected recovery session calls `completePasswordRecovery` exactly once, a direct guest visit and a plain `authenticated` (non-recovery) visit both show the same expired/invalid copy with no inputs, mismatched/weak input never calls the action, an offline-shaped error keeps the form for retry, a `session_expired` error shows expired-specific copy, and no rendered text/mock-call argument contains the raw password outside the controlled input value. `yarn expo export --platform web` (both via `verify.mjs` and a standalone run) confirmed the static output includes `auth/reset-password.html` among the 22 exported static routes. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test: 77/77 auth-suite tests plus full suite, lint, typecheck, web export).
- **Notes:** From S032. Depends on T153.4–T153.5. This route precedes T153.7 so Expo Router typed routes can validate the callback's fixed recovery destination in every intermediate commit. Followed the committed plan (`.claude/plans/T153.6.md`, deleted in this commit) as written with no material deviation.

### [T136] Show the footer bar only on the Profile route

- **Area:** Navigation / app shell
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `AppFooterBar` currently renders inside `CustomTabList` (`src/components/app-tabs.web.tsx`), used app-wide. Render it only when the active route is `/app/profile`, and remove the bottom-padding allowance other routes (Home, Sesh, Tricks, Stats) currently reserve for it.
- **Exit criteria:** The footer bar with "Leave app" renders on `/app/profile` and does not render on `/app/home`, `/app/sesh`, `/app/tricks`, or `/app/stats`, at both mobile and desktop widths; scrollable content on those other routes no longer reserves space for a footer that isn't there (confirmed via each route's bottom padding/inset). Confirmed: `CustomTabList` (`src/components/app-tabs.web.tsx`) now reads `usePathname()` from `expo-router` and only renders `AppFooterBar` when `pathname === '/app/profile'`; new unit tests in `app-tabs.web.test.tsx` mount `CustomTabList` with a mocked `usePathname` returning each of `/app/home`, `/app/sesh`, `/app/tricks`, and `/app/stats` and assert `findFooterBar` throws (no `testID="app-footer-bar"` node), plus a positive case asserting the footer renders on `/app/profile`. The conditional render doesn't depend on viewport width, so this behavior is identical at mobile and desktop widths. Removed the footer-reservation bottom padding from every non-Profile scrollable screen that previously carried it: `daily-home.tsx` (Home), `stats/index.tsx` and `stats/[trickId].tsx` (Stats), `tricks/index.tsx`'s custom-trick form scroll and `trick-directory.tsx`'s shared list (Tricks, plus its `goals`/`notes`/`laced` sub-routes and the Sesh multi-trick picker, all of which share the same component), and `sesh.tsx`/`multi-trick-sesh.tsx` (Sesh) -- each dropped from `120`/`110`/`140` (+ the native-only, web-inert `BottomTabInset`) down to a plain `24px` end-of-scroll allowance, leaving `profile/index.tsx`'s `120 + BottomTabInset` untouched since Profile still renders the footer. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).
- **Notes:** From story S005. Reverses part of T126's own scope; T126's archived entry in `TASKS_DONE.md` stays as-written. Used `expo-router`'s `usePathname()` to gate rendering within `CustomTabList` rather than duplicating `AppFooterBar` per-route. No interactive browser preview tool was available in this run; relied on the unit tests directly exercising `CustomTabList`'s render output per mocked pathname (functionally equivalent to a browser check for a conditional-render change with no width-dependent styling) plus the full automated verification suite.

### [T137] Give the screenshot showcase heading a more prominent visual treatment

- **Area:** Landing page
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `ScreenshotHeading` ("See it in action", `landing-page.tsx`) is 17px/900-weight body-adjacent text. Give it a larger, more visually distinct treatment consistent with the page's other section headings.
- **Exit criteria:** `ScreenshotHeading` renders visibly larger and stylistically distinct (size, color, and/or letter-spacing) from surrounding body copy — confirmed via a resolved-style unit test asserting its font-size increased from the current 17px. Confirmed: `ScreenshotHeading` (`landing-page.tsx`) now renders at `font-size: 28px` with `letter-spacing: -0.6px` (up from 17px/900-weight body-adjacent text), matching the page's `Headline`/`FeatureHeading` voice as a distinct section heading. A new test in `landing-page.test.tsx` (`LandingPage screenshot showcase heading`) mounts the page, resolves `ScreenshotHeading`'s style via `resolvedStyleFor`, and asserts its font-size is greater than the previous 17px. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).
- **Notes:** From story S007. Exact size/treatment is an implementer call, matching the page's existing voice (e.g. compare to `Headline`/`FeatureHeading`).

### [T142] Substantially enlarge the landing hero animation

- **Area:** Landing page
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `LandingHero`'s `HeroPanel` caps at `max-width: 320px`, reading as small per repeated QA feedback. Increase it noticeably at both mobile and desktop widths.
- **Exit criteria:** `HeroPanel`'s resolved size is noticeably larger than the current 320px cap at both mobile and desktop viewport widths — confirmed via a resolved-style unit test — while T133 (S002)'s no-overlap check with "Why Daily Dama" still passes. Confirmed: `HeroPanel`'s `max-width` raised from 320px to 440px (`landing-hero.tsx`); a new `landing-hero.test.tsx` test resolves the panel's style via `StyleSheet.flatten` and asserts `maxWidth` is greater than 320. Re-verified T133's mobile-overlap fix (the `margin-bottom: 24px` clearing HeroPanel's hard-edged `boxShadow`) against the enlarged panel with a headless Chromium (Playwright) session against the dev server at 320/375/414/1280px: at every width the eyebrow's top edge sits 16px below the rendered shadow's bottom edge (`heroBox.bottom + 8`), an unchanged clearance since the margin is a fixed value independent of panel width — no new overlap introduced. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).
- **Notes:** From story S012. Chose 440px (37.5% larger than the old 320px cap) — HeroPanel is inside `Content`'s existing max-width column (620px mobile / 1100px desktop) with fixed horizontal padding, so the panel's own cap only binds once the column is wide enough (roughly >364px available width); below that (e.g. the 320px viewport) the panel already fills 100% of the available column regardless of the cap, unaffected by this change. `HeroPanel`'s `max-width` isn't viewport-dependent (no `$isWebLayout`-style breakpoint, unlike `Content`/`ScreenshotItem`), so a single resolved-style assertion covers both mobile and desktop.

### [T143] Make the entire trick goal card tappable

- **Area:** Goals
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** `GoalRow`/`GoalCard` (`src/features/goals/components/trick-goals-screen.tsx`) only makes its "Practice toward this goal" link and "Delete" button individually tappable. Make the whole card one tap target for the primary (practice) action, matching the press feedback already used for tappable rows elsewhere (e.g. `RowAction` in `trick-directory.tsx`), while keeping Delete as its own distinct control.
- **Exit criteria:** Tapping anywhere on a goal card other than its Delete control triggers the same navigation as today's "Practice toward this goal" link, with visible press feedback; tapping Delete still opens its own confirm step and does not also trigger navigation. `yarn test --runInBand` passes, including a test asserting a card-level press invokes the practice navigation and that pressing Delete does not.
- **Notes:** From story S013. No literal "Choose" button exists on `GoalCard` today — the app's "Choose"-labeled full-row tap pattern lives in the trick picker (`trick-directory.tsx`) used when adding a new goal, which is what the underlying feedback was referencing for feel, not a component to copy verbatim. Once the whole card is tappable, the now-redundant button-styled "Practice toward this goal" label can be dropped or restyled as plain (non-button) text — exact treatment is an implementer call. Nesting Delete's own `Pressable` inside the card's new outer `Pressable` needs its press event to not also fire the card's primary action (stop propagation on the inner press, or position Delete outside the primary tap target instead).

### [T144] Show which trick each goal belongs to, directly on the goal card

- **Area:** Goals
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Neither `TrickGoalsScreen` (a single trick's goals) nor `AllTrickGoalsScreen` (all tricks with goals) shows a trick-identifying heading gated on goal count — the latter always shows the trick name as a section title regardless of count, and the former shows no trick name inside the card at all. Make both show a "<Trick name> goals" heading only when that trick has more than one goal.
- **Exit criteria:** On both `TrickGoalsScreen` and `AllTrickGoalsScreen`, a trick with more than one goal shows a heading reading "<Trick name> goals" (e.g. "Big Cup goals") grouping its cards; a trick with exactly one goal shows no such heading. `yarn test --runInBand` passes, including tests covering both the single-goal (no heading) and multi-goal (heading present, correct trick name) cases on both screens.
- **Notes:** From story S014. `AllTrickGoalsScreen`'s existing `SectionTitle` (always-shown, per `group.trickName`) needs to become conditional on `group.progress.length > 1` rather than a new mechanism; `TrickGoalsScreen` needs the heading added since it currently has none. Raised from `Implementer` to `Required` during scoping review.

### [T145] Eliminate the visible flash of unstyled content when the landing page loads

- **Area:** Web launch surface (cross-cutting)
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** The landing route (`src/app/index.tsx`) renders under the same provider tree as the rest of the app (`src/app/_layout.tsx`: custom-tricks, practice-history, goals, theme, and profile providers all mount above it), and its CTA/redirect logic reads `hasCompletedOnboarding` from `useUserProfile()`, which returns placeholder state until `AsyncStorage` hydration finishes. QA reports a jarring sequence of sub-second paints as a result. Scope rehydration-driven UI to the `/app` routes only, and make the landing page's first paint depend on as little async state as possible — ideally just the one flag it needs to pick a CTA variant.
- **Exit criteria:** Loading `/` shows its final visual state on first paint, with no visible sequence of intermediate/partial states before it settles — confirmed via a frame-by-frame browser recording at both mobile and desktop widths — and the landing route's render path no longer blocks its first paint on any persistence provider's async hydration. Confirmed: moved `CustomTrickProvider`, `UserProfileProvider`, `PracticeHistoryProvider`, `NoteProvider`, `GoalProvider`, `AppThemeProvider`, and `KenteiRankAutoPromotion` out of the root `src/app/_layout.tsx` and down into `src/app/app/_layout.tsx`, so `/` no longer mounts or waits on any of them; added `DefaultAppThemeProvider` (`resolveTheme({})`, no user preferences) at root so the CTA button's `styled(Button)` still resolves real theme colors outside `UserProfileProvider`. Added a new synchronous, pre-hydration `readCachedOnboardingStatus`/`useCachedOnboardingStatus` (mirroring `read-cached-theme-background.ts`'s `getItem`-injection and `useSyncExternalStore` hydration-gate precedent) and swapped `LandingCta` and `useRedirectOnboardedVisitorHome`'s `useUserProfile()` calls for it. Verified in-browser via the Expo dev server at both a desktop and a 375px mobile viewport, for both a fresh visitor (no cached profile) and a returning visitor (seeded `localStorage` with a completed-onboarding profile, `?from=app` to skip the redirect): each settled directly into its final CTA copy/colors with no intermediate loading/placeholder frame, and the CTA button resolved real (non-`undefined`) ink/yellow colors from `DefaultAppThemeProvider`. Confirmed `/app/home`, `/app/tricks` still hydrate the seeded profile correctly (Rank 5 frontier, focused-tricks state) with no console errors. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test: 805 tests, lint: 0 errors, typecheck, web export); the static export's prerendered `/` HTML contains no persistence-provider fallback text (`"Loading your practice..."`/`"Loading your tricks..."`) while `/app/home`'s prerendered HTML still shows its own hydration fallback, confirming the boundary moved as intended.
- **Notes:** From story S015. `UserProfileProvider` already exposes a `hasHydrated` flag (`src/features/profile/state/user-profile-provider.tsx:57`) that isn't currently used to gate anything on the landing route. A precedent for synchronously reading a single persisted value before async hydration completes already exists (`src/features/profile/persistence/read-cached-theme-background.ts`) — worth consulting during planning, though the exact mechanism (sync pre-read vs. restructuring the provider tree vs. something else) is this task's `Required` planning pass to decide, per this project's persistence-boundary rule in `AGENTS.md`. Plan committed at `.claude/plans/T145.md` also flagged and fixed a pre-existing bug (the CTA button's theme dependency had no fallback outside `UserProfileProvider`) as part of this change, and accepted a documented tradeoff: a returning user with custom theme colors now sees the landing CTA button in the fixed default palette (matching every other landing surface) rather than personalized colors, and navigating `/app/*` → `/` → `/app/*` now re-triggers each persistence domain's hydration fallback on the way back in, since the providers mount/unmount with route-group navigation instead of staying mounted for the app's lifetime.

### [T146] Let users preview a theme during onboarding before committing to it

- **Area:** Onboarding
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** In `kentei-onboarding.tsx`'s `themeSelect` step, `OnboardingThemeStep`'s `onSelectPreset` handler currently both applies the preset and calls `finishOnboarding()` immediately, so tapping any preset instantly exits onboarding. Split preview from commit: selecting a preset should only apply it live; a separate "Finish onboarding" action should be what advances into the app.
- **Exit criteria:** On the onboarding theme step, tapping a preset previews it (background/accent visibly change) without leaving the step or navigating; a distinct "Finish onboarding" control is what calls `completeOnboarding` and navigates to `/app/sesh`. `yarn test --runInBand` passes, including a test asserting preset selection alone does not trigger `completeOnboarding`. Confirmed: `onSelectPreset` in `kentei-onboarding.tsx` now only calls `setThemeColors` (the same live-apply/persist mechanism `RainbowThemePresets` uses on the Profile screen); `OnboardingThemeStep` gained a distinct `onFinish` prop and a primary "Finish onboarding" button (styled like `onboarding-selection-step.tsx`'s `FinishButton`), wired to the unchanged `finishOnboarding` closure; `onSkip` untouched. Also threaded `selectedPresetId`/`isPresetSelected` through to `ThemePresetGrid` so the active preset shows a selection indicator during preview, mirroring the Profile screen. Verified in-browser via the Expo dev server: tapping a preset (Blue) updated the persisted profile's `themeBackgroundColor`/`themeAccentColor` and the underline/accent-color indicator without navigating or setting `hasCompletedOnboarding`; a separate "Finish onboarding" tap then navigated to `/app/sesh` with `hasCompletedOnboarding: true`. `kentei-onboarding.test.tsx`'s single preset test was split into "previews a preset without finishing onboarding" (asserts `router.replace` not called, `hasCompletedOnboarding: false`, persisted colors match, selection indicator true) and "finishes onboarding after previewing a preset..." (asserts `router.replace('/app/sesh')`, `hasCompletedOnboarding: true`, rank/trick data as before). `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps.
- **Notes:** From story S016. Marked `Required` because it changes `kentei-onboarding.tsx`'s own step/reducer flow (adding a distinct confirm action to the `themeSelect` step), which this project's Planning rules call out for a separate planning pass regardless of size. "Skip for now" (`onSkip`) already bypasses theme selection entirely and calls `finishOnboarding` directly — left as-is; only the preset-select path changed.

- **Description:** Add a pink entry to `RAINBOW_THEME_PRESETS` (`src/features/profile/model/theme-presets.ts`), following the existing `{id, label, backgroundColor, accentColor}` shape used by the current seven presets.
- **Exit criteria:** A pink preset appears in the theme picker alongside the existing presets and can be selected and applied like any other preset. `yarn test --runInBand` passes, including any preset-count or preset-list assertions updated for the new entry. Confirmed: added `{ id: "pink", label: "Pink", backgroundColor: "#fbdcea", accentColor: "#c22a75" }` to `RAINBOW_THEME_PRESETS`. All existing preset tests (`rainbow-theme-presets.test.tsx`, `use-resolved-theme.test.ts`, `kentei-onboarding.test.tsx`) iterate via `.each`/`.map` over the array, so no hardcoded count/index assertions needed updating. Verified in-browser via the Expo dev server: Pink appears in both the onboarding theme step and the Profile screen's Quick Presets grid, selecting it live-previews the pink background/accent with automatically derived dark text and button colors, and it persists through `finishOnboarding` into the rest of the app (Sesh/Profile nav bar). `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).

### [T153.5] Centralize Auth errors and sensitive-data redaction

- **Area:** Authentication observability
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Define structured, actionable Auth error categories and one redacted application logging/analytics boundary before any Auth route is added. Normalize enumeration-sensitive failures and prevent raw Supabase errors, callback URLs, or entered credentials from reaching UI, application-owned console calls, or Vercel Analytics.
- **Exit criteria:** Unit tests feed representative Supabase errors and URLs containing emails, passwords, JWT-like values, refresh tokens, PKCE codes, and nested identifiers through the boundary and prove UI categories remain actionable while application logs and Analytics events contain none of those values; the root Analytics `beforeSend` handling drops or sanitizes callback query and fragment data. Confirmed: added `src/features/auth/model/auth-errors.ts` (`categorizeAuthError`, a closed `AuthErrorCategory` union mapping Supabase `AuthApiError.code` values -- `invalid_credentials`/`user_not_found` intentionally collapse to one `invalidCredentials` category) and `src/features/auth/model/redact.ts` (`redactValue` deep-walks strings/arrays/objects with a regex pipeline for emails/JWTs/long opaque tokens plus a deny-listed-key-name fallback for short values like passwords; `redactUrl` sanitizes both query and fragment params via `URL`/`URLSearchParams`, with a regex fallback over the reconstructed string for free-text params like `error_description`). `src/features/auth/observability/auth-observability.ts` wraps `console.warn`/`@vercel/analytics`'s `track` as `logAuthWarning`/`trackAuthEvent`, with `trackAuthEvent`'s second parameter typed to only accept the closed category enum so a raw error/email/URL can't reach Analytics through it; `sanitizeAnalyticsEvent` reuses `redactUrl` and is wired into `_layout.tsx`'s `<Analytics beforeSend={sanitizeAnalyticsEvent} />` (its first-ever `beforeSend`, confirmed against the installed `@vercel/analytics/react` `BeforeSendEvent` type's `url: string` field). Also swapped `auth-provider.tsx`'s and `supabase-client.ts`'s two pre-existing `console.warn` calls to `logAuthWarning` for one console-call-site pattern app-wide. New tests: `auth-errors.test.ts` (12 cases including the collapsed invalid-user/wrong-password category and offline detection), `redact.test.ts` (10 cases covering every listed sensitive-value type plus bounded-depth recursion and an unparseable-URL fallback), and `auth-observability.test.ts` (8 cases proving `logAuthWarning`/`trackAuthEvent`/`sanitizeAnalyticsEvent` never leak a raw value while `trackAuthEvent` stays a no-op off web). `yarn test --runInBand` (871 tests), `yarn lint` (0 errors), `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` all pass.
- **Notes:** From S032/S033 and roadmap Auth/client security requirements. Depends on T153.4. This relocates former T154.3 so signup, sign-in, recovery, callbacks, credential maintenance, and logout share the boundary from their first implementation. `accountAlreadyExists` enumeration handling at the UI call site is intentionally out of scope here and left for T153.8, per a comment in `auth-errors.ts`.

### [T148] Add a wood-grain "Raw" theme preset

- **Area:** Profile / theming
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** Add a "Raw" entry to `RAINBOW_THEME_PRESETS` using a flat wood-toned `{backgroundColor, accentColor}` pair (e.g. a warm tan background, deep brown accent) — the existing preset model has no texture/gradient/image support, and the product decision (confirmed during refinement) is to stay within that flat-color model rather than add one.
- **Exit criteria:** A "Raw" preset appears in the theme picker alongside the existing presets and can be selected and applied like any other preset. `yarn test --runInBand` passes, including any preset-count or preset-list assertions updated for the new entry. Confirmed: added `{ id: "raw", label: "Raw", backgroundColor: "#e8d9bd", accentColor: "#6b4423" }` to `RAINBOW_THEME_PRESETS` (now 9 entries, following T147's pink addition). All existing preset tests iterate via `.each`/`.map` over the array, so no hardcoded count/index assertions needed updating; the initial accent choice (`#5c3a21`) was darkened/lightened once — bg `#e8d9bd`, accent `#6b4423` — after `use-resolved-theme.test.ts`'s textMuted-contrast assertion for the Raw preset failed with a too-dark accent that flattened both the ink/cream contrast scores, mirroring how the existing Indigo/Violet presets pair a light background with a dark accent. Verified in-browser via the Expo dev server: Raw appears in the Profile screen's Quick Presets grid alongside Default through Pink, and selecting it live-applies and persists the tan background/brown accent (`themeBackgroundColor`/`themeAccentColor` in the stored profile). `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).

### [T149] Hide focused tricks while searching in trick goals

- **Area:** Tricks
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `TrickDirectory` (`src/features/tricks/components/trick-directory.tsx`) keeps its focused-tricks section visible regardless of `search.query`, obscuring search results in the trick-goals picker. Hide that section while a search query is present.
- **Exit criteria:** In the trick goals picker (`GoalTrickPicker`, which renders `TrickDirectory` with `showFocusedSection`), typing any text into the search input hides the focused-tricks section; clearing the search restores it. `yarn test --runInBand` passes, including a test covering both states. Confirmed: in `TrickDirectory`'s `sections` memo, the sectioning-ids argument to `createTrickSections` is now gated on `showFocusedSection && normalizedQuery.length === 0` instead of just `showFocusedSection`, so typing any query drops back to plain rank sectioning (no "Focused tricks" header) and clearing the query restores it. Consumer audit confirmed this is a no-op for the Tricks route and multi-trick sesh entry (the only other `showFocusedSection` callers), since neither passes a `search` prop. Added a `trick-directory.test.tsx` test that types a query, asserts "Focused tricks" is absent, clears it, and asserts it's restored. `yarn test --runInBand` (813 tests) and `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`'s test/lint/tsc/web-export steps all pass.
- **Notes:** From story S019. Confirmed in code that `sectioningTrickIds`/`createTrickSections` apply regardless of `search.query` today. Scope this to wherever `showFocusedSection` is used (currently only `GoalTrickPicker`) rather than every `TrickDirectory` consumer, unless another consumer is found to have the same section during implementation. Raised from `Implementer` to `Required` during scoping review — `TrickDirectory` is a shared component with several consumers across the app (onboarding, the main Tricks route, this picker), so confirming the change stays scoped to `showFocusedSection` callers and doesn't affect other consumers is worth a planning pass first.

### [T150] Automatically focus a trick when a goal is set for it

- **Area:** Goals / Tricks UI
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Creating a goal (`useGoalActions().addGoal`) for a trick that isn't currently in the user's focused tricks (`selectedTrickIds`, via `UserProfileProvider`) should add it to that list as part of saving the goal.
- **Exit criteria:** Creating a goal for a trick not currently in `selectedTrickIds` adds that trick to `selectedTrickIds` as part of the same save action; creating a goal for an already-focused trick is a no-op on the focused list. Confirmed via a unit test asserting the profile's focused-trick list before/after `addGoal` is called in both cases. Added `selectedTrickAdded`, an add-only mirror of the existing no-op-safe `selectedTrickRemoved` action, to the user-profile reducer/provider, and called it from `GoalProvider.addGoal` right after `dispatch({ type: 'goalAdded', goal })` (GoalProvider is rendered inside UserProfileProvider in `_layout.tsx`, so it can call `useUserProfileActions()` directly; no cross-queue write coordination needed since the two domains persist under distinct AsyncStorage keys, each behind its own `createWriteQueue`). New `src/features/goals/state/goal-provider.test.tsx` renders real `UserProfileProvider` + `GoalProvider` together and asserts `selectedTrickIds` before/after `addGoal` for both the not-yet-focused and already-focused cases; a reducer unit test in `user-profile.test.ts` covers `selectedTrickAdded` directly. This also surfaced that `all-trick-goals-screen.test.tsx` rendered a real `GoalProvider` without a `UserProfileProvider` ancestor (previously harmless since `GoalProvider` had no such dependency) and that `sesh.test.tsx` nested `UserProfileProvider` *inside* `GoalProvider` in 8 harnesses — both fixed to nest `UserProfileProvider` outermost, matching `_layout.tsx`'s real provider order. `yarn test --runInBand` (817 tests) and `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`'s test/lint/tsc/web-export steps all pass.
- **Notes:** From story S020. Marked `Required` because it has the Goals feature's `GoalProvider` triggering a write into the User Profile persistence domain's `selectedTrickIds` (via the existing `toggleSelectedTrick` action or an equivalent add-only variant) — a cross-domain interaction `AGENTS.md`'s persistence-boundary section calls out for a plan before implementation, including how the two providers' writes should be sequenced/queued relative to each other.

### [T152] Play an audio cue when a timed sesh's timer reaches zero

- **Area:** Sesh
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** No audio playback exists anywhere in the app today. Add one: when a timed sesh's countdown reaches zero via natural expiration (the `timerElapsed` action), play an audible cue as a component-level side effect of that transition — not an early manual end (`seshEnded`).
- **Exit criteria:** When a timed sesh's countdown reaches zero naturally, an audible cue plays once; ending a timed sesh early (before the deadline) does not play it. Confirmed via a test asserting the audio-trigger side effect fires on the `active`→`completed` transition driven by `timerElapsed` and not on one driven by `seshEnded`, plus a manual browser check that sound is audible at zero for a real timed sesh. Added `useAudioPlayer(require('@/assets/audio/sesh-timer-complete.wav'))` in `Sesh` (`src/features/sesh/components/sesh.tsx`) and a `timerElapsedTransitionRef` set only in `onTimerElapsed`, checked in the existing `active`→`completed` effect right before `recordCompletedSesh` and reset immediately after — so the cue plays exactly once per natural-expiration completion and never on a `seshEnded`-driven one. Two new `sesh.test.tsx` cases assert the mocked player's `play()` fires on natural expiration and not on early end. Manual browser check (Expo web dev server on an isolated worktree port, not the shared repo checkout): patched `HTMLMediaElement.prototype.play` in the live page to capture calls, ran a real 1-minute timed sesh to natural zero — `play()` fired exactly once against the `sesh-timer-complete.wav` asset — then ran a second timed sesh and ended it early via "End Sesh" (recorded total time 00:08 of the 01:00 plan) — no `play()` call recorded. `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` all pass (`node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`, full sequence, not `--skip-export`).
- **Notes:** From story S022. Uses `expo-audio`, this SDK's supported audio API for Expo ~57 (`expo-av` is deprecated and not in the project's dependencies). A root-level `__mocks__/expo-audio.ts` manual Jest mock was required because the real `expo-audio` module crashes under `jest-expo` at import time for any test transitively importing `sesh.tsx`; the placeholder timer-complete `.wav` asset is committed alongside it.

### [T156.1] Define identity-scoped storage and generation ownership

- **Area:** Shared persistence
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Introduce an explicit `guest` / `user:<verified-user-id>` identity model, a scoped `KeyValueStorage` adapter, and generation tokens that let async persistence/sync results prove they still belong to the current identity.
- **Exit criteria:** Unit tests prove identical logical keys resolve to isolated physical namespaces, unverified or malformed identities are rejected, generation changes invalidate late results, and the adapter never accepts identity from mutation payload data. Confirmed: added four pure modules per the committed plan — `src/shared/persistence/identity.ts` (binary `guest`/`user:<uuid>` `Identity`, strict UUID validation, `identityNamespace`), `src/shared/persistence/identity-context.ts` (`createIdentityContext` with a monotonic generation counter, `withGenerationGuard`), `src/shared/persistence/scoped-storage.ts` (`createScopedKeyValueStorage`, deriving the physical key only from the live identity context on every call, never from call arguments), and `src/features/auth/model/identity-from-auth-state.ts` (pure `deriveIdentityFromAuthState` mapping, colocated in `auth` to keep `shared/` feature-agnostic). Each new file has a directly corresponding test file proving its exit criterion: `identity.test.ts` (UUID acceptance/rejection incl. colon-injection case, namespace/equality proofs), `identity-context.test.ts` (same-identity `setIdentity` calls don't bump generation; `withGenerationGuard` reports `stale` for a late result after a mid-flight identity change and `applied` otherwise), `scoped-storage.test.ts` (an in-memory fake `KeyValueStorage` proves identical logical keys resolve to isolated physical namespaces per identity, that payload content passed to `setItem` never affects the resolved physical key, and that identity is read fresh per call rather than memoized at construction), and `identity-from-auth-state.test.ts` (`initializing` maps to `undefined`, `guest` maps to `GUEST_IDENTITY`, well-formed `authenticated`/`passwordRecovery` sessions map to `user:<id>`, malformed `session.user.id` maps to `GUEST_IDENTITY` without throwing). `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passed all four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export).
- **Notes:** From S038 and roadmap synchronization security. Depends on T153.4. Do not move native credentials or add outboxes yet. Deliberately does not touch any of the five existing providers, `_layout.tsx`, or legacy unscoped keys — that wiring, stale-hydration cancellation, and a migration wrapper for existing unscoped data are T156.2's job.

### [T156.2] Rehydrate every provider from its current identity namespace

- **Area:** Persistent provider composition
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Inject identity-scoped storage into the profile, custom-trick, practice-history, goal, and note providers; on identity change, cancel stale hydration, restore the new namespace, and preserve each provider's safe fallback, write queue, and skipped initial write.
- **Exit criteria:** Provider integration tests switch guest → user A → user B → guest and prove each domain rehydrates only its own data, no empty initial state overwrites restored data, writes remain ordered, and a deliberately delayed user-A load cannot commit after user B becomes current. Confirmed: added a single `useIdentityScopedDomain` hook (`src/shared/persistence/use-identity-scoped-domain.ts`) implementing the shared hydration/generation/write-queue mechanics once, and a new `IdentityProvider` (`src/shared/persistence/identity-provider.tsx`) as the single Auth-derived source of truth wrapping the five-provider nest in `src/app/app/_layout.tsx`. `hasHydrated` is redefined as "hydrated for the current identity generation" (`hydratedGeneration === generation`) rather than a one-shot boolean, so every identity switch briefly re-shows each provider's existing fallback screen — a deliberate, flagged product tradeoff that closes the write-race window by construction. A load started for a superseded generation is discarded before dispatch; a queued write tagged with a superseded generation self-discards immediately before `setItem` is actually called (checked synchronously, closing the race regardless of how long the caller-supplied `save` takes to get there). A new `createGuestMigratingStorage` (`src/shared/persistence/scoped-storage.ts`) preserves pre-T156.1 guest data at the old bare key via a one-time read-and-write-through fallback. All five providers (profile, custom-tricks, practice-history, goals, notes) were mechanically converted onto the shared hook with no reducer/action changes. New `src/features/auth/state/__tests__/identity-rehydration.test.tsx` mounts the real five-provider tree under real `AuthProvider`/`IdentityProvider` and real `AsyncStorage`, seeding distinct guest/user-A/user-B fixtures and driving actual Auth transitions; it covers per-identity rehydration in sequence, cross-identity write isolation, and the delayed-load-discard race (using two representative domains — user profile and custom tricks — since all five share the identical `useIdentityScopedDomain` call shape already proven in isolation by `use-identity-scoped-domain.test.tsx`'s fake-domain unit tests; the full five-domain isolation matrix is T156.3's job). Fixed three pre-existing tests (`kentei-rank-auto-promotion.test.tsx`, `kentei-onboarding.test.tsx`, `all-trick-goals-screen.test.tsx`) that asserted against the old bare `AsyncStorage` key directly — writes now correctly land at the guest-namespaced key. Also fixed a set of `react-hooks/refs`/`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` violations from the project's enabled React Compiler: `IdentityProvider`'s context is now created via lazy `useState` instead of a render-time ref check, its `setHandle` call is deferred to a microtask (matching `auth-provider.tsx`'s existing convention) instead of calling `setState` synchronously inside the effect body, and the hook's `optionsRef` sync moved into a layout effect. Notably, an attempt to eliminate `optionsRef` entirely in favor of depending directly on the (React-Compiler-memoized) inline callbacks each provider passes caused an infinite hydration loop and OOM crash under `yarn test`, because Jest's transform doesn't run the React Compiler babel pass — reverted to the ref-based design with a narrowly scoped, documented `eslint-disable` for the one line the linter can't reason about (a ref read inside an async callback body invoked later, never synchronously during render). `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`'s four steps (999 tests across 91 suites, lint, typecheck, web export) all pass.
- **Notes:** From S038. Depends on T156.1. This changes provider orchestration but not reducer state shapes. Two independent implementation attempts existed on this branch across interrupted sessions; the smaller one (touching only `scoped-storage.ts`) was redundant with this one's `createGuestMigratingStorage` and discarded without being merged.

### [T156.3] Prove cross-account local-data isolation

- **Area:** Persistence security testing
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add an end-to-end provider test matrix that seeds distinct profile, custom-trick, practice-history, goal, note, and pending-metadata fixtures for guest, user A, and user B and exercises repeated identity switches.
- **Exit criteria:** One automated matrix proves no identity can render, overwrite, or submit another identity's fixtures; a late queued write and a late hydration result are both discarded after switching; and returning to each identity restores exactly its own state. Confirmed: added `src/features/auth/state/__tests__/identity-isolation-matrix.test.tsx`, mirroring T156.2's real five-provider-tree test harness (`AuthProvider` > `IdentityProvider` > `CustomTrickProvider` > `UserProfileProvider` > `PracticeHistoryProvider` > `NoteProvider` > `GoalProvider`) but extending coverage to all five identity-scoped domains (profile, custom tricks, practice history, goals, notes) instead of the two representative ones T156.2 covered. Four tests: (1) a full seed/switch/re-switch/switch-back matrix across guest → user A → user B → user A → guest proving every domain renders exactly the current identity's fixtures at every step; (2) a real user-driven write through each domain's own action hook (`toggleSelectedTrick`, `addCustomTrick`, `recordCompletedSesh`, `addGoal`, `addNote`) proving no write lands in, or is later visible under, another identity's namespace; (3) a delayed `AsyncStorage.getItem` load race (reusing T156.2's capture-before-replace `getMockImplementation()` gating technique) on notes, proving a stale load for a superseded identity is discarded and never regresses the tree to a hydration-fallback screen; (4) a late-queued-write race on goals, which turned out not to need an artificial `AsyncStorage.setItem` gate at all — dispatching the write and emitting the identity switch in the same synchronous tick (before any `await`) reliably produces the real race the exit criteria describe, because `useIdentityScopedDomain`'s write-queue only checks its captured `taggedGeneration` inside a `.then()` microtask that can't run until every synchronous effect from that commit (including the identity-switch effect that bumps the generation counter) has already completed; an earlier attempt using an explicit `setItem` gate across separate `act()` calls was a false negative, since by the time the gate is even reached the guard checks had already both passed against the still-current generation. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs`'s four steps (test, lint, typecheck, web export) all pass. Pure test-only change; no production code touched.
- **Notes:** From S038. Depends on T156.2. Outbox-specific assertions are extended when T157.1 lands.

- **Notes:** Umbrella from S038. Complete T156.1–T156.3 before domain synchronization. The existing hydration fallback and skipped-initial-write behavior must survive unchanged. All three children (T156.1–T156.3) are Done.


### [T153.1] Establish reproducible local Supabase Auth development

- **Area:** Backend development environment
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add repository-owned Supabase CLI configuration, local Auth policy and email templates, ignored local environment files, and the smallest documented workflow needed to rebuild and test Auth locally without committing credentials. Configure verified email, a ten-character password minimum, one-hour JWTs, and `http://localhost:8081/auth/callback` as the fixed local callback.
- **Exit criteria:** From a fresh checkout with the documented CLI and Docker-compatible prerequisites, `supabase start` and a clean local database reset succeed; a nine-character signup is rejected, an unverified user cannot sign in, Mailpit receives the repository-owned confirmation email targeting the exact local callback, and `git check-ignore` confirms every documented local credential file is ignored while the tracked configuration contains only placeholders or environment references. Confirmed live with Colima/Docker: `npx supabase@2.115.0 start` booted the full stack cleanly and `db reset` completed without error. A 9-character signup against `/auth/v1/signup` returned 422 `weak_password` ("Password should be at least 10 characters"). A valid 10+ character signup left the account unconfirmed, and `/auth/v1/token?grant_type=password` for that account returned 400 `email_not_confirmed`. Mailpit received the repository-owned "Confirm your Daily Dama account" email (matching `supabase/templates/confirmation.html`, not Supabase's generic default), and its confirmation link's `redirect_to` was exactly `http://localhost:8081/auth/callback` once the request supplied `redirect_to` as a query parameter on `/auth/v1/signup` rather than nested under a JSON body `options` field — the README's curl examples were corrected to reflect this real GoTrue wire behavior (a body-nested `options.emailRedirectTo` is silently ignored by the raw REST endpoint and falls back to `site_url`). `git check-ignore -v` confirmed `supabase/.temp`, `supabase/.env.local`, and root `.env.local` are all ignored, and `supabase/config.toml` contains only placeholders/`env(...)` references, no live secrets. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --skip-export` (test/lint/typecheck) passes.
- **Notes:** From S032 and `BACKEND_ROADMAP.md` Phase 1 backend items 1, 3, and 6. This establishes the local backend contract only; application consumption begins in T153.4. When Auth flows are being tested, use the fixed port rather than selecting an arbitrary fallback port because the PKCE verifier and redirect origin must match.

- **Area:** Hosted Supabase Auth configuration
- **Priority:** High (inherited from parent T153)
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Create the single hosted free development project and mirror T153.1's verified-email password policy, exact web callback allowlist, ten-character password minimum, and one-hour access-token lifetime without creating the reserved production project.

Confirmed live via the Management API against the `Daily Dama Dev` hosted project (user-created, free plan): `GET`/`PATCH`/`GET` `/v1/projects/{ref}/config/auth` show `mailer_autoconfirm: false`, `password_min_length: 10`, `jwt_exp: 3600`, and `uri_allow_list` exactly `http://localhost:8081/auth/callback,https://dailydama.com/auth/callback`. A 9-character password signup against `/auth/v1/signup` returned 422 `weak_password` ("Password should be at least 10 characters"). A valid 10+ character signup left the account unconfirmed, and an immediate `/auth/v1/token?grant_type=password` for that account returned 400 `email_not_confirmed`. Record written to `supabase/HOSTED_DEV_CONFIG.md`. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --skip-export` passes.
