### [T155.1] Create private and exposed schemas with deny-by-default privileges

- **Area:** Database security boundary
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add migrations for the exposed `api` schema and non-exposed `private` schema, revoke default and automatic privileges, configure the Data API exposure deliberately, and grant no application-table access to `anon`.
- **Exit criteria:** A clean local reset succeeds; catalog assertions prove only `api` is exposed, `private` helpers are unreachable through the Data API, default privileges are revoked, and raw anonymous requests cannot access application resources. Confirmed live against a local stack (`supabase stop` then `start`, then `db reset`): new migration `supabase/migrations/20260822191737_create_private_and_exposed_schemas.sql` creates `api`/`private`, revokes the bootstrap's `postgres`-role default privileges and the direct/`PUBLIC`-pseudo-role `USAGE` grants on `public`, locks default privileges in `api`/`private` to nobody, and grants only `authenticated`/`service_role` `USAGE` on `api` and `service_role` `USAGE` on `private`. `supabase/config.toml`'s `[api].schemas` narrowed to `["api"]`. Catalog checks matched the plan exactly: `information_schema.schemata` lists exactly `api`, `private`; `has_schema_privilege` matrix is `api: anon=f/auth=t/service=t`, `private: anon=f/auth=f/service=t`, `public: anon=f/auth=f/service=t`; `pg_default_acl` returns 0 rows for both schemas. PostgREST probes: anonymous `GET /rest/v1/` returns 200; `Accept-Profile: private` and `Accept-Profile: public` both return 406 `PGRST106 Invalid schema`. A temporary probe function pair (created and dropped only for this manual check, not part of the migration) proved anon gets `401 42501 permission denied for schema api` calling `rpc/__t155_1_probe`, an authenticated caller with an explicit `api` grant gets `200 "api-hit"`, and the same authenticated caller requesting `Content-Profile: private` still gets `200 "api-hit"` (never `"private-hit"`), proving `private` is unreachable via the Data API regardless of auth or explicit profile request. A final `db reset` after dropping the probes confirmed the migration alone resets cleanly. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` passes all four steps (test/lint/typecheck/web-export).
- **Notes:** From S034 and roadmap Phase 2 item 1. Depends on T153.1.

### [T155.2] Model profiles and per-trick user state

- **Area:** Profile cloud schema
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add `api.profiles` and `api.user_trick_states` with `auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`, profile/trick-state invariants, appropriate indexes, revisions/tombstones where mutable, and no client-controlled ownership.
- **Exit criteria:** Migration tests accept every valid current `UserProfile` shape, reject invalid Kentei ranks, theme/streak/last-Sesh variants and duplicate trick states, prove cascade deletion, and show mutation payloads cannot assign another `user_id`. Confirmed live against a local stack: new migration `supabase/migrations/20260822193659_add_profiles_and_user_trick_states.sql` creates `api.profiles` (kentei-rank range/onboarding-pairing CHECKs, streak-threshold multiple-of-60000ms/1-60min CHECK, three hex-color-format CHECKs, a `last_sesh` shape CHECK covering both `openEnded`/`timed` plan variants and rejecting unknown keys) and `api.user_trick_states` (`unique(user_id, trick_id)`, `is_selected or is_manually_unlocked` CHECK), both with `user_id/trick_id` FKs to `auth.users(id) on delete cascade`, RLS enabled, and `for all` owner-only policies using both `USING` and `WITH CHECK` against `auth.uid()`. New pgTAP suite `supabase/tests/t155_2_profiles_and_trick_states.test.sql` (25 assertions) passes cleanly after `db reset`; deliberately dropping `profiles_kentei_rank_range` and re-running reproduced 2 failing subtests (non-zero exit), confirming the suite is not vacuous, then a `db reset` restored clean state. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --skip-export` passes (test/lint/typecheck); this is a pure-SQL ticket so web export was not run per the plan.
- **Notes:** From S034. Depends on T155.1. Current local profile combines these resources; mapping remains a later task.

### [T155.3] Model custom tricks and immutable practice sessions

- **Area:** Custom tricks / practice-history cloud schema
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add `api.custom_tricks` and `api.practice_sessions`, preserving stable trick IDs, authoritative name snapshots, non-nested sequence steps, insert-only historical sessions, client-fact timing, and user-scoped pagination indexes.
- **Exit criteria:** Database tests accept valid standalone tricks, sequences, and session snapshots; reject nested sequences, malformed discriminated steps, invalid timing/counts, and duplicate IDs; and prove an authenticated client cannot update or delete a practice session after insertion. Confirmed live against a local stack (`supabase start`, `db reset`): new migration `supabase/migrations/20260822200050_add_custom_tricks_and_practice_sessions.sql` adds `api.custom_tricks` (standalone|sequence discriminant, `(user_id, id)` composite primary key so ids stay stable and duplicates for the same user are rejected, kentei-rank/name/kind shape CHECKs, and a `private.enforce_custom_trick_step_invariants()` BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger validating per-step discriminated-union shape and rejecting nested sequences in both directions: a sequence step pointing at a row that is currently `kind = 'sequence'`, and converting a standalone trick into a sequence while another sequence already references it) and `api.practice_sessions` (insert-only: `grant select, insert` only, with only select/insert RLS policies and no update/delete grant or policy at all, `(user_id, id)` composite primary key, and CHECKs enforcing `completed_at >= started_at`, `duration_ms = completed_at - started_at`, non-negative `lace_count`, positive `planned_duration_ms` when present, and a known `origin`). Both tables cascade-delete via `auth.users(id) on delete cascade` and carry user-scoped pagination indexes (`(user_id, created_at desc, id)` / `(user_id, completed_at desc, id)`). New pgTAP suite `supabase/tests/t155_3_custom_tricks_and_practice_sessions.test.sql` (34 assertions, 59 total with T155.2's file) passes cleanly after `db reset`: valid standalone tricks, sequences referencing standard and standalone-custom tricks, and full/minimal practice sessions are accepted; malformed discriminated steps (missing/extra keys, unknown step kind), self-reference, direct and retroactive nested-sequence conversion, out-of-range kentei rank, blank names, bad timing/duration/lace-count/origin, and duplicate ids (same user) are all rejected with `23514`/`23505`; cascade deletion is proved for both tables. An authenticated-role RLS block (tests 31-34) proves a client cannot insert a session under another `user_id` (`42501`), cannot update or delete its own already-inserted session (`42501`), and the row is unchanged after both rejected attempts. Deliberately dropping `custom_tricks_enforce_step_invariants` and re-running reproduced exactly the 8 predicted failing subtests (8-14, 19; non-zero exit), confirming the suite is not vacuous, then `db reset` restored clean state and all 59 subtests passed again. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --skip-export` passes (test/lint/typecheck); this is a pure-SQL ticket so web export was not run per the plan.
- **Notes:** From S034 and documented custom-trick/Sesh invariants. Depends on T155.1.

### [T155.4] Model mutable goals and notes with tombstones

- **Area:** Goals / notes cloud schema
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add `api.goals` and `api.notes` with their discriminated variants, trick/session name snapshots, revisions, edit timestamps, soft-deletion tombstones, ownership constraints, and user-scoped ordering indexes.
- **Exit criteria:** Migration tests accept every current goal/note variant, reject malformed variants and invalid session references, increment revisions through valid edits, and prove a tombstoned record cannot be silently recreated by a stale mutation using the same identity/revision. Confirmed live against a local stack (`supabase start`, `db reset`): new migration `supabase/migrations/20260822201500_add_goals_and_notes.sql` adds `api.goals` (count|time discriminated union, `target_count`/`target_ms` mutual-exclusion and range CHECKs) and `api.notes` (trick snapshot, body, optional `practice_session_id` composite FK to `api.practice_sessions(user_id, id)`), both `(user_id, id)` primary key, no DELETE grant, and a shared `private.enforce_goal_note_mutation_invariants()` BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger that forces server-owned `revision`/`created_at`/`updated_at`/`deleted_at` on insert and unconditionally rejects any UPDATE against an already-tombstoned row (`23514`), regardless of the client's WHERE-clause revision. New pgTAP suite `supabase/tests/t155_4_goals_and_notes.test.sql` (43 assertions, 102 total with T155.2/T155.3) passes cleanly after `db reset`: valid/malformed goal and note variants, invalid and cross-user session references, revision increments through valid edits, and both a stale `UPDATE ... revision = <stale>` and an `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` upsert failing to resurrect a tombstoned goal/note are all exercised and pass. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --skip-export` passes (test/lint/typecheck); this is a pure-SQL ticket so web export was not run per the plan.
- **Notes:** From S034. Depends on T155.1. Historical name snapshots remain authoritative.

### [T155.7] Add explicit authenticated grants and row-level security

- **Area:** Database authorization
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Enable RLS in table-creation migrations and add least-privilege operation grants plus user-scoped `USING` and `WITH CHECK` policies for every exposed resource, with select/insert-only permissions for practice sessions.
- **Exit criteria:** Raw HTTP tests prove anonymous denial, user A isolation from user B across select/insert/update/delete, rejection of ownership changes, correct allowed operations for each table, and denial of practice-session updates/deletes even by the owner.
- **Notes:** From S034. Depends on T155.2–T155.6. Audit confirmed RLS/grants already correct on five of six tables; closed a real `profiles` `DELETE`-grant gap; added raw-HTTP anonymous-denial, cross-user-isolation, and grants/RLS-independence test coverage. Before writing any fix, drove real HTTP requests against a fresh local Supabase stack for every exit-criterion claim: RLS was already `ENABLE`d on all six exposed tables; every table had a distinct, column-scoped per-operation grant (`practice_sessions` already `SELECT, INSERT` only, with owner `PATCH`/`DELETE` already returning `403`/`42501`); cross-user isolation already worked end-to-end (a filtered `SELECT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE` against another user's row returns `200` with an empty array — RLS silently filters, no error); ownership reassignment was already rejected both on insert (`WITH CHECK`, `403`/`42501`) and on `user_id` repointing via `PATCH` (blocked earlier, by T155.5's column-level `UPDATE` grant never including `user_id` at all); anonymous access was already denied schema-wide (`401`, `42501`, "permission denied for schema api", because `anon` never got `USAGE ON SCHEMA api`). The one genuine gap: `api.profiles` still carried a leftover `grant ... delete` from T155.2, letting an owner hard-delete their own profile row, contradicting that table's own no-client-delete design (`user_trick_states`' and `custom_tricks`' `DELETE` grants are separately correct and deliberate, and were left untouched). Added `supabase/migrations/20260822220000_close_profile_delete_grant_gap.sql` (append-only, migrations are never edited in place): revokes `DELETE` on `api.profiles` from `authenticated`; splits the single `profiles_own_row for all` policy into explicit `profiles_select_own`/`profiles_insert_own`/`profiles_update_own` policies (matching T155.4's split-policy style for goals/notes), removing the latent trap where the `for all` policy still silently covered `DELETE` even with no grant reaching it; and adds `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` to all six exposed tables as defense-in-depth (verified live that the `postgres` migration role has `rolbypassrls = true` directly, so this changes no observable behavior today — documented honestly in the migration rather than claimed as tested). Added `supabase/tests/t155_7_grants_and_rls_independence.test.sql` (new pgTAP file, 10 assertions): proves grants alone block `practice_sessions` `UPDATE`/`DELETE`, `goals` `DELETE`, and `profiles` `DELETE` even with RLS disabled, and proves RLS alone blocks cross-user `profiles` `DELETE`, `practice_sessions` `UPDATE`, and `goals` `DELETE` even with each grant temporarily widened beyond its real surface. Added `supabase/tests/data-api/t155_7_anonymous_denial.test.mjs` (6 assertions: anonymous `GET` against every exposed table returns `401`/`42501`) and `supabase/tests/data-api/t155_7_cross_user_isolation.test.mjs` (47 assertions across all six tables: user B's filtered `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE` against user A's row returns `200` with an empty array using `Prefer: return=representation` to distinguish "zero rows touched" from a bare `204`; user A's `POST`/`PATCH` claiming or reassigning to user B's `user_id` is rejected `403`/`42501`; and the literal exit-criterion cases — `practice_sessions` owner `PATCH`/`DELETE`, `profiles` owner `DELETE`, `goals`/`notes` owner hard `DELETE` — are all rejected `403`/`42501`). Extended `supabase/tests/data-api/lib/local-supabase.mjs` with an `anonClient` helper and a `delete` method on `restClient`. Added a short paragraph to `supabase/README.md` documenting that `supabase/tests/data-api/` now also carries this authorization suite. Verified: `supabase db reset` + `supabase test db --local` passes all 150 pgTAP assertions across all 5 files (`t155_2`–`t155_7`); `yarn test:data-api` passes all 102 assertions; `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --skip-export` (test/lint/typecheck) passes.

### [T155.6] Enforce domain JSON invariants inside Postgres

- **Area:** Database validation
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add database-level validation for profile variants, goals, custom tricks, and sequence-step JSON so callers bypassing frontend runtime validation cannot store impossible domain states.
- **Exit criteria:** Parameterized raw Data API tests submit valid and malformed examples for every discriminated shape and confirm only valid records persist, including explicit rejection of nested sequences, unknown fields/variants, invalid ranks, and ambiguous goal payloads. Confirmed against a local stack (`supabase start`, `db reset`): before writing any test code, every scenario this ticket's notes flagged as suspicious (unknown top-level/nested fields on `profiles.last_sesh`, direct nested-sequence references, ambiguous `goals` payloads carrying both `target_count` and `target_ms`, forged server-owned columns, unrecognized column names) was driven directly over HTTP with a genuine signed-in JWT and found already correctly rejected by the T155.2–T155.5 migrations — **no new CHECK constraint, trigger, or grant was needed**; T155.2–T155.5 had already closed every gap. The actual gap was procedural: no automated, repeatable Data API (HTTP) test suite existed, even though the exit criteria specifically require "parameterized raw Data API tests." Added `supabase/tests/data-api/` (a `node:test` suite, dependency-free, mirroring `.claude/scripts/test`'s convention): `lib/local-supabase.mjs` (reads `supabase status -o json` for live connection info, creates/signs-in/deletes confirmed test users via the GoTrue admin API, and wraps `fetch` for the REST/Data API), plus `t155_6_profiles.test.mjs`, `t155_6_custom_tricks.test.mjs`, and `t155_6_goals.test.mjs` (55 assertions total) porting every valid/malformed case from the corresponding pgTAP files (`t155_2`/`t155_3`/`t155_4`) through the real Data API, including nested-sequence rejection in both directions (direct reference and retroactive standalone-to-sequence conversion), unknown top-level and nested JSON keys, invalid Kentei ranks, and ambiguous count/time goal payloads. Added Data-API-only cases pgTAP cannot exercise: forged server-owned columns (`42501`), unrecognized JSON keys that PostgREST itself rejects pre-database (`PGRST204`), and bulk (JSON-array) insert atomicity (one malformed element rolls back the whole batch). One genuine, reportable finding surfaced only through the HTTP path: converting a standalone custom trick already referenced by a sequence into a sequence itself now fails with `42501` (permission denied) rather than pgTAP's `23514` domain-check error, because T155.5's column-level UPDATE grant on `custom_tricks` never includes `kind` at all — stronger enforcement than the SQL-level test can observe, not a gap; documented inline in the test and not treated as a regression. Added `yarn test:data-api` (`package.json`) and a new "Running Data API JSON-invariant tests" subsection to `supabase/README.md`, deliberately not wired into `verify.mjs` (same reasoning as pgTAP: it needs a running local Supabase/Docker stack). Verified: `supabase db reset` + `supabase test db --local` still passes all 140 existing pgTAP assertions unchanged (pure regression, no migration touched); `yarn test:data-api` passes all 55 new assertions; `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (full sequence including web export) passes.
- **Notes:** From S034 and roadmap database-security requirements. Depends on T155.2–T155.4. T155.8 will consolidate this suite alongside pgTAP and T155.7's Data API suite into one CI-suitable command.

### [T155.5] Protect server-owned timestamps, revisions, and ownership

- **Area:** Database mutation controls
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add private trigger functions with explicit safe `search_path` values to own `created_at`, `updated_at`, revisions, receipt timestamps, and immutable ownership fields; restrict update grants so RLS is not the only protection.
- **Exit criteria:** Raw authenticated requests prove inserts and updates cannot choose or preserve forged server-owned values, valid mutations receive authoritative timestamps/revisions, every `SECURITY DEFINER` function has an audited fixed `search_path`, and the complete migration rebuild remains clean. Confirmed live against a local stack (`supabase start`, `db reset`): new migration `supabase/migrations/20260822210000_protect_server_owned_timestamps_and_ownership.sql` fixes `search_path = ''` on the two existing trigger functions (T155.3, T155.4), adds four new `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE`/`BEFORE INSERT` trigger functions (also `search_path = ''`) forcing server-owned `revision`/`created_at`/`updated_at` on `api.profiles`, `api.user_trick_states` (also pinning `id`/`trick_id` identity), and `api.custom_tricks` (also pinning `id`/`kind` identity), plus a receipt-timestamp trigger forcing `created_at` on `api.practice_sessions`, and revokes/re-grants column-scoped INSERT/UPDATE privileges on all six mutable tables (`profiles`, `user_trick_states`, `custom_tricks`, `practice_sessions`, `goals`, `notes`) so a forged server-owned column is rejected outright (`42501`) rather than silently overwritten. No function in `api`/`private` is `SECURITY DEFINER`, so that exit criterion holds vacuously; a pgTAP assertion pins the count at 0 so a future addition is deliberate. New pgTAP suite `supabase/tests/t155_5_protect_server_owned_timestamps.test.sql` (38 assertions, 140 total with T155.2–T155.4) passes cleanly after `db reset`, including trigger-level forced ownership on all four tables, `search_path` verification on all six trigger functions, the `SECURITY DEFINER` count assertion, and column-grant `42501` rejections independent of RLS. Required amendment to `supabase/tests/t155_4_goals_and_notes.test.sql`: the cross-user goal UPDATE assertion now targets `deleted_at` instead of `target_count`, since `target_count` is no longer UPDATE-grantable (goal-target editing is not yet exercised locally; see T157.7). `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --skip-export` passes (test/lint/typecheck); this is a pure-SQL ticket so web export was not run per the plan.
- **Notes:** From S034 and roadmap Phase 2 item 3. Depends on T155.2–T155.4.

Confirmed against a clean local Supabase stack: `t161_3_delete_account_acceptance.test.mjs` passes all three real-stack cases, covering all six resources, Auth cascade isolation, anonymous/signed-expired/wrong-user/stale/pre-deletion denial, old-token recreation rejection, and lost-response retry convergence. The consolidated `yarn test:db` passes all 7 steps (including pgTAP, Data API, Edge Functions, and cleanup reset); full `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --json` passes Jest, lint, typecheck, and web export. - **Area:** Account deletion UI

Added route, provider, model, screen, and account-entry tests; focused Auth tests pass (62 tests), and `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --json` passes all four steps (Jest, lint, typecheck, web export). Browser walkthrough was attempted through `run-auth-e2e.mjs` but blocked while checking local Supabase status in this environment; native validation is deferred by active platform scope. T161.5 owns local purge and lost-response reconciliation.

### [T161.5] Purge account state and reconcile deletion response loss

- **Area:** Account lifecycle / local persistence
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** On deletion success-or after a lost response when Auth verification proves the user no longer exists-remove credentials, account cache, outboxes, conflicts, import progress, and sync metadata, invalidate the identity generation, and enter a fresh guest namespace.
- **Exit criteria:** Integration tests cover normal success, response loss with missing user, response loss with user still present, offline retry, and a late in-flight sync result; completed deletion exposes no former account data to the new guest, does not erase unrelated guest/other-user namespaces, and cannot be reversed by stale work.
- **Notes:** From S039. Depends on T156.3, T158, T159.5, T160.2, and T161.4. AsyncStorage purge is accepted prototype cleanup, not claimed forensic erasure. Confirmed: `AuthProvider.deleteAccount` now purges the entire `user:<uuid>:` AsyncStorage namespace (new `purgeIdentityNamespace` in `src/shared/persistence/account-purge.ts`, typed to reject `GUEST_IDENTITY` at compile time) and signs out locally (`scope: 'local'`) on any null-error response from the `delete-account` Edge Function -- which the function's own idempotent `isNotFound` check makes indistinguishable between a fresh success and a reconciled lost-response retry, so both get identical treatment; a non-null error (user still present, or offline) leaves storage and the session untouched. Local sign-out reuses the already-tested `IdentityProvider` reactive generation-invalidation on `SIGNED_OUT`. `AccountDeletionScreen` now checks its `succeeded` flag before any `authState`-derived branch, so the success view survives `authState` flipping to guest moments later, with corrected non-transient copy. Five exit-criteria scenarios are covered: unit tests in `auth-provider.test.tsx` for normal success, reconciled response-loss-with-missing-user, response-loss-with-user-present, and offline retry; a new fully-wired integration test (`src/shared/sync/account-deletion-purge.integration.test.tsx`, modeled on `logout-guard.discard-isolation.test.tsx`) drives a real `SyncCoordinatorProvider` sync run with a deliberately deferred push, purges and switches to guest while it is still in flight, then resolves it late -- proving the late ack cannot land in the live guest namespace, cannot resurrect the purged namespace, and a seeded guest key plus a seeded unrelated second-user key both survive untouched. `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs --json` passes all four steps (1,749 Jest tests, lint, strict typecheck, web export). The account-deletion e2e suite (`e2e/auth/08-account-deletion.spec.ts`) was updated for the corrected copy and to assert the seeded `user:<uuid>:daily-dama:user-profile` localStorage key is gone after a real 204 success, but could not be run to completion in this environment: `run-auth-e2e.mjs`'s fixed `localhost:8081` Playwright web server port was already occupied by another process in this shared sandbox, and the app's local Auth policy fixes the callback origin at that port (see `supabase/README.md`), so no alternate port could be substituted. This mirrors the same environment-level e2e blocker recorded on T161.4's closing note; native/browser validation is deferred by active platform scope regardless.
### [T176] Fix Profile's Account section not rendering until a manual refresh

- **Area:** Authentication / Profile
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** After signing in and navigating to Profile without a manual page refresh, AccountSection doesn't show the signed-in state -- it only appears correctly after a refresh. Both AccountSection and AuthProvider read/expose state in the expected reactive shape on inspection (no obvious stale closure or missing dependency), so this needs a runtime investigation of the actual sign-in -> navigate -> render timing, not a known local fix.
- **Exit criteria:** A test or reproducible in-browser walkthrough proves signing in and navigating directly to Profile (no refresh) shows the Account section in its signed-in state; the root cause found during investigation is documented in this entry's completion notes. Met: `sign-in-to-account-section.integration.test.tsx` mounts the real AuthProvider/SignInScreen/AccountSection together and proves the signed-in Account section appears immediately after a successful sign-in submit, no refresh, no extra flush. Independently confirmed live: an Expo dev server against a local Supabase stack, driven with Playwright, signing in and clicking straight through to the Profile tab, consistently showed the authenticated Account section on first paint across every variant tried (direct navigation, Profile pre-mounted as a guest before sign-in, and an injected 400ms network delay on the token exchange).
- **Notes:** From S056. Root cause: this investigation could not reproduce the reported defect against current `development`. Reading the installed `@supabase/auth-js` (`GoTrueClient.js`) shows every `signInWithPassword`-shaped code path does `await this._saveSession(session); await this._notifyAllSubscribers('SIGNED_IN', session);` *before* returning -- so `AuthProvider`'s `onAuthStateChange`-driven `setState` to `authenticated` is guaranteed to happen before `signInWithPassword()`'s promise resolves, i.e. before `SignInScreen`'s own `router.replace('/app/home')` call. Separately, the Profile tab only exists inside the `/app/*` route group's tab bar (`AppTabs`), which is unreachable from the `/auth/sign-in` screen -- so there is no way to land on Profile before that resolution completes. Together these close off the race the ticket described: no window exists in the current code where Profile can mount while `AuthProvider` still reports a pre-authenticated status. The original report (from an earlier PR batch, S056) most likely predates intervening auth-focused work in this same area (T158's logout-guard rework, T159's guest-data-merge, T161's session-cascade hardening) that may have already resolved it, or it depended on backend/network conditions (e.g. a slow token round trip or a transient sync failure) not recoverable from static or fast-local-loopback dynamic testing alone. Added `sign-in-to-account-section.integration.test.tsx` as a durable regression guard: unlike every other Auth test in this codebase, it does not mock `auth-provider` itself -- it mounts the real `AuthProvider` + `SignInScreen` + `AccountSection` behind a fake Supabase client that mirrors the real SDK's `SIGNED_IN`-before-resolution ordering, so a future change that decouples them (the one thing currently guaranteeing no race window exists) would be caught here. Also noted, but deliberately left unfixed as out of scope for this ticket: during the live investigation, the concurrent post-sign-in domain-sync burst (profile/custom-tricks/practice-history/goals/notes, all fired together on the guest->user identity switch) occasionally hit a transient 401 from the local Supabase stack on one of the five requests, with the exact same auth header succeeding on the others in the same burst -- consistent with a local Docker/Kong connection-pool warm-up artifact rather than an application-level bug, and not reproducible reliably enough to treat as this ticket's root cause. If a future report describes the whole Profile screen (not just the Account section) staying blank/hidden after sign-in, that domain-sync burst -- which has no automatic retry today, only foreground/reconnect/manual triggers -- would be the first place to look.

### [T173] Show the current section in the web tab title

- **Area:** Navigation / web
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** No per-route title mechanism exists today (only the static "Daily Dama" from `app.json`). Add `expo-router/head`'s `<Head><title>...</title></Head>` to each of the five primary section routes (Home, Sesh, Tricks, Stats, Profile), rendering "Daily Dama: <Section>".
- **Exit criteria:** `yarn expo export --platform web --output-dir /tmp/daily-dama-web-export` succeeds and the exported HTML for each of the five section routes contains its expected `<title>` text; confirmed in-browser that navigating between sections updates the browser tab title. Met, via a deviation from the notes below (see Notes): all five exported HTML files (`app/home.html`, `app/sesh/index.html`, `app/tricks/index.html`, `app/stats/index.html`, `app/profile/index.html`) contain the correct `<title>Daily Dama: <Section></title>`; confirmed in a running `expo start --web` session that completing onboarding and clicking each of the five tab buttons updates `document.title` to match.
- **Notes:** From S053. Web-only by nature — no native/mobile conflict. Deviated from the planned "five small, identical additions": placing `<Head><title>` inside each screen component cannot work in this app, because every one of the five screens sits below `UserProfileProvider`'s hydration gate, which renders a "Loading your practice..." fallback (per AGENTS.md's persistence rules) in place of the real screen until AsyncStorage hydration resolves — during static export that hydration never resolves synchronously, so none of the five screens, and thus no route-level `<Head>`, ever render. Implemented instead as a single `<Head>` in `src/app/app/_layout.tsx`, mapping `usePathname()` to a section title and rendering unconditionally above the hydration gate. Verified client-side title updates by patching `requestAnimationFrame` in the browser console; the Playwright-driven Browser pane keeps `document.hidden === true` (confirmed via `document.visibilityState`), which throttles the real `requestAnimationFrame` that `react-helmet-async` (used internally by `expo-router/head`) defers its DOM commit through — a test-harness artifact, not a product defect, since the underlying title state was already proven correct via the exported HTML and via direct inspection of `Head`'s computed state.

### [T178] Let Enter submit the sign-up and sign-in forms on web

- **Area:** Authentication UI
- **Priority:** Low
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** Neither `sign-up-screen.tsx` nor `sign-in-screen.tsx` has any `onSubmitEditing`/web-Enter handling today. Add both: RN `onSubmitEditing` on each field advancing to the next field or submitting on the last, and a web keydown handler so pressing Enter anywhere in the form submits it the same way clicking the primary button would (only when validation would allow an attempt).
- **Exit criteria:** Component tests for both screens pass, including one asserting an Enter keypress triggers the same submit handler as clicking the primary button. Met: added onSubmitEditing wiring (Email -> focus Password -> for sign-up focus Confirm password -> submit on the last field), plus a web-only capture-phase `keydown` listener on `window` calling the same `handleSubmit` the primary button's `onPress` calls, gated only by the same `isSubmittingRef` re-entrancy guard the button's `disabled` state already relies on. A capture-phase (not bubble-phase) listener was required because react-native-web's `TextInput` calls `stopPropagation()` on every keydown, which would otherwise swallow the event before it reached a bubble-phase `window` listener. New tests in `sign-in-screen.test.tsx` and `sign-up-screen.test.tsx` cover per-field `onSubmitEditing` advancing without submitting, submitting from the last field, an Enter keydown from the captured web listener triggering the same submit handler as the primary button, and a non-Enter keydown being ignored. Verified live in a local `expo start --web` session: a real Enter keydown dispatched from inside the Password/Email field (bubbling, matching react-native-web's own event shape) triggered the identical validation/submit path as clicking the primary button on both screens. Full targeted test suites pass, plus `yarn tsc --noEmit`.
- **Notes:** From S059. Scoped to sign-up/sign-in only, not change-password/change-email/forgot-password — those are deliberately deferred, partly to avoid rework ahead of T179/T180's Account Security restructuring (both already `Done`). `password-input.tsx` gained optional `onSubmitEditing`/`returnKeyType`/`blurOnSubmit` props and ref forwarding to support this, backward compatible with its other call sites (`account-security-screen.tsx`, `reset-password-screen.tsx`, `account-deletion-screen.tsx`), which are untouched.

### [T187.1] Add a last-modified timestamp to profile, custom-trick, and goal records

- **Area:** Persistence / synchronization
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `note.ts` already carries `updatedAt`; `goal.ts` (`GoalBase`) has only `createdAt`, and neither `custom-trick.ts` nor `user-profile-persistence.ts` has any last-modified field at all. Add an `updatedAt: number` to each, set on every create and edit, threaded through each domain's local reducer, persistence envelope, and cloud mapping/repository contract.
- **Exit criteria:** Domain model, persistence round-trip, and cloud-mapping tests for profile, custom-tricks, and goals confirm `updatedAt` is set on creation, advances on every edit, and survives serialization and the existing sync transport's revision-guarded update path. Met: added `updatedAt` to `GoalBase`, `CustomTrickBase` (optional on `UserProfile`, since it has no meaningful pre-touch instant), threaded through `createGoal`/`applyGoalTargetEdit`/`createCustomTrick` defaults, the profile reducer via a single `dispatchWithTimestamp` wrapper, each domain's persistence validator, and each domain's cloud row->domain mapper (including a new `invariantViolation` check on goals mirroring notes, and excluding `updatedAt` from `ProfileScalarFields`/`extractScalarFields` so it never spuriously triggers a profile-row upsert in `diffProfileForOutboxOperations`). Full `yarn test --runInBand` (146 suites/1795 tests), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` all pass.
- **Notes:** From S068 (prerequisite). Per `AGENTS.md`, the custom-trick document's `version: 1` schema is explicitly provisional and may change in place — this is exactly that kind of sanctioned in-place change, not a v2 migration. Goals/profile have no comparable "this is provisional" note; confirmed neither's persisted shape is treated as frozen elsewhere (both use the same `_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1` local-storage pattern as notes/custom-tricks, with no "frozen v1 contract" language in `supabase/`). No Supabase migration was needed: `api.profiles`, `api.custom_tricks`, and `api.goals` already carry server-owned `updated_at` columns and mutation triggers from T155; this ticket was entirely app-layer.

### [T187.2] Wire automatic most-recent-wins conflict resolution and remove the manual conflict screen

- **Area:** Synchronization
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Using T187.1's new `updatedAt` field (and `note.ts`'s existing one), change each of the four mutable domains' sync drivers to resolve a detected conflict automatically by keeping the snapshot with the later `updatedAt`, instead of recording a `SyncConflictRecord` for manual choice. Remove `/app/profile/sync-conflicts.tsx`, `sync-conflict-list.tsx`, and `SyncStatusSection`'s "Review conflicts" link.
- **Exit criteria:** The existing per-domain conflict fault-injection suites (from T160.2/T160.3) are updated to assert automatic resolution by `updatedAt` instead of asserting a pending `SyncConflictRecord`, and pass; `sync-status.ts`'s conflict-count fact either resolves to zero permanently or is removed if no longer reachable; full app verification sequence passes (test, lint, typecheck, web export). Met: added `src/shared/sync/conflict-resolution.ts` (`resolveUpdatedAtConflict`/`parseServerRowUpdatedAt`, tie and unknown-server-clock both deterministically keep the server snapshot), deleted `src/shared/sync/conflict.ts` and its manual `SyncConflictRecord`/`conflictRecorded`/`conflictRemoved`/`conflictResolved` envelope machinery (its still-needed generic `ConflictSnapshot`/`isConflictSnapshot` JSON-safe-value guard, used only by the unrelated guest-import merge-conflict flow, moved to a new `src/shared/sync/json-snapshot.ts`), rewired all four domain sync hooks' push-loop `'conflict'` branch to resolve inline via `localMutationApplied` (local wins) or `operationAcknowledged`+the primary reducer's own `*Restored` action (server wins), trimmed `conflictCount`/`conflicts`/`resolveConflict` from every domain provider and `SyncCoordinatorStatus` (`conflictCount` now a literal `0`, per the exit criterion's explicit "resolves to zero permanently" option — `sync-status.ts` itself is untouched), and deleted the manual-conflict UI (`sync-conflicts.tsx` route, `sync-conflict-list.tsx`, and `SyncStatusSection`'s "Review conflicts" link/router usage). Updated the four `use-*-domain-sync.test.tsx` fault-injection suites and both `cross-domain-conflict-fault-injection.integration.test.tsx`/`two-browser-convergence.integration.test.tsx` files to add a realistic `serverRow` to every fake transport's conflict return and assert the automatic local-wins/server-wins/exact-tie outcomes instead of a parked `SyncConflictRecord`, including at least one explicit exact-tie case per domain. Full `yarn test --runInBand` (149 suites), `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` all pass.
- **Notes:** From S068. Depends on T187.1. This reverses UI T160.2 shipped (the conflict-review route and comparison UI), not just polishes it — flagged in S068's Notes. Deterministic tiebreak: an exact `updatedAt` tie (e.g. two offline edits with clock skew) keeps the server snapshot, the same rule `resolveUpdatedAtConflict` also applies when the server's `updated_at` can't be parsed at all — local only wins when it can prove it is strictly newer.

### [T186.1] Auto-trigger guest-to-account import and skip repeat onboarding when local data exists

- **Area:** Guest conversion / onboarding
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `use-guest-import.ts` currently stops at `awaitingConfirmation` until a user presses "Import my practice data" on `GuestImportPreview` (rendered on `/app/profile`), and `home.tsx`'s onboarding gate (`profile.hasCompletedOnboarding && profile.kenteiRank`) runs before that import ever happens, so a guest with local data is sent through onboarding again. Auto-call `confirmImport()` as soon as `awaitingConfirmation` is reached with any local data present (skipping the confirm/"Not now" choice), delete `GuestImportPreview`'s user-visible rendering, and make the onboarding gate aware of an in-progress or completed auto-import so a user with existing local data is never shown onboarding a second time.
- **Exit criteria:** `use-guest-import.test.tsx` passes, including a test asserting `awaitingConfirmation` with local data transitions to `uploading` without any `confirmImport()` caller action; a new `home.tsx`/onboarding integration test asserts a freshly-signed-up identity with existing local guest data skips onboarding, while one with no local data still sees it. Met: added `hasAnyLocalGuestData`/`isGuestImportPendingResolution` to `guest-import.ts` with unit tests; `use-guest-import.ts` gained a per-`importId`-gated effect that auto-dispatches `confirmPressed` as soon as `awaitingConfirmation` is reached with local data (leaving an all-zero inventory permanently parked, matching its pre-existing behavior), and dropped `cancelImport` (its only caller was removed). `home.tsx` now gates both the onboarding background and the `DailyHome`/`KenteiOnboarding` choice on `isGuestImportPendingResolution`, rendering `null` while an import is pending. New `home-guest-import.test.tsx` covers all three exit-criteria scenarios (pending-with-data hides onboarding, completed-with-seeded-profile shows `DailyHome`, all-zero-inventory still shows onboarding). Full `yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, and `yarn expo export --platform web` all pass.
- **Notes:** From S067. Covers the common case: a brand-new account has no existing cloud data, so `GuestImportPreview`'s own trace shows the state machine goes `uploading → completed` directly, never reaching `awaitingProfileChoice`/`conflicts` — this ticket's exit criteria doesn't need those states resolved. `cancelImport`'s "Not now" path was removed (unreachable once the manual confirm/"Not now" UI was deleted). Deviated from the plan's literal step 6 (deleting `GuestImportPreview`'s only caller in `profile/index.tsx`): the plan's own "Scope decision" section argued for keeping the still-live `uploading`/`awaitingProfileChoice`/`conflicts` branches reachable (an untouched, real existing-account-merge conflict flow), which requires `profile/index.tsx` to keep rendering `<GuestImportPreview />` — removing that render call would have silently stranded that flow despite the stated intent to preserve it. Only the `awaitingConfirmation`/`inventorying` branches and their now-orphaned styled-components (`CountRow`/`CountText`/`ImportButton`/`NotNowButton`) were deleted from the component. No confirmation was available for either of the plan's two flagged judgment calls (partial vs. full `GuestImportPreview` deletion; treating an empty-inventory `awaitingConfirmation` as permanently parked), so both were resolved per the plan's own reasoned defaults.

### [T186.2] Resolve guest-import's own profile-preference and record conflicts automatically

- **Area:** Guest conversion
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** When an existing account (with its own cloud data) reaches `awaitingProfileChoice` or `conflicts` during guest import, `guest-import-preview.tsx` today blocks on a manual guest-vs-cloud choice and prints raw JSON snapshots. Resolve both automatically using T187.2's most-recent-wins primitive, and delete the manual choice/JSON-snapshot UI entirely.
- **Exit criteria:** `use-guest-import.test.tsx` and `guest-import-existing-account.test.ts` pass, including a test asserting a profile-preference comparison and a record conflict both resolve automatically by `updatedAt` with no `chooseProfile`/`resolveConflict` caller action, and that no raw record data reaches any rendered UI. Met: both suites pass, including `resolveGuestImportChoice` unit tests, per-domain guest-newer/cloud-newer/tied `planExistingAccountMerge` cases (including a tombstone case comparing against the cloud deletion's own `deletedAt`, not the pre-deletion `updatedAt` still sitting on the snapshotted record), and a new `use-guest-import.test.tsx` case that drives a genuine `targetKind: 'existing'` merge with a real guest/cloud mismatch through a fuller mocked transports object all the way to `completed`, asserting `'chooseProfile' in result` etc. are all `false` and the pushed payload carries the winning side's value. `guest-import-preview.test.tsx` asserts the deleted choice buttons are never findable.
- **Notes:** From S067. Depends on T187.2 (reuses its resolution primitive rather than building a second one). Ordered after T186.1, which already covers the common empty-account path independently. Implemented design (B) from the plan: resolution moved into `planExistingAccountMerge` itself (merge-planning time) rather than auto-firing the old manual actions, so `awaitingProfileChoice`/`conflicts` states, `GuestImportConflict`/`GuestImportProfileComparison`, and `guest-import-decisions.ts` (`planProfileChoice`/`planConflictChoice`/`runExistingGuestImport`) are deleted outright, not just made unreachable; `src/shared/sync/json-snapshot.ts` (now fully dead) was deleted too. `GUEST_IMPORT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped 1→2 (a mid-flight v1 record recovers cleanly to `INITIAL_GUEST_IMPORT_STATE` and re-inventories). Resolved the plan's flagged judgment calls: a tombstone's cloud-side "last modified" time is its `deletedAt`, not the stale pre-deletion `updatedAt` still on the snapshotted `goal`/`note` (otherwise an older guest edit than the *original* record would incorrectly out-rank a *later* cloud deletion); `practiceHistory` (no `updatedAt` on either side) always keeps cloud on a payload mismatch by letting `guestUpdatedAt: undefined` fall through `resolveUpdatedAtConflict`'s own "can't prove local is newer" default; the profile scalar operation's `expectedRevision` stays `null` always, matching the pre-existing `planProfileChoice` behavior it replaces (that function's caller always passed `expectedRevision: null` too — there was never a real revision threaded for the profile row the way there is for other domains). Also fixed a latent bug surfaced while porting `domainUploadSettled`: naively collapsing it to reuse `isGuestImportFinalizable` (as the plan's step 1 suggested) can never fire, since that guard checks `state.status === 'uploading'` against a `next` state whose status is by definition still `'uploading'` at that point; extracted the operations-settled check into its own `operationsSettled` helper so `domainUploadSettled` (the one action actually allowed to leave `'uploading'`) can use it directly.

### [T188] Fix account deletion returning a 503

- **Area:** Authentication / Supabase
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** The `delete-account` Edge Function currently returns a 503 when invoked from the app, blocking T161.4's already-shipped deletion flow entirely. Diagnose whether this is a local-environment issue (per `supabase/README.md`, the function needs a running local stack via `supabase start`/`db reset`) or an actual handler/deployment defect, and fix it.
- **Exit criteria:** A real account created for this purpose is deleted successfully end-to-end through the app UI (204 from the function, Auth user and application rows removed), and the deletion control cannot be resubmitted after success; if the root cause is environmental, document the required running-stack precondition where a developer would look for it (`supabase/README.md` or `DEPLOYMENT_HANDOFF.md`). Met: root cause was environmental (confirmed by reproduction, see Notes) and `supabase/README.md` now documents the recovery step. A real disposable account was created via the local stack's admin API, signed into through the real app UI, and deleted through the real `AccountDeletionScreen` form -- the network call returned `204`, `docker`/GoTrue confirmed the Auth user was gone (`getUserById` → `404 user_not_found`; the pgTAP/Data-API cascade to every `api.*` row is already proven by `t161_1_delete_account.test.mjs`, not re-derived here), and the deletion control became permanently unreachable (see below).
- **Notes:** From S069, follow-up on T161.4. Confirmed environmental: a bare `supabase start` on an already-mixed-state stack (or one previously started from a different directory/worktree) silently leaves `supabase_edge_runtime_<project_id>` either `Exited` or bound to a stale `supabase/functions` path, both surfacing as `503` to every Edge Function caller; a full `stop` → `start` cycle from the working directory fixes it. `yarn test:functions` (26/26, including `t161_3_delete_account_acceptance.test.mjs`) passes against a freshly cycled stack. Documented in a new "Troubleshooting: Edge Functions return 503 after a stale local stack" subsection of `supabase/README.md`, cross-referenced from its existing delete-account-tests section. Manual UI verification surfaced a second, genuine (non-environmental) defect beyond the plan's scope: `AccountDeletionScreen`'s terminal "permanently deleted" message never became visible in practice, because a successful deletion signs out locally, and the resulting fresh guest identity's own (unrelated) onboarding-incomplete status causes `AppTabs`/`AppTabs.web` to conditionally unregister every non-Home tab -- including the "profile" tab this screen lives under -- which resets the active tab back to Home before the user could see it, regardless of the screen's own local `succeeded` guard. Fixed by adding a new top-level `/account-deleted` route (`src/app/account-deleted.tsx`, `src/features/auth/components/account-deleted-screen.tsx`) outside the `/app/*` tabs tree entirely, and having `AccountDeletionScreen` `router.replace` there on success instead of relying on staying mounted in place; `account-deletion-screen.tsx`'s existing `succeeded` guard is kept as a defensive fallback. New/updated tests: `account-deleted-screen.test.tsx`, and `account-deletion-screen.test.tsx`'s success case now also asserts the `replace('/account-deleted')` call. All 321 `src/features/auth` tests, the three targeted route/component tests, `yarn eslint`, and `yarn tsc --noEmit` pass.

- **Area:** Authentication UI
- **Priority:** Medium

- **Description:** `forgot-password-screen.tsx` (reached from `account-security-screen.tsx`'s gated link, T180) and `account-deletion-screen.tsx` (reached from Profile) currently offer no way back to Profile, only "Sign in"-style links — unlike `account-security-screen.tsx` itself, which already has a "Back to Profile" button (T181). Add the same "Back to Profile" control to both, shown only when reached from the authenticated Profile context (forgot-password is also reachable pre-auth from sign-in, where there is no Profile to return to).

Reused the existing `useLocalSearchParams` pattern (precedented in `auth-callback-screen.tsx`) via a new `?from=account-security` query param on that one `router.push` call, rather than navigation state. `account-deletion-screen.tsx` is only ever reached from Profile and already gates its form on `authState.status === 'authenticated'`, so its "Back to Profile" button (reusing T181's `account-security-screen.tsx` pattern) needed no extra conditional — it renders only inside that already-authenticated branch.

Restyle it to match.

### [T197] Fix account deletion still returning a 503 in the real environment

- **Area:** Authentication / Supabase
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** T188 diagnosed and fixed a 503 caused by a stale **local** Supabase stack, and verified the fix against a freshly cycled local stack. The user reports the 503 still happens. Given T195's parallel finding that T192's local-stack verification didn't hold in the real environment, first confirm whether this is the same local-vs-real gap (i.e., the real environment the user hits was never actually fixed) before re-diagnosing from scratch.
- **Exit criteria:** A real disposable account, in the environment the user actually tests against, is deleted successfully through the app UI (no 503) and can no longer sign in afterward. Met: the hosted "Daily Dama Dev" project (ref `ylyoqswauqyesuwmetnl`) had zero deployed Edge Functions (`supabase functions list` returned `{"functions":[]}`) -- T188's fix was entirely local-stack recovery and never reached the environment real signups/deletions hit, the same local-vs-real gap T195 diagnosed for the confirmation-email template. `delete-account` was deployed (`supabase functions deploy delete-account --project-ref ylyoqswauqyesuwmetnl`), which surfaced a second, real defect: the hosted gateway rejected the CORS preflight because `handler.js`'s `CORS_HEADERS` only allowed `authorization, content-type`, not the `apikey`/`x-client-info` headers supabase-js sends automatically. Fixed by widening `Access-Control-Allow-Headers` to include both. Verified end-to-end against the real hosted project: a fresh disposable account was signed up and confirmed through the real app UI (`localhost:8081` pointed at the hosted project), deleted through the real `AccountDeletionScreen` form (redirected to `/account-deleted`, no 503/CORS error), and a follow-up `POST /auth/v1/token?grant_type=password` against the hosted project with the same credentials returned `400 invalid_credentials`, confirming the Auth user is gone.
- **Notes:** From S078. Coordinate with T195 on identifying the real target environment first — resolving that question likely un-blocks both tickets at once. Documented the gap in a new "Troubleshooting: Edge Functions never reach the hosted project on their own" subsection of `supabase/README.md`, cross-referenced from the existing delete-account-tests section; also tightened that section's T162.4 rate-limiting caveat now that a real hosted-deployment gap has been confirmed rather than hypothetical.

### [T215] Fix Stats showing a false empty state right after a guest-to-account merge

- **Area:** Stats / guest conversion
- **Priority:** High
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** `StatsScreen` (`src/app/app/stats/index.tsx`) reads `usePracticeHistory().sessions` synchronously and renders `EmptyStats` whenever `sessions.length === 0`, with no check for a pending guest-to-account data merge — unlike `home.tsx`, which already gates rendering on `isGuestImportPendingResolution(guestImportState)` for exactly this class of race (T199/T206). Diagnose the render-order race for Stats specifically and gate it the same way, so a guest who signs up with existing local data doesn't see a false empty state before a manual refresh.
- **Exit criteria:** A new integration test (mirroring `home.guest-import-upload-failure.test.tsx`'s approach) confirms `StatsScreen` does not render `EmptyStats` while a guest import is still resolving, and renders the correct populated stats once it resolves without requiring a remount. Confirmed against a real guest-to-account signup with existing local practice data that Stats shows that history immediately, with no manual refresh needed. Met: traced the actual race -- `isGuestImportPendingResolution` alone is insufficient, since `useGuestImport`'s upload phase pushes practice-history rows straight to the cloud repository via its own outbox push, bypassing `PracticeHistoryProvider`'s reducer entirely, and `SyncCoordinatorProvider`'s once-per-identity-generation login pull fires before that upload finishes, so it never picks up the newly imported sessions on its own. `StatsScreen` now adds the same `isGuestImportPendingResolution` gate `home.tsx` uses (T199/T206) plus its own explicit `runSync('login')` via `usePracticeHistorySync()`, triggered once `guestImportState.status === 'completed'`, deduplicated per `importId`, gating render so `EmptyStats` never paints during that window. Added `stats.guest-import-resync.test.tsx`, mirroring `home.guest-import-resync.test.tsx`'s scaffolding with two cloud-transport mocks (profile and practice-history) sharing mutable state between push and pull, driving a real guest-import sequence to completion (Home first, matching real `/app/home` post-signup navigation, then swapping the mounted route to Stats via `root.update` on the same provider instances) and asserting `EmptyStats`'s copy never appears while the populated view surfaces without a remount; confirmed the test fails without the fix and passes with it. Browser-verified with Playwright against the local Expo web dev server and a real local Supabase stack (seeded guest `localStorage` with a completed-onboarding profile and one practice-history session, created a confirmed account via the admin API, signed in): pre-fix, Stats briefly rendered its empty-state copy before the imported session appeared; post-fix (three consecutive runs), Stats never showed the empty-state copy and the imported session populated within a few hundred milliseconds of landing on the route, with no manual refresh.
- **Notes:** From S097. `Required` for the same class of reason as T188/T189/T199/T206: an identity-change/hydration-timing race needs runtime tracing, not a known pattern applied blindly. Confirmed Stats' `usePracticeHistory()` hook has its own independent timing gap from Home's (the profile-domain resync T199 added to `home.tsx` does not cover practice history), matching the plan's diagnosis.

### [T217.1] Add `api.feedback` table with anon insert-only RLS

- **Area:** Persistence / Supabase
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** New migration adding `api.feedback` (`id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()`, `created_at timestamptz not null default now()`, `name text`, `email text`, `message text not null`), following the `api`/`private` schema convention established in `supabase/migrations/20260822191737_create_private_and_exposed_schemas.sql`. Grant `anon` `INSERT` only (no `SELECT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`) via an RLS policy with a `WITH CHECK` clause enforcing non-empty, length-bounded `message` (e.g. ≤2000 chars), length-bounded `name`/`email` (e.g. ≤100/≤254 chars), and a basic email-format check on `email` when present. This is a deliberate, narrow, documented exception to that migration's "anon gets nothing here or anywhere else" comment — update or annotate that comment so it reflects the real, current policy rather than going stale.
- **Exit criteria:** Per `supabase/README.md`'s dedicated suite (not the app Jest suite): migration applies cleanly against the local CLI stack; manual REST verification with the anon key confirms (a) a well-formed insert succeeds, (b) `SELECT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE` with the anon key are rejected, (c) an insert violating a length/format constraint is rejected, and (d) `service_role`/direct DB access can read back a row inserted in (a), confirming it actually persisted. Met: new migration `20260902000000_add_feedback_table_with_anon_insert.sql` adds `api.feedback` with table-level `CHECK` constraints mirrored in a `feedback_insert_anon` RLS `WITH CHECK` policy, grants `anon` schema `USAGE` (new — previously absent) and `INSERT` only. `npx supabase db reset` applied it cleanly from clean. Regenerated and committed `src/shared/supabase/database-types.ts`. Added `supabase/tests/t217_1_feedback.test.sql` (11 pgTAP assertions, all passing alongside the full existing suite: `Files=6, Tests=162 ... Result: PASS`). Manual REST verification against the local CLI stack with real anon/service-role keys: (a) well-formed anon `POST` returned `201 Created`; (b) anon `SELECT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE` all returned `401` with `code":"42501"` ("permission denied for table feedback" — no grant exists at all, confirming no accidental privilege leak); (c) empty message, 2001-char message, and malformed email were each rejected with `401`/`42501` ("new row violates row-level security policy" — the RLS `WITH CHECK` fired before the table `CHECK` in these anon-role cases, both layers are live per the pgTAP coverage of the equivalent table-CHECK-only paths); (d) confirmed persistence via direct DB access (`docker exec` into the local Postgres container as the `postgres` superuser) reading back the exact row inserted in (a) — matching `name`/`email`/`message`, a generated `id`, and a `created_at` close to insert time. Note: `service_role` also has no standing REST-visible `SELECT` grant on this table (a `42501` from the service-role key too), consistent with this schema's deny-by-default philosophy; direct DB access was the applicable half of "service_role/direct DB access" for confirming persistence. Reset the stack clean afterward.
- **Notes:** Blocks T217.2. Service-role/server-side insertion was considered and rejected during scoping: it would bypass RLS entirely for that connection, giving a bug in application code a much larger blast radius than an RLS-scoped anon grant, for no actual increase in the anonymous caller's capability. Did not edit the historical migration's "anon gets nothing here or anywhere else" comment in place (already-applied migration risk); the new migration's header comment explicitly annotates the exception instead, per the plan.

### [T217] Add an in-app feedback channel backed by a new `api.feedback` table

- **Area:** Feedback / Supabase
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Description:** Umbrella for T217.1 (schema) and T217.2 (UI). Give users a way to send feedback without exposing a real email address to the public internet. Originally scoped as a Resend Inbound `feedback@dailydama.com` address forwarded to a personal inbox, but rejected during scoping (2026-09-02): inbound receiving shares Resend account quota with the Supabase Auth transactional email already wired up (`supabase/RESEND_DNS_AUTH.md`), so a spammed public address could exhaust the daily/monthly send cap and break real signup confirmation emails — a materially worse failure than losing feedback. A Supabase-backed table sidesteps this: spam becomes low-stakes noisy rows the owner deletes, with no shared blast radius and no DNS/MX setup.
- **Notes:** No exit criteria of its own; done when both children are `Done`.

### [T217.2] Build the standalone feedback route and submission form

- **Area:** Feedback / navigation
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Implementer
- **Description:** New standalone Expo Router route (not nested under Profile) with a form (optional `name`, optional `email`, required `message`) that inserts directly into `api.feedback` via the Supabase client and the anon key — no server route or service-role key, per T217.1's RLS grant. Link to the route from Profile (a simple "Send feedback" entry point); Profile itself stays a placeholder otherwise, per AGENTS.md. Show a success state after a successful insert and an error state on failure. No rate limiting — deliberately deferred (see T217 notes on why spam here is low-stakes).
- **Exit criteria:** A route/component test confirms the form renders all three fields, keeps submit disabled until `message` is non-empty, and shows success/error state after a mocked insert resolves/rejects; full `yarn tsc --noEmit`; confirmed in-browser (web) that submitting the form inserts a real row visible via Supabase Studio, the route is reachable at its path, and the 320px five-link web nav is unaffected (this route isn't part of it). Met: route/component test (src/features/feedback/components/__tests__/feedback-screen.test.tsx) confirms all three fields render, submit stays disabled until `message` is non-empty (including an all-whitespace message), and success/error state renders after a mocked insert resolves/rejects; full `yarn tsc --noEmit` passes. Browser-verified against the local Expo web dev server and a real local Supabase stack (anon key): submitting the form at `/feedback` inserted a real row, confirmed via direct DB read (`docker exec` into the local Postgres container) with matching name/email/message and a generated id/created_at; the route is reachable directly at `/feedback`; and the 320px five-link web nav (Home/Sesh/Tricks/Stats/Profile, seeded via a completed-onboarding profile) still renders all five links with no horizontal overflow, confirming this new route -- deliberately outside `/app/*` -- doesn't affect it.
- **Notes:** Depends on T217.1. Submitting feedback requires no login — anonymous and authenticated users both hit the same anon-key insert path.


### [T218] Add Sentry error tracking for the web build

- **Area:** Observability / Configuration
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Status:** Done
- **Planning:** Required
- **Description:** Add Sentry (`@sentry/react-native`, which covers the Expo web target as well as native) to capture uncaught exceptions and unhandled promise rejections, initialized once at app startup (e.g. `src/app/_layout.tsx` or an entry-level file). Scope this to automatic capture only — no manual `Sentry.captureException` calls added through business logic, and no new graceful-fallback error UI/boundary; both are natural follow-ups, not this task. Per this project's active platform scope (AGENTS.md), wire this for **web only** — no iOS/Android native Sentry config in this task, even though the package also supports native. The Sentry DSN is designed to be public (unlike the source-map-upload auth token, which is a real secret and out of scope here) and can live in an `EXPO_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` env var following this repo's existing `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` convention (see `DEPLOYMENT_HANDOFF.md`).
- **Exit criteria:** Per AGENTS.md's "Configuration/dependencies" verification tier — the complete app sequence (`yarn test --runInBand`, `yarn lint`, `yarn tsc --noEmit`, `yarn expo export --platform web`) passes: confirmed via `node .claude/scripts/verify.mjs` (all four steps green — 4/4). The dashboard-confirmation half of this criterion (a deliberately thrown test error confirmed to appear in a real Sentry project's dashboard) is explicitly NOT satisfied in this run — this execution environment has no Sentry account/organization/project/DSN, so it cannot be exercised. As supporting-but-not-equivalent evidence, `initSentry()`'s wiring is covered by unit tests asserting `Sentry.init` is called exactly once with the resolved DSN and `sendDefaultPii: false` on web, and is never called on native platforms or with a missing/malformed DSN; this is code-level proof the automatic-capture wiring is correct, not proof of dashboard delivery. Deferred pending a real `EXPO_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` and dashboard access from the project owner.
- **Notes:** `Required` because this is both a dependency/configuration change and a version-sensitive Expo integration unfamiliar to this codebase. Followed the committed plan (`.claude/plans/T218.md`, deleted in this commit): added `@sentry/react-native@~7.11.0` via `npx expo install` (the version Expo SDK 57 resolves as compatible, not necessarily npm's absolute latest), then reverted the config plugin `expo install` auto-added to `app.json` — deliberately out of scope, since it and `metro.config.js` source-map upload both require a real `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` secret and only matter for native/de-minified-stack-trace concerns, not this task's "automatic capture only, web only" scope. Added `src/shared/observability/sentry-dsn.ts` (pure DSN validation, mirroring `resolveSupabaseConfig`) and `sentry-client.ts` (idempotent, web-only `initSentry()`, mirroring `getSupabaseClient()`), each with colocated unit tests. `initSentry()` is called only from a `useEffect` in `src/app/_layout.tsx`'s `TabLayout`, never at module scope, so it cannot run during Expo Router's Node static-render pass (matches the Supabase-client precedent's reasoning exactly). No `Sentry.wrap()`, tracing, profiling, or replay options added — confirmed via source that `Sentry.wrap` is not an error boundary and is unrelated to automatic capture. `.env.example` documents `EXPO_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` alongside the existing Supabase block. Source-map upload for de-minified stack traces on the Vercel static export remains deliberately deferred, as originally scoped. Dashboard-visibility verification is blocked on a real Sentry account/DSN not available in this environment — flagged in the plan up front rather than fabricated; see Exit criteria above.


### [T222] Auto-redirect from the account-deleted screen with an explanation

- **Area:** Authentication
- **Priority:** Medium

All six named auth-screen test files plus `reset-password-screen.test.tsx` (transitively affected via `PasswordInput`) pass, with only the four style-assertion tests updated to the row-based idiom (border/background moved from the input to `InputRow`) and `account-security-screen.test.tsx`'s one field-error-color assertion updated from `theme.text` to `AppColors.red` (an intentional consistency fix, not a regression). Full `yarn tsc --noEmit` and scoped `yarn eslint` pass. Browser-verified: a local Supabase-backed session (admin-created, cloud `has_completed_onboarding` seeded via a direct DB insert) confirmed sign-up, sign-in, forgot-password, account-security, and account-deletion all resolve the identical `2px solid` border, `4px` border-radius, background, and text color on their fields, plus a live accent-colored focus ring on the plain fields (previously unstyled) and unchanged icon-in-row placement on password fields.
- **Notes:** From S103, child 1 of 8. `Required` because it introduces a new reusable UI primitive under `src/components/ui/` and touches every Authentication screen — this project's Planning rules reserve unfamiliar shared-primitive introductions for the separate planning pass. Adopted `password-input.tsx`'s existing row-based border/background shape (border and background live on a row container, not the input itself) as the one canonical shape for all six call sites, rather than preserving the four plain fields' previous bordered-input shape — this is the deliberate "one consistent, polished visual style" the exit criteria calls for. Added: `Radii.control` (4px) corners (previously 0), a themed focus-border state (`theme.accent` while focused, none existed before), and standardized the field's top margin to `Spacing.four` (24px) from the two ad hoc values previously in use (28px/22px). `account-security-screen.tsx`'s "New email" field error color moved from `theme.text` to `AppColors.red` to match the majority convention — an intentional part of this task's consistency goal, not scope creep. `.claude/plans/T223.1.md` deleted in this commit.
